### Session 20 DESK MODEL (written BEFORE implementing; all from the file image + run156 captures, verified by me this session)

**Frame layout (msvc71 FH1, empirical from ca30/getter/8ea0 prologues):**
registration node = {prev @+0, stub @+4, STATE @+8}; for EBP frames
node = EBP-0xC (so EBP = node+0xC, state slot = [EBP-4]); for FPO
frames (getter, 8ea0) the state still lives at node+8 (getter keeps it
at [esp+0x1c] post-pushes, 8ea0 at [esp+0x11c]). Stub format:
`mov eax, <FuncInfo>; jmp [IAT __CxxFrameHandler]` (0x6511f726).
Throw helper 0x6511f77c = `jmp [IAT _CxxThrowException]`; the HLE
retaddr is therefore the caller's return address into the throwing
function. FuncInfo layout (standard FH1) VERIFIED against ca30:
magic 0x19930520, maxState, pUnwindMap, nTryBlocks, pTryBlockMap,
nIPMapEntries(0 here), pIPMap, pESTypeList. UnwindMapEntry {toState,
action} indexed by state (valid indices 0..maxState-1; catchHigh ==
maxState-1 here). TryBlockMapEntry {tryLow, tryHigh, catchHigh,
nCatches, pHandlerArray} (0x14 B). HandlerType {adjectives, pType,
dispPb, pHandler} (0x10 B).

**ca30's own tables (parsed from the binary):** FuncInfo 0x6513ca78,
maxState 11, ONE try block {tryLow 3, tryHigh 9, catchHigh 10}, ONE
handler {adj 0x8 (by-reference), pType 0x651410ac (.?AVOMGExceptionImpl@@
— pointer-identical to the thrown CT[0] typedesc in ThrowInfo
DAT_6513aca0; CTA @0x6513ac98, CT @0x6513ac7c), dispPb -0x68,
pHandler Catch@6510cd13}. Unwind: [7]->{4, 0x65136e55}, [4]->{3,
0x65136e34}, [3]->{2, 0}, [8]->{4, 0x65136e61}, [9]->{8, 0x65136e69},
[10]->{2, 0}. Catch@6510cd13 (46 B, VERBATIM from disasm): this =
[ebp-0x58]; **[this+0xbc] := 0** (pc 0x6510cd16); copy-ctor
(??0OMGExceptionImpl@@QAE@ABV0@@Z, IAT 0x65139300) of *[ebp-0x68] into
[ebp-0x1e4]; then `throw;` = call throw-helper with (obj=[ebp-0x1e4],
TI=0x6513aca0) — the rethrow carries the COPY, same ThrowInfo.
**Getter FuncInfo 0x6513caac: nTryBlocks=0. Node[2] FuncInfo
0x6513e800: nTryBlocks=0.** So the (0x50a) rethrow, once dispatched,
escapes the baseline call — the designed end-state (phase must end
with the same stop string as pre-fix).

**FUN_6510ca30 state machine (full disasm read):**
- [this+0xbc]!=0 -> return (wedged/in-progress/done all skip).
- +0xbc := 1. omgutil(2)!=0 -> throw (0x2,"Debugger is present",0xe7)
  under state 1 (inside the try range! would self-catch+reset+rethrow).
- Lock (this+4 vtable +8); states 2->3. Rebuild 3 filename strings
  (omgGetRepository() + table @0x651410cc names; states 4/5/6).
  bl = omgResolveRepositoryFile("icv.dat", this+0x10).
  FUN_65103dc0 openCore(this, &flagA@[ebp-0x4f]); [ebp-0x4d]:=1.
  - flagA==0 (core already open) -> DONE.
  - seed = guard(this); FUN_65116210(seed)!=0 -> DONE.
  - Seed invalid: if bl==0 -> RESTORE BLOCK (cc5c).
    Else gates: p = NULL; if FUN_65103a20() (= omgutil(4)!=0) &&
    FUN_65114d50() (an OS/env predicate via FUN_651118d0/14a20/14c20 —
    the "vista" gate) both pass: [ebp-0x4e]:=1 (pre-set), p = &[ebp-0x4e].
    al = FUN_65108ea0(this, p):
    - al==0 -> RESTORE BLOCK.
    - al!=0 && (p==NULL || *p==0) -> FUN_651048d0 closeCore;
      state:=7; throw (0x50a,"restore ICV",0x103) @cc57.  <-- RUN 156 PATH
    - al!=0 && *p!=0 -> omglog("vista migration ok"); [ebp-0x4d]:=0; DONE.
- RESTORE BLOCK (cc5c, states 8/9): pair = FUN_65103ec0(this) =
  {seed_addr, 20}; FUN_65108110(this, &local, pair) (a SAL-stream op
  via salwrap1 vt+0x40 — regenerates maclist state from the restorable
  pair); FUN_65107d70(this, &local); omglog("mac-list newly created");
  -> DONE. (Core stays OPEN here — no closeCore.)
- DONE (ccc9): FUN_6510a950(this) (post-init finalize; not dissected);
  state=3; destroy temp string; **+0xbc := 2**; state:=-1; unlock
  (vtable +0xc); return.

**FUN_65108ea0(this, out*) — "restorable pair" seed recovery (full
disasm read; FPO frame, stub 0x651364c7):**
- *out := 0 at entry (if out non-null).
- Loop over the 3 filename strings (this+0x30, stride 0x1c):
  GetFileAttributesA(name)==-1 -> next. Else readFromFile the file and
  FUN_65108330(this, &content, &sub1, &sub2, ?) parse/validate
  (extracts candidate fields incl. a seed substring); parse fail -> next.
  Parse OK -> success branch (90cc):
  - if out==NULL or seed-substring EMPTY -> SUB-PATH (91cf): build
    "restorable/"-resolved paths (omgResolveRepositoryFile("restorable/"
    ...), omgutil9 object method +0x34 checks); writeToFile refreshes a
    maclist file from the recovered content; return TRUE — *out NOT set.
  - else DIRECT PATH: copy 20 B of the parsed seed into the live seed
    mapping (guard FUN_65103e50), validate with FUN_651161b0(seed):
    invalid -> throw (0x307, "poi poi poi poi", line 0x139) (!!);
    valid -> writeToFile the content back to the maclist file AND to
    the icv-resolved file, *out := 1, return TRUE.
- No file exists/parses -> omglog("restorable pair not found"),
  return FALSE.
So run 156's cc0e outcome means: 8ea0 returned TRUE with the flag
unplanted — either gates failed (out==NULL forced the sub-path) or
the parse yielded no seed substring (sub-path). Which one is an
empirical question for the retry run's hooks (gates, 8330, out-flag).

**Harness mapping caveat (desk, from sal_emu156 source):**
h_CreateFileMappingA ignores the name; h_MapViewOfFile for a non-file
mapping returns a FRESH zeroed alloc every call. The harness therefore
has NO named-mapping persistence: every openCore sees 20 zero bytes,
and 8ea0's direct-path seed write only lives while that view stays
open (which suffices: on the direct path the core stays open into
FUN_65109380's guard). The retry question is thus exactly: does
8ea0#retry take the DIRECT path (seed planted + FUN_651161b0 valid ->
"vista migration ok" -> +0xbc:=2 -> guard passes -> FUN_65108840
verifies maclist1.dat) or the same sub-path (second closeCore+(0x50a)
escapes vt+8 = that call's faithful end-state)? File state that
changed during attempt #1: the restorable pair was (re)written at
log lines 35638-68 (before the (0x50a) at 35727) and 8ea0's sub-path
itself performs a writeToFile refresh — either could flip attempt #2
into the direct path. To be settled live, not by argument.

### Session 20 IMPLEMENTATION (sal_emu157.py; validation variant sal_emu158.py)

- h_cxxthrow_capture keeps its full run-155 capture logging, then
  records e.pending_cxx {obj, ti, retaddr, esp, register snapshot} and
  raises the new _CxxThrowSignal (HLE handlers run inside a code hook
  and cannot execute guest code, so dispatch cannot happen there).
- Emu.on_stub catches _CxxThrowSignal BEFORE the generic handler:
  stop_reason := 'cxxthrow pending dispatch', and pending_cxx gains
  'old_stop' = the EXACT pre-157 end-state string for that stub
  (f'handler error in {name}: {RuntimeError("guest called abort/exit")!r}').
- run_with_seh: at the top of its loop, a pending throw is dispatched
  by _cxx_dispatch at top level. Unhandled -> stop_reason := old_stop
  and the phase ends exactly as pre-157. Handled -> _cxx_transfer has
  already set state/chain/registers; _cxx_resume chunk-resumes guest
  execution at the catch handler; the loop then continues (a rethrow
  re-enters the same path; a hardware fault falls into the old SEH
  path unchanged). A stale pending throw is cleared+logged at phase
  start (throws only occur inside run_with_seh phases in this probe).
- _cxx_dispatch (TABLE-DRIVEN): parses the thrown ThrowInfo's
  CatchableTypeArray (props, TD, mdisp/pdisp/vdisp, size, copyfn);
  walks fs:[0]; per frame resolves FuncInfo from the node handler stub
  (B8 <imm32> E9 pattern only; FH1 magic required), reads the frame
  state at node+8, scans TryBlockMap for [tryLow..tryHigh] containing
  the state, matches HandlerType.pType == a thrown TD (pointer
  equality; pType==0 = catch(...)). Every frame's resolution is
  logged ([cxxeh] frame[i] ... nTryBlocks=).
- _cxx_transfer: unwinds intervening frames to -1 and the target
  frame to tryLow via the frames' own UnwindMap funclets (executed as
  guest code with EBP = node+0xC, ESP = EBP-0x400 with a halt
  sentinel); writes state := catchHigh at node+8; fs:[0] := target
  node; writes the exception pointer at [EBP+dispPb] for by-reference
  catches (adj 0x8), or copy-constructs / memcpys for by-value;
  restores the throw-time register snapshot, sets EBP = node+0xC,
  ESP = EBP-0x400 (+halt sentinel), returns pHandler as resume EIP.
- sal_emu158.py = sal_emu157 with ONLY the TypeLib REG seed withheld
  (validation (a): the (0x303)-class flow). sal_emu.py untouched.

### Session 21 DESK MODEL (written BEFORE the instrumented run; all
from fresh aligned disassembly of the corpus binaries this session —
dis21.py/dis21b.py in scratch/ — plus decomp-key.c for 8330/8110)

**ca30 gate region (byte-exact, 0x6510cbb9–0x6510cbf5):**
bl = icv.dat resolve flag (omgResolveRepositoryFile(this+0x10,
"icv.dat") at cb74); bl==0 -> RESTORE BLOCK (cc5c), 8ea0 never runs.
Otherwise: **[ebp-0x4e] := 1 PRE-SET** (cbc1); esi := NULL;
[ebp-0x54] := NULL; call FUN_65103a20; if AL==0 skip; call
FUN_65114d50; if EAX==0 skip; only if BOTH pass: esi := &[ebp-0x4e],
[ebp-0x54] := esi. push esi; ecx=edi(this); call FUN_65108ea0.
After: AL==0 -> RESTORE BLOCK; esi==NULL -> closeCore+(0x50a);
[esi]==0 -> closeCore+(0x50a); else omglog("vista migration ok"),
[ebp-0x4d]:=0, DONE. NOTE: ca30's preset of the out-byte to 1 is
irrelevant — 8ea0 zeroes it at entry (below). The flag ends 1 ONLY
via 8ea0's direct path.

**FUN_65103a20 = omgutil(4) != 0** (3-insn wrapper over OMGUtils!
omgutil, IAT 0x651392d4). omgutil (OMGUtils 0x6391a2e0) is a
selector switch over a cached OS LEVEL computed by FUN_63919d40
from **GetVersionExA** (IAT 0x639221dc; OSVERSIONINFO 0x94):
platform != VER_PLATFORM_WIN32_NT(2) -> level 1; major < 5 ->
level 2; major == 5 -> level 3; major >= 6 -> level 4. Cached at
[0x6392dea0]. omgutil(4) := (level >= 4) = **Vista-or-later**.
omgutil(3) := level>=3; omgutil(1) := level==1; omgutil(2) is a
separate helper (63919f60, the "Debugger is present" input).
**Harness answers TODAY: h_GetVersionExA = 5.1.2600 platform 2
(XP SP2); h_GetVersion = 0x0A280105 (also XP) -> level 3 ->
omgutil(4) = FALSE.** The corpus install is Lola's Win7 Toshiba
(major 6.1 -> level 4 -> TRUE, absent compat shims). So gate #1 is
an OS-version HLE answer we control, and the code evidence says
our current answer is the WRONG OS for this install. (Licensed
correction candidate for step 3, after live confirmation.)

**FUN_65114d50 = FUN_65114a20(desc) || FUN_65114c20(desc)** over a
0x204-byte descriptor built by FUN_651118d0: copies a 0x1ae-byte
obfuscated blob from salwrap .rdata 0x65139d50, transforms it in
place (FUN_6511de30 -> FUN_6511dd60), then strtok's it on "\n"
(delims string @0x65139f00) into ~13 string fields stored at
desc+0x1b0, +0x1b4, ..., +0x1e8 — registry key/value NAMES.
- 14a20: via the omgutil9 registry object ([0x651392d0], vt+0x1c
  open, FUN_65103bd0 query, [0x65139004] read): opens **HKLM**
  (0x80000002) key named desc+0x1bc, queries values desc+0x1e4 and
  desc+0x1cc, reads desc+0x1e8; then repeats with desc+0x200 in
  place of +0x1e4. Returns 1 iff all succeed, else 0.
- 14c20: first FUN_65112260(desc, &out) — opens HKLM key desc+0x1b0,
  reads value desc+0x1b4, compares via FUN_65111fb0; fills `out`.
  Then formats `out` (FUN_6511dda0, FUN_65111e80) and queries the
  HKLM desc+0x1bc key with FUN_65103bd0 using the formatted data;
  returns 1 on its success combination, else 0.
**So gate #2 is NOT an OS-version check at all** (the "vista"
nickname was wrong): it probes for the presence of specific HKLM
registry keys/values — the migration-era layout markers. Its
answers come from the harness's FAKE registry (advapi HLEs under
OMGUtils' real code); the guest-decrypted names will be visible in
the HLE trace during the gate window, and Lola's Toshiba .reg
export can be checked for exactly those keys afterwards.

**FUN_65108ea0(this ECX, out [esp+4]) — byte-exact entry/branch:**
ebp := out; **if (out) *out := 0** (8f2d). Local S0 (OMG-string at
esp+0x88) := copy of the singleton's +0x10 name string. Loop i=0..2
over filename strings (this+0x30, stride 0x1c): GetFileAttributesA
[0x6513905c]; ==-1 -> next. Else three empty OMG-strings
(+0xbc SEED, +0xc0-ish SUB, +0xf8 SUB2 locals), readFromFile
[0x65139310](1,0,0,name) into the CONTENT local, then call
**FUN_65108330(this, &CONTENT, &S0, &SUB2, out ? &SEED : NULL)**
(call @8ff1; arg4 = &[esp+0xbc] IFF out!=NULL — **if the gates
fail, 8330 is never even asked to extract a seed**). AL==0 -> next
file. AL!=0 -> success branch @90cc: out==NULL -> SUB-PATH (91cf);
**[SEED.size]==0 -> SUB-PATH** (cmp [esp+0xd0],0 — SEED object:
data@+4 (or [ +4 ]), size@+0x14, cap@+0x18, OMG-string layout).
DIRECT PATH: guard FUN_65103e50 -> copy 20 B of SEED data into the
live mapping; FUN_651161b0(mapping) validate — ==0 -> throw
(0x307, "poi poi poi poi" @0x651398ac, line 0x139); valid ->
writeToFile content back to the maclist file AND to the icv file,
***out := 1** (@91a1/91c0), return TRUE. SUB-PATH @91cf: resolve
"restorable/" [0x651398a0] via omgResolveRepositoryFile; possibly
omgutil9 vt+0x34 create/check (with a (0x1a)-throw branch if a
combine step fails); writeToFile the parsed content to
restorable/<name> (FUN_651015f0 join with 0x651395c4) and to
restorable/icv.dat; return TRUE, *out untouched (stays 0).
No file parses -> omglog("restorable pair not found" @0x651398bc),
return FALSE. 8ea0 is `ret 4` (stdcall, 1 stack arg).

**FUN_65108330 (decomp-key.c)**: a dual SAL-stream invoke driver:
first dword-compare of a content string's first 8 bytes against
DAT_651395f0 ("MACLIST\0" magic — the DB head) decides the branch:
maclist branch builds a stream [octet16(vt+8 OMG ID), buffer-ref,
buffer-ref, int(param!=0), module "maclist.ocm"], executes via
vt+0, reads back a status int (vt+0x9c) + byte; if the int is 0 it
short-returns (AL semantics mangled by Ghidra — settle live); on
success reads back TWO strings via vt+0x80 (read-string) into the
caller's out strings. icv branch is the same shape with module
"icv.ocm" and an extra write-int 4. This is the same maclist.ocm
the ctor's op 1/op 4 use — the "parse" is a VM program run over
the file content + S0 string inputs.

## Session 22 (2026-10-29) — Phase 1: gate #2 faithfulness (desk only)

QUESTION: in run 163, ensure-init's gate #2 (salwrap FUN_65114d50)
returned TRUE under the harness's fake registry. Is that pass
LEGITIMATE for the real target install (Lola's Win7 Toshiba)?

salwrap.dll used: corpus copy
/home/hatch/workspace/atrac-cd-stage0/batch2/Sony Shared/OpenMG/salwrap.dll
MD5 8bb3f0258a178e099700d00acd444a98 (the same file the harness
loads via SONY_DIR; image mapping verified against known .rdata
strings at their Session-21 VAs).

### 1. Descriptor extraction — SOLVED (with a decoy to record)

FUN_65114d50 (103 B, verified by aligned disassembly) =
build descriptor via FUN_651118d0, then return
FUN_65114a20(desc) || FUN_65114c20(desc) (short-circuit OR).

FUN_651118d0 copies a 0x1ae-byte obfuscated blob from salwrap
.rdata 0x65139d50 into the descriptor and transforms it in place,
then splits it with strtok on "\n" (delims string @0x65139f00)
into 21 token pointers at desc+0x1b0 .. desc+0x200 step 4.

DECOY WARNING (premise correction): the obvious transform reading
is WRONG. FUN_6511de30's direct path calls FUN_6511dd60 (a
quadratic keystream: c <- c*0x1f770825+0x0c9f03d1, byte =
((c+5)*c sar 15) low) — but de30 executes an int3 (0x6511de5d)
BEFORE that call; the breakpoint exception's SEH handler
(0x6511de85) is what actually runs, and it calls FUN_6511dd00
instead. dd60 NEVER executes (a brute-force over all 2^32 seeds
confirmed no seed makes dd60 produce sensible text from this
blob; the run-163 trace's [eh3] "breakpoint filter MATCHED;
transferring to handler 0x6511de85" shows the same mechanism
live). The REAL transform (FUN_6511dd00, seed = arg3):
  S0 = (0x1f6d8cbc - seed) * (seed + 0x6e13cb41) mod 2^32;
  per byte: out = in XOR (S & 0xFF); state is then fed BY THE
  INPUT BYTE b: t = (b*4) XOR b; t ^= t>>5; t ^= (t<<0xb);
  S = t ^ (t>>0x13)  (all 32-bit).
With seed 0 (the arg 118d0 pushes) this decrypts the blob to
clean text, and the result reproduces run 163's strtok token
lengths exactly, incl. token 14 in cleartext in that log.

THE DESCRIPTOR (desc offset = token):
  +0x1b0  SOFTWARE\Sony Corporation\OpenMG
  +0x1b4  IntegrityCheckID
  +0x1b8  System\CurrentControlSet\Control\DeviceClasses\{9a775800-9c47-11d3-9f21-00400550e38d}
  +0x1bc  Software\CLASSES\CLSID
  +0x1c0  MiscStatus
  +0x1c4  1
  +0x1c8  TypeLib
  +0x1cc  InprocServer32
  +0x1d0  ole32.dll
  +0x1d4  Version
  +0x1d8  \RICHED20.DLL
  +0x1dc  ThreadingModel
  +0x1e0  Both
  +0x1e4  {336475D0-942A-11CE-A870-00AA002FEAB5}
  +0x1e8  {186A6EAC-807C-11D0-8936-2EC0925CBC57}
  +0x1ec  Image Convolution for VB
  +0x1f0  \dxtmsft.dll
  +0x1f4  Repository
  +0x1f8  OMGRIGHT
  +0x1fc  *.ICV
  +0x200  {3B158650-D9FD-4ABF-9B87-0D762113274F}
(Tokens +0x1b8, +0x1c0..+0x1e0, +0x1ec..+0x1fc are NOT used by
gate #2: the descriptor is a shared probe table — FUN_651118d0
and FUN_65112260 each have 5 further callers at

### 2. What gate #2 actually computes (polarity CORRECTED)

Session 21's desk model said "14a20 returns 1 iff all succeed".
That was INVERTED. Aligned disassembly of FUN_65114a20
(0x65114a20-0x65114c14), FUN_65114c20 (0x65114c20-0x65114d4d),
FUN_65103bd0 (open-subkey helper over the OMGUtils omgutil9
object's vt+0x1c open; 0 = success throughout, advapi-style),
and FUN_65112260/FUN_65111fb0 gives:

FUN_65114a20 — a NEGATIVE registration probe, two rounds:
  round 1 GUID = desc+0x1e4 {336475D0...}, round 2 GUID =
  desc+0x200 {3B158650...}. Each round: open HKLM key
  desc+0x1bc (Software\CLASSES\CLSID, access 0x20019); open
  subkey <GUID>; open subkey desc+0x1cc (InprocServer32);
  RegQueryValueExA (IAT 0x65139004) the value NAMED desc+0x1e8
  ({186A6EAC...}) with a 0x100 buffer. A round COMPLETES only
  if every step returns 0. 14a20 returns 0 iff round 1 or round
  2 completes; returns 1 iff BOTH rounds fail somewhere. I.e.
  14a20 = 1 exactly when this legacy COM registration chain is
  ABSENT/broken for both GUIDs.

FUN_65114c20 — also negative at its decisive step:
  stage 1 = FUN_65112260(desc): open HKLM key desc+0x1b0
  (SOFTWARE\Sony Corporation\OpenMG); RegQueryValueExA the
  value desc+0x1b4 (IntegrityCheckID) into a 0x100 buffer;
  FUN_65111fb0 validates the string is a canonical GUID
  ('{' + dashes at 9/14/19/24 + '}' at 37) and parses it to a
  16-byte GUID (FUN_65111b60). NO comparison against any fixed
  GUID — any well-formed IntegrityCheckID passes stage 1.
  12260 returns 1 on success; if it returns 0, 14c20 returns 0.
  stage 2: the parsed GUID is run through the dda0-family
  transform (FUN_6511dda0 -> handler -> FUN_6511dca0, the
  output-fed twin of dd00) and re-formatted as a GUID string by
  FUN_65111e80 (fields 4-2-2-2-6, '{'/'}', hex tables
  0x65139f18 UPPERCASE except field 3 which uses 0x65139f04
  lowercase); then open HKLM\Software\CLASSES\CLSID and try to
  open a subkey NAMED BY THAT TRANSFORMED GUID (FUN_65103bd0).
  14c20 returns 1 iff the subkey open FAILS (transformed GUID
  not registered); returns 0 if it opens, or if the CLSID key
  itself will not open, or stage 1 failed.
  The transform recipe was REPRODUCED byte-exact in Python: a
  sibling probe using the identical recipe in run 161 (vt+8
  flow, run161.txt line 36120) probed CLSID subkey
  '{0F78C500-E106-d0d1-CEAF-D9ECC2AF8823}' — exactly
  dca0-transform(string-order GUID bytes, seed 0, state fed by
  output byte) of the Toshiba IntegrityCheckID
  {B34A50CC-E17B-ce5e-4445-D84F8778899C}, formatted with the
  field-3-lowercase quirk ('d0d1'). So on the Toshiba, 14c20's
  stage-2 probe name is '{0F78C500-E106-d0d1-CEAF-D9ECC2AF8823}'
  (modulo 14c20's own dda0 arg — see the robustness sweep in
  section 4: every seed/layout/feed variant of the transform
  names a GUID that is absent from the export, so the stage-2
  outcome does not depend on that residual).

So gate #2 = "the old registration layout is NOT in place":
14a20 fires when the two dxtmsft-era class chains are missing;
14c20 fires when the install's own IntegrityCheckID GUID is
not registered as a CLSID (under its transformed name). TRUE
licenses ensure-init to hand 8ea0 the out-pointer (migration
path) — consistent with a "needs migration" detector, not a
"migration already done" detector.

### 5. VERDICT

(a) HONEST PASS in outcome — with a mechanism divergence that
must stay on the record. On the real install, gate #2 returns
TRUE just as it did in run 163, so ensure-init's grant of the
8ea0 out-pointer (the direct path, the seed recovery, "vista
migration ok") is LEGITIMATE for the real Toshiba: real
hardware would take the same path. But the TRUE is reached by
different arms: the harness short-circuited on 14a20 = 1 (its
fake tree lacks both class chains entirely, so "both rounds
fail" for a reason that does not exist on the real machine),
whereas the real machine has 14a20 = 0 (round 2 genuinely
completes — the {3B158650} class is registered and carries the
{186A6EAC} value) and passes on 14c20 = 1 (transformed-GUID
CLSID absent). The divergence is BENIGN for everything
downstream so far, because ensure-init consumes only the
boolean. It is NOT benign as a general model: the harness
registry is underpopulated for these probes, and any future
behavior that depends on WHICH arm fired (or on 14c20 having
run) would diverge from real hardware.

For completeness, the faithful gate-2 seed set (value-exact
from the export) that would make the harness take the REAL
path — gate still returns 1, via 14a20 = 0 then 14c20 = 1:
  'software\classes\clsid\{336475d0-942a-11ce-a870-00aa002feab5}'
    -> {} and its '\inprocserver32' -> {} (NO {186A6EAC} value)
  'software\classes\clsid\{3b158650-d9fd-4abf-9b87-0d762113274f}'
    -> {} and its '\inprocserver32' ->
    {'{186A6EAC-807C-11D0-8936-2EC0925CBC57}':
     '##.#00000000795b093f#{e02942f2-8633-7617-59e2-8cedde0125fc}'}
  OpenMG IntegrityCheckID seed unchanged; NO entry for the
  transformed-GUID key. (Harness paths are the guest's literal
  spellings — its HLE applies no WOW64 redirection.)
Phase 2 applies this seeding in a numbered copy so the harness
walks the real arm from now on; expected effect is path-only.

Easter eggs: NONE new in the descriptor area or the transform
family (the blob is all registry names; "Image Convolution for
VB" is a genuine dxtmsft effect name). The dd60/dd00 decoy-real
split via int3 + SEH handler is an anti-debug pattern, noted
here as technique, not an egg.
  Robustness sweep for the stage-2 name (appended after the
  section above was written): the transformed-GUID candidates
  under ALL recipe variants — {0F78C500-...} (string-order,
  seed 0, output-fed), {2BC9A1FA-...} (seed 4, output-fed),
  {0F2438D5-...}/{2B2438D5-...} (input-fed), {70EC0F81-...},
  {70B053DB-...}, {545D6B7B-...}, {54B053DB-...} (struct-LE
  layouts) — have ZERO matches as registry keys/values in the
  export (full-line awk pass; a single raw strings count of 1
  for '54B053DB' resolves to no registry line at all on the
  exhaustive pass). So 14c20's stage 2 fails on the Toshiba
  under every variant of its own recipe: the verdict does not
  hinge on the recipe residual. Parent key of the
  IntegrityCheckID line confirmed by the same pass:
  [HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE\SOFTWARE\Wow6432Node\Sony Corporation\
  OpenMG] — the 32-bit view, as expected.

### (c) FINALIZE QUESTION — SETTLED

ca30's tail after "vista migration ok" (log call at 0x6510cbfc)
is straight-line guest code (jmp 0x6510ccc9 -> call
FUN_6510a950 -> store [edi+0xbc] := 2 at 0x6510cce5 -> ret);
verified by disassembly, no guest branch diverts it.
Run 164 [s22] hooks: the omglog CALL site was reached exactly
once (run164.txt:9303); the return site 0x6510cc02 NEVER
fired; the DONE tail (ccc9), FUN_6510a950, its return (ccd0),
and the +0xbc store (cce5) NEVER fired; the (0x50a) throw site
(cc57) never fired. Singleton +0xbc read 0 at the first ca30
entry and 1 at all 9 later entries — it NEVER reached 2.
REASON: the omglog call itself never returns in the harness.
Execution proceeds inside OMGUtils and at run164.txt:9564 hits
the SAME wild jump as 163's baseline: invalid access at
0x74216ba2, 0xc0000005, SEH walk over OMGUtils frames finds no
handler, UNHANDLED; the harness's SEH rounds then dispose of
the baseline emu context (the phase still reports EAX=0x1,
run164.txt:9600) with ca30's frame abandoned mid-call.
So the finalize tail is not skipped by guest logic — it is
amputated by a harness-environment divergence inside
OMGUtils' logging path (the "OMGUtils-hosted VM continuation"
wild jump of Session 21). On real Windows omglog returns and
the tail runs; +0xbc := 2 is a bookkeeping state the harness
never sees. Every later getter/ca30 call in the process
therefore re-enters ensure-init with +0xbc == 1 (early-return
path) — the singleton's DATA (seed mapping at +0x0 =
0x107ea890, live seed) is nonetheless fully in place, as the
vt+8 flow below proves. Same behavior in both disk arms.

Baseline death: IDENTICAL in 163 and 164 (wild jump
0x74216ba2 inside the post-migration omglog call) — NOT

The singleton/live-state shape was the SAME in both runs
(baseline migration in-process, +0xbc stuck at 1, seed live in

generation the baseline started from — equivalently the
(record form + extracted seed) unit the ctor's verify consumes

VERDICT: the post-migration divergence is driven by the
guest-rewritten DB pair's generation, not by the live-seed
singleton state. Form-2 (one migration pass over the corpus
pair) sends the ctor's verify into garbage EH frames around
op 4; form-3 (a second pass) verifies cleanly. Form-2 is an
intermediate generation, not a stable end state.

### ⚠️ CORRECTION (post-report, from the completed robustness sweep): verdict flips to (b) LUCKY PASS

The background sweep finished AFTER the Phase-1 verdict above was written, and it contradicts
it: prefix `2BC9A1FA` has 12 matches in the export, not zero. Verified by a parent-attributed
pass: **`{2BC9A1FA-E82A-483c-8EE4-B43A536E5085}` EXISTS as a full CLSID registration in the
32-bit view** — `[HKLM\SOFTWARE\Classes\Wow6432Node\CLSID\{2BC9A1FA-E82A-483c-8EE4-B43A536E5085}]`
with subkeys `InprocServer32` (@="ole32.dll"), `MiscStatus`, `MiscStatus\1`, `TypeLib`,
`Version` (and the mirror spelling under `Wow6432Node\Classes\CLSID`). The subkey vocabulary
matches the descriptor's probe tokens exactly.

Why this GUID: 14c20's dda0 call re-verified from raw disassembly (0x65114c77–ca8): four
stack slots are cleaned by the caller's `add esp,0x10`; dda0 consumes three —
(buf=[esp+0x2c] transformed GUID bytes, len=0x10, **seed=4**) — the 4th slot is a saved edi.
`dca0(string-order GUID bytes of {B34A50CC-E17B-ce5e-4445-D84F8778899C}, seed 4, output-fed)`
formatted by 11e80 (field-3 lowercase) = **`{2BC9A1FA-E82A-483c-8EE4-B43A536E5085}`** — the
registered key, byte-exact including the lowercase `483c`. The sibling probe's `{0F78C500…}`
is the seed-0 transform used by a DIFFERENT consumer (0x65112xxx) — my earlier identification
of 14c20's probe with the sibling's was the error; the "robustness sweep" text above wrongly
extended it. The OpenMG installer evidently registers a COM class (InprocServer32 ole32.dll)
under the obfuscated transform of the install's own IntegrityCheckID, and 14c20 checks for it.

**Corrected real-machine evaluation of gate #2:**
- 14a20 = 0 (unchanged: round 2 completes — `{3B158650…}\InprocServer32` carries the
  `{186A6EAC…}` value, exhaustive 2-occurrence attribution held).
- 14c20: stage 1 passes (IntegrityCheckID present, GUID-shaped) → stage 2: CLSID opens,
  transformed-GUID subkey **opens successfully** → **14c20 = 0**.
- **FUN_65114d50 = 0 || 0 = 0 → GATE #2 FAILS on the real Toshiba.**

**VERDICT (corrected): (b) LUCKY PASS.** The harness passed gate #2 purely from registry
poverty (14a20=1 because its tree lacks both class chains; 14c20 never ran, and would also
have returned 1 for lack of the `{2BC9A1FA}` key). On the real install both of 14d50's arms
find the legacy registrations PRESENT, so the gate returns 0: ensure-init would NOT grant
FUN_65108ea0 the out-pointer (esi stays NULL) and, from a cold zero seed, would take the
closeCore+(0x50a) path with the ICV singleton left uninitialized. **The direct-path migration
of runs 163/164 — and everything downstream of it (pair rolling form-1→2→3, rolled seeds
f1e9db56…/824155f8…, op 4 = 0) — is a gates-open counterfactual trajectory, faithful given
the gates but NOT the Toshiba's cold-start behavior.** The real install's seed must arrive
via the genuine session flow (consistent with the Toshiba OMG_RAND_SEED watch: the mapping
never appears outside a working session; and with the real pair's form-1 state having been
produced by historically successful real sessions).

**Revised faithful-seed specification (supersedes the Phase-1 one):** to model the real
machine, REG_TREE must ALSO contain
`software\classes\clsid\{2bc9a1fa-e82a-483c-8ee4-b43a536e5085}` → {} (its presence is what
14c20's stage 2 tests; InprocServer32 @="ole32.dll" may be seeded for completeness), in
addition to the {336475D0}/{3B158650} chains specified above. Under that registry the harness
gate #2 also returns 0. Any future migration-trajectory run must be labeled as modeling the
gates-open counterfactual explicitly.

Phase 2's measurements stand as harness-internal observations; their interpretation is
bounded by this correction. Easter eggs: still none new.

#### Session 23 verdict

The harness now walks the Toshiba's actual road, and the
road is a dead end BY DESIGN from a cold start: gate #2 = 0
-> no out-pointer -> 8ea0 validates the pair and refreshes
restorable/ but cannot plant the seed -> closeCore + (0x50a)
-> the singleton can never initialize inside this process.
That is precisely consistent with the two pieces of real-
machine evidence: (1) the Toshiba OMG_RAND_SEED watch — the
mapping NEVER appears outside a working session; (2) the
real pair's form-1 state was produced by historically
successful sessions. The seed on real hardware must be
written into the OMG_RAND_SEED mapping by a component
OUTSIDE salwrap's ensure-init (a working session's flow —
e.g. the app/OMG core process that owns the mapping), after
which openCore's verdict would be 1 and ensure-init would
take its DONE tail. Cold-start emulation of salwrap alone
structurally cannot reproduce that input; it is an
environment input, not a code path.
BLOCKER, restated at its new precision: session create is
blocked at the ICV singleton's uninitialized state on the
real road ((0x2) guard in FUN_65109380), because the
OMG_RAND_SEED mapping's writer is outside the emulated
process set. Named next directions (future sessions):
(a) find WHO writes OMG_RAND_SEED on a real install — desk
    work: search the corpus binaries (Omgjbox/OMG core/app
    layer, OpenMG services) for the "OMG_RAND_SEED" string /
    CreateFileMapping callers beyond salwrap's openCore;
(b) if the writer is another OpenMG DLL the harness can
    load, drive it; (c) the gates-open counterfactual remains
    the only road that has passed op 4 — its invoke-#5
    (0x2)/kind-0x136 frontier and the [cxxthrow] fs:[0]
    aliasing choke are still open there.

decryption and the (0x50a)/(0x2) messages are long-collected
or diagnostic). NOTE: scratch/root's omglog.txt accumulates

attributable to run 165; run 165's own invokes carried the

## Session 24 (2026-10-29) — who writes OMG_RAND_SEED? omginit.exe characterized (DESK ONLY, no runs)

QUESTION: Session 23's verdict says the seed must be written
into the OMG_RAND_SEED mapping by a component OUTSIDE
salwrap's ensure-init. The main agent's string census of the
whole corpus OpenMG tree (ASCII + UTF-16LE) found the string
"OMG_RAND_SEED" in exactly TWO binaries: salwrap.dll and
omginit.exe (20,992 B, MD5 b6d417361172cf7a5e06352448a20461,
Nov 28 2007 — same vintage as salwrap/pfcom; FileVersion
5.0.00.11280). This session: characterize omginit statically
(Ghidra decompile of ALL its functions ->
out/decomp-omginit.c; OMGUtils side ->
out/decomp-omgutils9.c + ctor/vtable follow-ups), decide
writer/reader/neither, and census-extend if it is not the
writer. NO emulation runs were made: the statics exclude
writer-hood by construction (below), and the brief's drive
step was conditional on a writer verdict.

### omginit.exe imports (pefile, copy in scratch/omginit/)

KERNEL32: CreateFileA, ReadFile, FindFirst/NextFileA,
GetFileAttributesA, CreateDirectoryA, LoadLibraryA,
GetProcAddress, QueryPerformanceCounter/Frequency,
GetTickCount, GetSystemTimeAsFileTime, GetCurrentProcessId,
ExitProcess... — **NO CreateFileMappingA, NO MapViewOfFile,
NO OpenFileMapping, NO CreateProcess/WinExec, NO event
APIs in the import table**, and the string "MapViewOfFile"
appears nowhere in the binary (its only GetProcAddress
target is "omgutil9"). ADVAPI32: RegOpenKeyExA /
RegQueryValueExA / RegSetValueExA. ole32: CoInitialize.
MSVCR71/MSVCP71: fopen/fread/fwrite, _access, _stat,
std::string. It resolves ALL OS primitives beyond its
imports through the OMGUtils "omgutil9" interface object
(FUN_00401000: LoadLibraryA(repo\OMGUtils.dll) +
GetProcAddress("omgutil9"); FUN_004011a0 lazy singleton
getter; on load failure it falls back to a LOCAL stub
vtable PTR_PTR_00405020 inside omginit itself).

### main flow (FUN_00401a10; args = raw command-line tail)

Usage: "usage: omginit [-reset]". Flags parsed in a loop:
-d (sets a debug/log flag DAT_0040593c), **-new -> returns
0 immediately (vestigial no-op in this build)**, -reset
(sets local_b15), anything else -> usage + exit 1.
Then, ALWAYS (both modes):
 1. Via the omgutil9 object (vt+0x1c): open
    HKLM\SOFTWARE\Sony Corporation\OpenMG; RegQueryValueExA
    "Repository" -> repo path (DAT_00405940).
 2. Build paths: repo\OMGKEY\omg_sal.dat, repo\OMGKEY\
    omg_id.dat, repo\OMGKEY\, repo\maclist.dat,
    repo\maclist1.dat, repo\maclist2.dat.
 3. _access/_stat the three maclist files; pick the
    LARGEST existing one; fopen it and fread up to 0x800
    bytes (header only). If none exists, fall back to
    OMGKEY\omg_sal.dat; if that yields 0 bytes too, the
    input is NULL.
 4. FUN_004012d0 ("GetOpenMGIDImage" — main logs that name
    on failure) -> the 22-byte (0x16) ID image + size:
    - input with "MACLIST" magic AND bytes[8..11) ==
      01 0f 50: image = dword 0x10048030, then maclist
      bytes [8..24) copied verbatim into image[4..20),
      image[20..22) = 0.
    - else input[4..7) == 01 0f 50: image = input[0..20)
      verbatim (+2 zero bytes).
    - else if input == NULL: GENERATE fresh (below).
    - else return 1 -> main logs "GetOpenMGIDImage",
      exit 1.
 5. Create OMGKEY dir if missing; fopen
    repo\OMGKEY\omg_id.dat "wb" (FUN_00401700); fwrite the
    22-byte image ("fwrite: id_image" on short write). If
    the image was GENERATED (no input), log through omglog
    (FUN_00402e20): "omgid generated: %s" with %02X hex of
    image bytes [4..20) — the Session 23 bookkeeping note's
    fossil "omgid generated" line in the accumulated
    omglog.txt is this message from a real omginit run on
    the Toshiba (install-time ID minting; its tail differs
    from the current genuine omg_id because the live
    install's ID was later re-derived from its maclist).
 6. Ensure repo\procfile\ exists; compare repo\device.sal
    vs repo\procfile\device.sal via FUN_004017c0 (size/
    checksum compare): copy the newer over the older
    (FUN_00401740 CopyFile wrapper; "CopyFile(%s, %s)
    failed" on error); equal -> exit 0.
-RESET mode additionally, between steps 5 and 6:
 7. **vt+0x2c of the omgutil9 object called with
    (INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE, NULL, PAGE_READWRITE=4, 0,
    0x14=20, "OMG_RAND_SEED")** — CreateFileMappingA
    semantics exactly (arg order from the call site; the
    slot's own signature is thiscall + ret 0x18, six args).
    - returns 0 -> log "CreateFileMapping: %d", exit 1.
    - GetLastError() == 0xb7 (ERROR_ALREADY_EXISTS) ->
      MessageBoxA(NULL, "!!", NULL, 0) — the message text
      is LITERALLY the two-character string "!!" at
      0x4043ec (placeholder/stub text; flagged as a
      dev-trace curiosity, not claimed as an Easter egg)
      — and exit 1: **a live session owns the mapping;
      do not reset under it.**
    - fresh creation -> **CloseHandle immediately. No
      view is ever mapped; no byte is written.**
 8. RegSetValueExA(OpenMG key, "IntegrityCheckID", 0,
    REG_SZ, "\0", 1) — CLEARS the IntegrityCheckID to the
    empty string (datum byte at 0x4043e9 verified 0x00;
    "RegSetValue(IntegrityCheckID) failed" on error).
 9. Delete repo\icv.dat (object vt+0x44 delete op; "%s:
    cannot delete" on failure); recursively remove
    repo\procfile (FUN_00401880); omglog "reset OpenMG".
omginit NEVER writes maclist.dat/1/2, NEVER touches
restorable/, NEVER reads beyond the first 0x800 bytes of
a maclist, launches/signals NOTHING (no child-process or
event imports; CoInitialize only), and contains neither
"OMG_ICV_LOCK" nor "OMG_KDRM_EVENT_COUNTER" (the lone
"kdrm" string is a repo subdirectory name in a path
build). The seed substring f1e9db56... lives in the
maclist DB BODY, far past the 0x800 header window omginit
reads, and no code path in the exe can carry 20 arbitrary
bytes into the mapping.

### Fresh-ID generation (FUN_004012d0, NULL input)

QueryPerformanceCounter/Frequency -> microsecond counter
-> an 8-byte key/IV pair; a DES-shaped crypto core
(FUN_00402d30/00402240 ctx setup over a 256-byte state,
FUN_00402340 byte output, FUN_004023d0/00402610/
00402820/004028d0/00402950 with 56-entry bit-permutation
tables at 0x405040-0x405120) generates bytes: 16-byte and
8-byte outputs are ground through a loop until the first
generated byte is < 8 (those outputs feed NOTHING else),
then 5 further generated bytes land at image[0x0f..0x13].
All other image fields are constants (0x10048030,
0x00500f01 -> bytes 01 0f 50 00, 0x400, zeros). So a fresh
OMG ID's ONLY entropy is those 5 bytes (~40 bits, QPC-
seeded) — and this mints the OMG ID image, NOT the seed.

### The omgutil9 interface's mapping slots (OMGUtils.dll)

omgutil9 (export ord 279 @0x6390cd70) returns &static
object 0x6392dd64; its ctor FUN_6390d1d0 installs vtable
0x63923268 and constructs an OmgCriticalSection at +4
(dtor FUN_6390cd20). Slots used by omginit: +0x1c =
FUN_6390e4b0 (registry wrapper family), +0x2c =
FUN_6390ee40 (CreateFileMappingA), +0x44 = FUN_6390f3a0
(delete wrapper family); +0x28 = FUN_6390ed10
(CreateFileMappingW). Both mapping slots are CREATE-ONLY:
 - flag byte DAT_6392dd20 == 0 (direct mode): tail-call
   the KERNEL32 import (verified: 0x6390ee40's direct
   branch calls IAT 0x639221b4 = CreateFileMappingA with
   the caller's six args unchanged; FUN_6390ed10 same
   shape for W).
 - flag set (IPC mode): the call is forwarded to a
   second interface object (FUN_6390da50(&DAT_6392dd20))
   whose vt+0x24 method creates the mapping IN THE
   OWNING PROCESS and returns (handle, already-exists
   flag) — FUN_6390ed10 re-raises the flag as
   SetLastError(0xb7). The payload across the IPC call
   is (handle, protect, size, name) + two out-params:
   NO byte content crosses; the remote side creates
   only. This is a mapping-server indirection (one
   process owns named mappings for all OpenMG clients),
   not a fill channel.
FUN_63913340 (OMGUtils-internal thunk) confirms the slot
semantics: it forwards CreateFileMappingA's exact
signature to vt+0x2c for OMGUtils' own OmgMmap classes.

   OMGUtils.dll, OpcKdr.dll, SST.dll, omgmisc.dll.
 MapViewOfFile only: OmgPdLocker.dll, kdrmcom.dll.

#### Session 24 verdict

**omginit.exe is NOT the seed writer — it is not even a
reader. It is the install/reset utility.** Its only
contact with OMG_RAND_SEED is the -reset mode's
existence probe: create the 20-byte PAGE_READWRITE
mapping; if it already exists, a live session owns it
and the reset ABORTS ("!!" MessageBox); if fresh, close
the handle at once, unwritten. In default mode it never
touches the mapping at all — it (re)derives
OMGKEY\omg_id.dat from the maclist header (or mints a
fresh ID) and syncs procfile\device.sal. **The seed's
provenance is therefore unchanged and sharpened: the
20 bytes exist in exactly one place in this corpus —
inside the maclist DB (the substring FUN_65108330
extracts gates-open).** What remains unknown is the
MOVER on the real road: the component that, inside a
working session, gets those bytes (or session-fresh
equivalents) into the mapping before salwrap's openCore
validates them. In-corpus suspects by capability
(CreateFileMappingA + MapViewOfFile importers that a
session loads: SST.dll, omgmisc.dll, OpcKdr.dll,
MemStick.dll) hold no plaintext name — the name can
arrive as a parameter (notably via the OMGUtils IPC
mapping server, whose clients pass names at runtime).
Prime suspect by architecture remains the app layer
(Omgjbox.exe / the SonicStage session process), which
is NOT in this corpus.
BLOCKER, restated at its new precision: the seed-writer
hunt is exhausted inside the OpenMG corpus — both
string-holders (salwrap, omginit) are create/open-only
for the mapping, and no other corpus binary names it.
Next directions (future sessions, main agent's call):
(a) obtain/search the SonicStage app-layer binaries
(Omgjbox.exe and its folder) for the string / mapping
writes — off-corpus material; (b) desk-check the four
capability suspects (SST/omgmisc/OpcKdr/MemStick) for
CreateFileMappingA call sites whose name argument is a
parameter rather than a literal — a parameter-fed site
is a live candidate, a literal-only set closes them;
(c) the gates-open counterfactual remains the only road
past op 4, its invoke-#5 frontier still open there.
Easter eggs: NONE new claimed. Recorded curiosity: the
-reset already-exists MessageBox text is the literal
string "!!" (0x4043ec) — a placeholder-looking stub in
shipping code; reported for the main agent to judge.
No runs this session; mirror untouched; corpus files
read-only throughout (all analysis on copies under
scratch/omginit/).

### Omgjbox.exe — call-site verdicts (Ghidra, copies in scratch/s25/)

Import+analysis of the 5,961,272 B exe took ~9 min
headless; decompilations in scratch/s25/decomp-jbox1..3.c.
Every CreateFileMapping/OpenFileMapping NAME in the
binary is now resolved by construction:

Create side (only THREE direct sites, all wrappers):
- FUN_0073eb70/FUN_0073e9f0 = a generic A/W wrapper pair
  (all six args forwarded; when an internal flag is set,
  creation is forwarded to an interface object's vt+0x3c
  which creates the mapping in another process and
  returns an already-exists flag re-raised as
  SetLastError(0xb7) — the SAME mapping-server shape as
  OMGUtils' IPC mode, Session 24). Name = caller's
  param_6. Its eight A-side callers resolve to:
  * FUN_0052b140: literal "SonicStage_CdrMediumInit",
    size 0x10, view memset to ZERO on create (open-half
    of the same function opens it 0xf001f). CD-burn
    handshake struct. NOT the seed.
  * FUN_00538a30: literal "OpenMG Jukebox2 by sony",
    size 0x20 — an app-presence marker (callers only
    test handle != 0). NOT the seed.
  * FUN_0053f6e0: literal
    "SonySonicStageMiniModeWindowHandle", size 0x20;
    writes ONE DWORD (a window handle) via a
    FILE_MAP_WRITE view. NOT the seed.
  * FUN_0053fed0: literal "SonyUSBSircsWindowHandle",
    size 0x20; READS one dword. NOT the seed.
  * FUN_005a2fc0: literal "Sony KSWServ WindowHandle",
    size 0x20; READS one dword. NOT the seed.
  * FUN_0053ff80 / FUN_00540060: name built by
    CString::Format with the literal format "%d"
    (DAT_00822660) from a numeric parameter — a
    per-instance numeric channel; one reads a dword,
    the other strcpy's a string into the view. A
    decimal-formatted name cannot be "OMG_RAND_SEED".
  * FUN_0052bf60: pure (name,size) passthrough; its
    sole meaningful caller FUN_0052c130 passes literal
    "SONICSTAGE_CONTENT_DATA", size 0x3e90 (16,016 B)
    — the content-data shared struct. NOT the seed.
- FUN_00649ea0 (the 0x649 helper): CreateFileMappingA
  (INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE, world-accessible SD,
  PAGE_READWRITE, size=param_1, name=param_2) + MVOF
  ALL_ACCESS. SOLE caller FUN_00649a10, which creates a
  mapping named <locker-name>+"Name" (special case:
  "Sony_OpenMGImportLocker" -> "Sony_OpenMGImporterName")
  and STRCPY'S ITS OWN NAME STRING (param_1,
  strlen+1 bytes) into it; the sibling FUN_00649ba0
  READS the peer's name-string back out of the same
  <X>Name mapping (OpenFileMappingA access 4,
  MapViewOfFile access 4). Guarded by the wait object
  "Sony_OmgBootCheckerWait1" (also seen in Omg1to2/
  Omgbkup strings). This is the OpenMG import-lock
  identity-announcement channel: variable-size,
  NUL-terminated app names. NOT the seed (wrong size
  discipline, wrong content kind).
Open side: all eight OpenFileMappingA sites resolve to
literals: "DFS_CONTENT_DATA" x6 (FUN_0050b0d0,
FUN_0050bca0, FUN_0052c110, FUN_0057a1c0, FUN_005b29c0,
and the Ghidra-undefined-function site @0x4bfe16 —
resolved by an aligned capstone sweep: push
"DFS_CONTENT_DATA" @0x4bfe06 feeds it) — ALL with
access 4 (FILE_MAP_READ): READERS of a content struct
created elsewhere; "SonicStage_CdrMediumInit" open
branch (above); the <X>Name reader (above).
View writes in the whole exe: memset-zero 16 B
(CdrMediumInit), one dword (MiniMode HWND), one
strcpy of an app-name string (<X>Name), one strcpy
into the numeric channel. NO 20-byte mapping is ever
created or opened; NO file/DB buffer is ever copied
into a view; nothing resembling FUN_65108330's seed-
substring extraction exists on a mapping path.
**Omgjbox.exe VERDICT: NOT the mover.** The session
owner uses named mappings for presence markers, window
handles, an import-lock name board, and content-data
structs — never for 20 bytes of key material, and the
name "OMG_RAND_SEED" is constructed NOWHERE in it.

### The three utilities + SSAAD (Ghidra, scratch/s25/decomp-{oma,1to2,bkup,ssaad}*.c)

ALL THREE utilities statically link the SAME two library
pieces found in Omgjbox:
 (1) the "boot-checker name board" pair — create method
     (size 150; CreateFileMappingA(INVALID, world SD,
     PAGE_READWRITE, size=param, name=param) + MVOF
     ALL_ACCESS) and the <X>Name reader (size 347;

     called ONLY from each app's publish/read-name
     method (size 277; OMG2OMA/Omgbkup FUN_00403550,
     Omg1to2 FUN_00401e90), byte-equivalent to Omgjbox's
     FUN_00649a10: it publishes the app's OWN NAME STRING
     (strlen+1 bytes) into the <locker>Name mapping or
     reads the peer's name back. Wrong content kind,
     wrong size discipline — never 20 binary bytes.
 (2) the ATL-style A/W CreateFileMapping wrapper pair
     (W size 365, A size 263, vt+0x3c forwarding when a
     flag is set — same mapping-server shape as
     OMGUtils' IPC mode). In OMG2OMA (W FUN_00436150 /
     A FUN_004362d0) and Omgbkup (W FUN_0043ee20 /
     A FUN_0043efa0) the pair is UNREACHABLE: a raw E8
     rel32 scan finds ZERO calls to the A wrapper (the W
     wrapper's only call is from inside the A wrapper),
     and neither wrapper's address appears anywhere as
     data (no vtable/pointer use). Dead library code,
     linked wholesale. Omg1to2.exe does not contain the
     wrapper pair at all (its only CFMA site is the
     name-board create).
**OMG2OMA.exe, Omg1to2.exe, Omgbkup.exe VERDICTS: NOT
the mover.** Their only live mapping traffic is the
import-lock name board (app identity strings).
SSAAD.exe: its single OpenFileMappingA site is
FUN_004065b0, a pure EXISTENCE PROBE —
OpenFileMappingA(4,0,name); CloseHandle; return
handle!=0 — and the exe imports no MapViewOfFile, so
it cannot touch any mapping's bytes. **NOT the mover**
(same probe species as omginit's -reset check).

### The name question, answered generally (goal 3)

No site in ANY examined binary constructs, loads,
decrypts, formats, or receives the string
"OMG_RAND_SEED". The app layer never references
OMGUtils/omgutil9 at all (no import, no literal
LoadLibrary/GetProcAddress strings in any of the
five) — the app and OpenMG layers share NO interface
object; the app layer's mapping use is raw KERNEL32
for its own channels. A "mover receives the name as a
call parameter from salwrap-side code" architecture
would require a caller who possesses the name; the
only possessors in all delivered material are
salwrap.dll and omginit.exe, and neither calls into
any examined binary's mapping code (salwrap's mapping
traffic is its own openCore/validate/8ea0 cluster via
OMGUtils slots; omginit only probes). Inside native
code, the ONLY write of the DB seed bytes into the
mapping that exists anywhere in the delivered corpus
is salwrap's own gates-open 8ea0 direct path
(Session 21) — unreachable on the real road
(Session 23).

#### Session 25 verdict

**No writer is identifiable in the app layer either.**
Every CreateFileMapping/OpenFileMapping call site in
both delivered folders (app folder: 4 capable exes +
SSAAD; AVLib: 7 capable DLLs + 2 create-only services)
is resolved: presence markers, window handles,
name boards, content structs, device lock/heartbeat
trios, a path-named DB header block, an event ring.
None creates or opens a 20-byte mapping; none writes
DB-derived bytes into a view; none knows the name.
The hunt is restated at its new precision — the
mover, if it is native code at all, is in one of:
 (a) A .ocm SAL module OTHER than maclist/atpcd/
     device.sal (~dozens in the corpus; only those
     three modules' invoke streams have ever been
     observed). A SAL op would have to reach a salwrap
     host function that fills the mapping; the known
     mapping-touching salwrap functions are the
     openCore/FUN_65116210/FUN_65108ea0 cluster, so
     this requires either an un-censused salwrap host
     entry or a module that drives 8ea0's environment
     differently. Disfavored but not excludable from
     app-layer evidence.
 (b) The OMGUtils IPC mapping-server's HOST process:
     the server creates mappings (zero-filled) for
     clients (Session 24: payload carries no content).
     Filling still needs a client-side view — all
     client-side viewers are now accounted for — OR
     an un-censused omgutil9 vtable slot that transfers
     content server-side (the vtable's non-mapping
     slots were never enumerated; OMGUtils.dll does
     import MapViewOfFile for its own OmgMmap
     classes). Named residual: census the full
     omgutil9 vtable + identify the server host
     process on a real install.
 (c) Material in NEITHER delivered folder (the full
     Sony install tree beyond Sony Shared\OpenMG,
     Sony Shared\AVLib and the SonicStage app folder —
     e.g. further Sony Shared components, add-ons, or
     the ATRAC CD burn engine's own binaries if they
     live elsewhere). Both delivered folders are now
     exhausted; any next material request should name
     the whole C:\Program Files (x86)\Sony and
     C:\Program Files (x86)\Common Files\Sony Shared
     trees as the census boundary.
 (d) The seed is written by salwrap ITSELF on the
     real road through a path the harness has not
     reproduced — e.g. ensure-init under a REAL

     from another module having already run, registry

     its registry/process model; Sessions 22-23
     bounded that model's gate inputs, not its whole

Easter eggs: NONE new. (A light string pass over all

## Session 26 — 2026-10-30 (desk only): DOOR (b) — full omgutil9 vtable census + IPC mapping-server hunt

Question: Session 23 proved salwrap's ensure-init never materializes the
20-byte seed on the real road (gate #2 fails). Door (b): does OMGUtils'
omgutil9 interface — the one doorway omginit and salwrap both use — expose
any slot or internal path that WRITES caller-supplied bytes into a file
mapping, and which process hosts its IPC server side?

### Step 0 — brief claims verified against the binary
- OMGUtils.dll located: corpus `batch2/Sony Shared/OpenMG/OMGUtils.dll`,
  208,896 B, file date Nov 28 2007, **MD5 2f9d56d4339dec3bfe3c18efe84561c2**.
- ImageBase **0x63900000** — brief claim CORRECT (pefile).
- Export `omgutil9` @ **0x6390CD70** returns `&DAT_6392dd64` — the static
  object @0x6392dd64 is CORRECT (file bytes there are zero; populated by

### Step 1b — every slot decompiled (out/decomp-s26-vt.c, out/decomp-s26-missing.c)

The ctor question is settled first: **FUN_6390d1d0 IS the ctor** (brief
claim CORRECT) — it writes `*this = &vtable_63923268` (Ghidra names the
table after slot 0's target) and constructs an OmgCriticalSection member
at +4. FUN_6390cd20 is the destructor (only tears down that member);
FUN_63920fb0 calls it (atexit-style cleanup).

The interface is a **Win32 file/registry/mapping API virtualization
shim**: each slot mirrors one API's exact signature. W slots do the work;
A slots convert the name A->W (FUN_6390a5d0) and delegate through the
object's own vtable to the W slot. Two modes: if the SessionHolder at
DAT_6392dd20 is uninitialized (first byte 0), W slots call the real Win32
API directly in-process; if initialized, they forward to a session object
(see Step 3). Slot table with purposes:

### Step 2 — Q2 verdict: NO content-write path exists in the interface

Decisive facts, all from the Step 1b decompilations:
- The ONLY mapping slots are the two CreateFileMapping mirrors. The
  forwarded (session) form passes exactly (file handle, protect, size
  hi/lo, name, &outHandle, &alreadyExists) — the Session 24 payload
  finding stands, now with full parameter visibility: **no source
  buffer, no size-of-content, no offset**.
- No slot wraps MapViewOfFile or OpenFileMapping. The two registry READ
  slots (+0x68/+0x6C) never even traverse the session — +0x68 is a bare
  import thunk to the local RegQueryValueExW.
- Inside OMGUtils, MapViewOfFile is used only by the OmgMmap helper
  classes (exported as C++ classes: ctors + addr()/data()/at()/size()),
  i.e. OMGUtils's mapping-content model is: the CALLER instantiates
  OmgMmap (or maps a view itself) and writes through the returned
  pointer IN ITS OWN PROCESS. The omgutil9 interface never moves bytes.
- The A-variant slots do pass byte content around locally (A->W name
  conversion buffers) — names only, never mapping content.

**Q2 answer: NO. No omgutil9 slot, and no OMGUtils-internal path
reachable from the interface, writes caller-supplied bytes into a file
mapping. 20 seed bytes cannot arrive through this interface. Whoever
fills OMG_RAND_SEED must hold a view of it — obtained outside omgutil9
(OmgMmap linked from OMGUtils, or its own MapViewOfFile import).**

### Step 4 — how the SessionHolder arms, and OMGUtils's content model

The holder is a static object too: initializer FUN_63920da0 (CRT init
table @0x6392d014) runs ctor FUN_6390d720 at DLL load and registers the
dtor via atexit. The ctor sets byte 0 (the mode flag every slot tests):

    armed (byte0 = 1)  <=>  omgutil(4) != 0  AND  omgutil(1) < 4

omgutil(4) = (level() > 3) — the same OS-level function family as
salwrap's gate #1: satisfied on Vista+. omgutil(1) = FUN_6391a2b0()
(small getter; its source not read this session — the one open detail
in the arming predicate). The decision is made ONCE, at OMGUtils load,
in every process that loads the DLL.
A capstone scan of .text found NO absolute-address store to
0x6392dd20 — all 40+ references are the slots' flag reads / `mov ecx,
holder` this-passing, consistent with ctor-only initialization.

OmgMmap check (FUN_639133b0, the file-mapping ctor variant, decompiled
in Session 24's batch): opens the file (via omgutil9 slot +0x08 or
direct), creates the mapping (via slot +0x2C wrapper FUN_63913340 or
direct CreateFileMappingA), then calls **MapViewOfFile in the client
process itself** and stores the view pointer in the object. Content is
whatever the object's owner later writes through addr()/data(). This is
OMGUtils's only content pattern, and it is a client-side class, NOT
part of the omgutil9 interface.

### Step 1 (desk) — omgutil(1) IDENTIFIED: a process-token privilege class

New decompilations: out/decomp-s27-omg1.c (FUN_6391a2b0,
FUN_63919d40, FUN_63919f60), out/decomp-s27-omg2.c
(FUN_6391a1a0), out/decomp-s27-omg3.c (FUN_63919de0,
FUN_63919e40, FUN_63919ea0, FUN_63919ee0). Raw capstone disasm
of FUN_6391a2b0 confirms the return path (EAX = cached global).

- FUN_63919d40 ("level", cached in DAT_6392dea0): GetVersionExA;
  platformId != 2 (non-NT) -> 1; major < 5 -> 2; major == 5 -> 3;
  major >= 6 -> 4. (omgutil(4) = level > 3; omgutil(3) = level > 2;
  omgutil(0) = level != 1 — dispatcher FUN_6391a2e0 verified.)
- FUN_6391a2b0 (42 B): if level != 1 and cache DAT_6392dea8 == 0,
  call FUN_6391a1a0(&local8); cache and return local8[0].
- FUN_6391a1a0 (272 B) — the classifier: OpenProcessToken
  (TOKEN_QUERY=8), GetTokenInformation(TokenGroups), then:
  * FUN_63919de0(0x220) builds S-1-5-32-544 (AllocateAndInitializeSid,
    NT authority, subauth 32 + RID) = BUILTIN\Administrators;
    FUN_63919e40 membership test TRUE -> class 4.
  * else FUN_63919ea0(...,0x223) S-1-5-32-547 Power Users -> 3.
  * else FUN_63919ea0(...,0x221) S-1-5-32-545 Users ->
    class = present ? 2 : 1.
  * Token-query failure (open/alloc/query) -> class 0.
  (Second output byte = FUN_63919ee0: S-1-2-0 LOCAL membership;
  unused by omgutil(1).)
- **FUN_63919e40's membership test requires EqualSid AND
  (Attributes & 4) — SE_GROUP_ENABLED.** Deny-only SIDs do NOT
  count. Consequences under UAC: an ELEVATED admin token
  (Administrators attrs 0x7) classifies 4; the same account's
  NON-elevated filtered token (Administrators deny-only, attrs
  bit 4 clear) falls through to BUILTIN\Users (enabled) -> 2.

**ARMED-ON-HER-MACHINE VERDICT:** holder ctor FUN_6390d720 arms
(byte0=1) iff omgutil(4)!=0 AND omgutil(1)<4, evaluated ONCE at
OMGUtils load in each loading process. On the Win7 SP1 Toshiba,
level = 4 -> arm #1 passes. SonicStage/OpenMG clients run
non-elevated (2007-era asInvoker apps; no elevation in normal
use), so the loading process's class is 2 (Users-enabled) in
every realistic launch configuration — admin account filtered
token and standard account alike. **The SessionHolder IS ARMED
on her machine in normal use; local mode occurs only if an
OpenMG client is run elevated ("Run as administrator"), which
nobody does.** The predicate in plain terms: "Vista+ and not
running with an enabled Administrators token" — i.e. arm for
exactly the ordinary per-user session, where a session service
(PACSPTISVR) should own the shared handles.

Brief-claim check for Step 1: Session 26's arming formula and
all addresses (FUN_6391a2b0, holder @0x6392dd20, ctor
FUN_6390d720) VERIFIED correct against the binary.

### Step 2 (harness archaeology) — the harness runs the holder, but its token answer disarms it

sal_emu165.py source + run165.txt evidence:
- OMGUtils.dll is loaded as REAL code (load_pe at OMG_BASE
  0x63900000) and its REAL entry point is executed by the
  harness's loader emulation ("Run each DLL's real entry point
  (DllMainCRTStartup: _initterm static ctors + the DLL's own
  DllMain)", sal_emu165.py:1664-1670; OMGUtils first, then
  salwrap). **The SessionHolder ctor therefore DOES run in the
  harness** and evaluates the genuine predicate against HLE
  answers.
- Run 165 boot-phase census proves the classifier ran at load:
  OpenProcessToken retaddr=0x6391a1c8 and GetTokenInformation
  (TokenGroups) retaddr=0x6391a1f0/0x6391a217 — all inside
  FUN_6391a1a0 (run165.txt:37323-37325). CreateFileMappingA x2
  + MapViewOfFile x2 in the same boot window = openCore's local
  create/view.
- HLE answers today: GetVersionExA -> Win7 SP1 (level 4, arm #1
  passes). **h_GetTokenInformation class 2 returns groups
  {Administrators (S-1-5-32-544) attrs 7, Users attrs 7}**
  (sal_emu165.py, the run-95 fix) — attrs 7 has SE_GROUP_ENABLED
  set, so FUN_6391a1a0 returns **class 4** -> omgutil(1) = 4 ->
  predicate 4 < 4 FALSE -> **holder byte0 = 0: LOCAL mode**.
  The harness models the process as an ELEVATED admin; the real
  SonicStage process is a UAC-filtered token (class 2, ARMED).
  This single HLE answer is the whole reason every harness run
  to date walked the local road.
- CoCreateInstance in run165.txt: **0 occurrences** — the
  factory was never contacted, consistent with local mode
  end-to-end. The HLE h_CoCreateInstance returns
  REGDB_E_CLASSNOTREG (0x80040154) unconditionally; per the
  FUN_6390ed10/FUN_6390da50/FUN_6390d870 decompilations there
  is NO local fallback in armed mode: worker throws
  OMGException "CreateInstance(IOmgHandleFactory): ...",
  get() propagates, the slot returns NULL/throws. **So the
  current harness cannot represent the armed road at all:**
  its token answer disarms the holder, and if armed by force
  its COM answer hard-fails the handshake.
- Mapping model (h_CreateFileMappingA/W, h_MapViewOfFile):
  fresh handle per call, NO name table, NO already-exists
  signal, and a pagefile view = a FRESH ZEROED allocation per
  MapViewOfFile call. Cross-process/cross-open persistence of
  mapping content is not modeled; only same-view-pointer reads
  (openCore's stored view) are self-consistent. For the armed
  road this matters: PACSPTISVR-side creation + another
  component's fill cannot be represented without a named-
  mapping store keyed by name.

### Step 3 (desk) — salwrap's trajectory DOES branch on the create: the pre-existing fast path

New decompilations: out/decomp-s27-sal.c (FUN_65103dc0
openCore, FUN_6510ca30 ensure-init, FUN_65108ea0, FUN_65116210,
FUN_651161b0), out/decomp-s27-sal2.c (FUN_651039d0,
FUN_6510a950), out/decomp-s27-mmap.c / -mmap2.c / -mmap3.c
(OmgMmap ctor 0x639143d0, FUN_63914360, FUN_63914090).

THE FLAG. openCore FUN_65103dc0(this, &flag): if the singleton
core is already open -> flag=false. Else FUN_651039d0 builds
OmgMmap(0x14, "OMG_RAND_SEED", &flag) — salwrap imports the
OmgMmap class from OMGUtils (ctor/addr/size/dtor in its IAT;
NO direct mapping imports, Session 24 confirmed). Inside
FUN_63914090 (impl init):
- on NT (omgutil(0)!=0) it builds a NULL-DACL (world-access)
  SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES;
- on level>2 (omgutil(3)!=0 — XP and later, incl. Win7) the
  name is rewritten to **"Global\\OMG_RAND_SEED"**;
- create goes through omgutil9 slot +0x2C (CreateFileMappingA,
  INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE, PAGE_READWRITE, size 20, name);
  NULL handle -> throw "CreateFileMapping(%s)";
- **flag := (GetLastError() != 0xb7)** — TRUE = the create was
  FRESH; FALSE = ERROR_ALREADY_EXISTS. (Armed slots set last
  error 0xb7 themselves from the factory's out-byte —
  FUN_6390ed10 — so the contract is identical in both modes.)
- MapViewOfFile is a DIRECT OMGUtils import (FILE_MAP_WRITE),
  view stored in the impl; salwrap reads/writes content only
  through this view.

ENSURE-INIT FUN_6510ca30 (+0xbc state at param_1[0x2f]):
state!=0 -> return; state:=1; omgutil(2) anti-debug check
("Debugger is present" throw); build the 3 maclist paths;
resolve icv.dat (bVar1); openCore(&flag); then:
- **flag == FALSE (mapping PRE-EXISTED, or core already open):
  the ENTIRE verify/migrate/restore block is SKIPPED** — no
  FUN_65116210 validation, no gates, no 8ea0, no (0x50a) —
  straight to the DONE tail: FUN_6510a950 finalize, state:=2.
  Pre-existing content is trusted UNCONDITIONALLY (never even
  read on this path).
- flag == TRUE (fresh): validate = FUN_65116210(core) — a
  dispatcher on the same axes (Vista+ -> FUN_65115de0; pre-
  Vista low-class -> FUN_65115ff0; non-NT -> FUN_65115c80). If
  validation != 0 -> DONE tail directly. If == 0 and icv.dat
  resolved: gates evaluated — gate #1 FUN_65103a20 AND gate
  #2 FUN_65114d50 != 0 -> out-pointer planted (points at a
  local whose [0] was preset 1); else out stays NULL. Then
  FUN_65108ea0(this, out):
  * 8ea0 loops maclist.dat/maclist1.dat/maclist2.dat; per
    existing file, FUN_65108330(file image, icv image)
    extracts (success + seedsize). SUB-PATH (out==NULL ||
    seedsize==0): write processed pair to restorable/,
    return 1. DIRECT (out planted, seedsize!=0): copy the
    20 extracted bytes into the core view (FUN_65103e50),
    validate FUN_651161b0 (Vista+ -> FUN_65115b20; pre-Vista
    class<3 -> hardcoded 0 -> the (0x307) "poi poi poi poi"
    throw), write both files back live, *out:=1, return 1.
    No file extracts -> "restorable pair not found", ret 0.
  * 8ea0 returns TRUE: out==NULL (or *out==0) -> FUN_651048d0
    closeCore + throw (0x50a) "restore ICV" — **run 165's
    death, reproduced structurally line-for-line.** out
    planted -> omglog "vista migration ok" -> DONE tail.
  * 8ea0 returns FALSE -> FUN_65108110 restore block +
    FUN_65107d70 verify + "mac-list newly created" -> DONE.

HOLDER-VISIBILITY: salwrap cannot distinguish armed from
local — same slot, same handle + last-error contract. The
armed road changes salwrap's trajectory ONLY by making
flag==FALSE reachable in practice: the factory creates the
Global\ object server-side, so it can pre-date salwrap's
first create in the process (created for an earlier client,
or held open by PACSPTISVR across client lifetimes). In a
single cold local process the first create is fresh by
construction — which is why every harness run walked the
fresh road.

**WATCH REAPPRAISAL (flagged):** the real-machine seed watch
cited in Session 23 polled the mapping by the bare name
"OMG_RAND_SEED" (per the main agent's watcher description).
The object created on any XP+ system is named
**"Global\\OMG_RAND_SEED"** — a poll of the bare name in the
caller's local namespace CANNOT see the Global\ object. The
watch's negative result is therefore inconclusive as

### Step 6 — Session 27 bottom line

1. omgutil(1) = FUN_6391a1a0's process-token privilege class
   (4 = enabled Administrators in token groups; 3 = Power
   Users; 2 = Users; 1 = none; 0 = query failure), gated on
   SE_GROUP_ENABLED so UAC deny-only SIDs do not count.
   ARMED on Lola's Win7 Toshiba in every realistic launch
   (non-elevated OpenMG clients -> class 2 < 4, level 4):
   the factory road is her machine's DEFAULT road; local
   mode is the elevated-launch exception.

   entry-point execution (holder ctor runs; classifier
   observed in run 165's boot census), but its TokenGroups
   HLE models an ELEVATED token (Administrators attrs 7) ->
   class 4 -> holder disarmed -> every harness run to date
   walked the local road. Its CoCreateInstance HLE
   (REGDB_E_CLASSNOTREG) could not serve an armed holder
   anyway (worker throws; no fallback exists in the guest).

### Step A1 — byte diffs (DONE, desk)

- maclist.dat: IDENTICAL in every generation (3a353016).
  Migration never touches it.
- maclist1.dat form-1 -> form-2: ZERO bytes differ in the
  common 21,420. Form-2 = form-1 + a 20-byte trailer:
  dwords {6, 0, 7, 4} + a big-endian generation counter
  (00 00 00 01). Form-2 -> form-3: exactly ONE byte differs —
  the generation counter 1 -> 2. The extracted seed is NOT
  present in plaintext in any form (derived, not stored).
- icv.dat: 20 bytes, wholly different in each generation
  (9cc59bd5... / 30e77690... / 434ff83e...) — a full rewrap
  per migration pass.
- restorable/maclist.dat (the 8ea0 sub-path's processed image,
  21,428 B) = pristine maclist1.dat's 21,420 bytes + an 8-byte
  trailer {6, 0} — the FIRST TWO dwords of the migration
  trailer. (0x53b4 = 21,428 matches the Session 20-era record
  of the VM reply's embedded image size.) restorable/icv.dat
  is a distinct 121-byte processed object.
So the entire on-disk pair-state delta across generations is:
(a) trailer stamps appended to maclist1.dat ({6,0} from VM DB
processing; {7,4,gen} added by ca30's migration write-back,
gen bumping per pass), and (b) icv.dat's 20-byte rewrap.

### Step A2 — FUN_65108840 / FUN_65109380 code read (DONE, desk)

Decomps: out/decomp-key.c (8840) and out/decomp-s27-9380.c.
FUN_65109380 (ProcessFile helper): holds 3 candidate filenames
at this+0x30 (stride 0x1c; rotation starts at (existing+2)%3 —
maclist1.dat first per Session 27's trace). For each existing
candidate: readFromFile -> content string; seed =
FUN_65103e50(this); calls FUN_65108840(content, seed, 0x14,
&migrated_flag). TRUE -> if migrated_flag, FUN_65107d70 writes
the content back; return (verify satisfied). All three FALSE
-> if icv.dat resolves AND FUN_65108ea0(this, NULL) returns
TRUE (sub-path: restorable/ written) -> throw (0x50a)
"restore ICV2" tag 0x173; else throw (0x302) "OpenMG oh my
god!" tag 0x175.
FUN_65108840(content, seedbuf, 0x14, *flag): *flag := 0.
Empty content -> FALSE. Dispatches on the content's first 8
bytes vs the constant at DAT_651395f0 — VERIFIED in
salwrap.dll this session: the 8 bytes are exactly
"MACLIST\0" (both maclist files carry it; icv.dat does not):
- MACLIST branch: loads maclist.ocm (FUN_65101730/1a80),
  builds ONE typed-stream invoke carrying the content string
  and the 20-byte seed, runs it via the manager (vt+0x64);
  TRUE iff the invoke's status int == 0. *flag stays 0.
- ICV branch (content lacks the magic): loads icv.ocm, runs
  an invoke (stream kind 10), then FUN_65103cd0, then a
  second maclist.ocm invoke (kind 6), FUN_65107f20(content)
  (rewrite), and sets *flag = 1 — i.e. this branch MIGRATES
  legacy content and reports it via the flag, whereupon 9380
  writes it back with FUN_65107d70.
So 8840's verdict is a maclist.ocm VM-op status over
(image, seed): the checked quantity lives inside the SAL op,
not in native code. Native evidence bounds it: the op's
inputs are exactly the file image and the 20-byte seed; the
only generation-varying inputs are the image trailer, the
icv.dat rewrap (9380 never passes icv.dat to the maclist
branch — but the VM op may read files itself), and which
generation's seed is live. A-runs below discriminate.

### Step A3 — what migrates the pair on a real machine (desk)

The write-back is performed by ca30's DIRECT path: gate #1
(FUN_65103a20) AND gate #2 (FUN_65114d50) grant the out-pointer
(pcVar7), FUN_65108ea0 processes the pair through the SAL VM
(8330 extracts the seed from the processed image), and the
migrated image + rewrapped icv.dat land on disk (Session 21/22
mechanics; decomp-s27-sal.c ca30 flow re-read this session).
On the faithful road gate #2 = 0, so that path never runs —
and NOTHING ELSE in the censused material performs the
migration write-back: omginit only resets; the omgutil9
interface and its PACSPTISVR factory are create-only
(Session 26). The remaining designed producer of a processed
pair on the real road is the RESTORE loop itself: 8ea0's
sub-path writes the VM-processed image to restorable/
(restored image = maclist1 content + the {6,0} stamp), the
(0x50a) signals the caller to retry, and a subsequent attempt
is the only place a stamped image can reach the live pair.
Whether 8840 accepts the {6,0}-stamped image (vs requiring
the full {6,0,7,4,gen} migration trailer) is exactly what the
continuation run below tests.
CORRECTION to the brief's shorthand: FUN_65108110 is not the

### Step B4 — catch-delivery diagnosis (run 166 log, pinned)

Run 166's (0x50a) "restore ICV2" WAS captured (run166.txt:
39129, retaddr 0x651094d9 inside 9380). _cxx_dispatch then
walked the fs:[0] chain: frames[0..6] sane (9380 state 7;
salwrap caller; five pfcom frames — ALL with nTryBlocks=0,
so no catch in the valid prefix). frame[7] @0xf3fe634 has
handler=0x2d95, state=0x65136520 (data, not a frame) and sits
BELOW frame[0] — a stale registration from an earlier
execution epoch whose memory is now VM stream data;
frame[8] @0x65145a20 is inside salwrap's image (data). The
next chain read raised UcError(2179763033, 4), which
propagated UNGUARDED out of _cxx_dispatch and aborted the
whole phase ("run152 factory vt+8 failed: UcError(...)").
Runs 164/165 died of the same family (chain nodes aliasing
VM stream buffers / heap). A genuine fs:[0] chain is
stack-resident and strictly ascending toward the stack base;
the corruption is a harness epoch artifact (chunked
emu_start boundaries never re-validate fs:[0]).
FIX (sal_emu167.py): harden the _cxx_dispatch walk — a node
outside [STACK_BASE, STACK_BASE+STACK_SIZE) or not strictly
greater than its predecessor ENDS the walk (logged), and all
per-node reads are guarded; an unreadable node likewise ends
it. No-match in the valid prefix then returns None -> the
pre-157 escape end-state, instead of a Unicorn abort.
h_cxxthrow_capture's diagnostic walk was already guarded.

### Step 3 — Encrypt call-shape reconstruction (desk, in progress)
Run 111's ERR_TYPE, with Sessions 8-10 hindsight: the
transplant record was [ref(60, host-fn-table), i1(2),
ref(atpcd image), i1(0)] and the engine raised ERR_TYPE
(kind 0x14402010) "in Encrypt". The driver grammar (from
decomp-omgatpcd.c writer thunks: 6160488c->vt+0x70 write-
buffer-ref, 6160486f->vt+0x74 write-int, 616048be->vt+0x64
write-module, 616048a5->vt+0x6c write-octet) shows what
atpcd selector 2 actually receives from the real driver:
FUN_61604e96 (media-info) = [buffer-ref(repo-file content),
int(2), module("atpcd.ocm")] and it RETURNS byte+string+
int+int (string must memcmp-match the 16-B content ID).
So selector 2's arg0 is a buffer-ref to a FILE IMAGE whose
content the op parses — run 111/112 fed it a 60-byte host
function table (nonsense content) and refs where the real
driver passes the module BY NAME. The state-free probes
(runs 115-121) further showed SEL=2/TAIL=1 records are
accepted and merely MATERIALIZE their last argument with
no data work — the landed shape was an open/select frame,
not the work frame. What atpcd's Encrypt wants beyond the
selector-2 media-info frame is NOT in omgatpcd's three
decompiled methods (OP-A sel 0, BIND sel 1, media-info
sel 2) — full omgatpcd function census next (Ghidra),
hunting the method that builds the bulk stream.

- +0x3c FUN_647102e7 (847 B): permission-flags computer. If
  ProcessFile+0x3d0 >= 0 returns it cached; else builds a
  bitmask from ProcessFile fields (+0x114 flag bits, +0x140

  window via time() vs +0x78-string property 8 through self

  caches the mask at ProcessFile+0x3d0 and returns it.
- +0x74 FUN_64710636: NOT a standalone Ghidra function — it is
  the byte immediately after FUN_647102e7's body (0x647102e7 +
  847 = 0x64710636); raw disassembly shows a fresh prologue:
  switch on arg index 0..0x22 (35 cases, jump table
  0x647109ed) returning int fields of ProcessFile. It is the
  session's INT-PROPERTY GETTER. The driver's probe
  vt+0x74(0xe) -> 0xffffffff reads the index-0xe field, which
  is -1/unset for the Ignite ProcessFile.
- +0x78 FUN_64714caf (976 B): "ProcessFile::getBinaryProperty"

  string field; case 6/7 iterate lists at +0x224/+0x230;

  +0x1b8 string; case 0x10 builds a 0x13-B blob via
  FUN_6470f41c. Default: OMGException (0xc)
  "ProcessFile::getBinaryProperty".
- +0x68 FUN_6470f9ae (411 B, read this step): calls SELF
  vt+0x18 with selector 3 (GET_OCMENC_CKEY), then requires

### Step 2 — invoker route reconstructed (desk, DONE)
- FUN_6512c840 (engine-decomp.c:11817, 934 B, __cdecl) =
  (vmctx, bytes_ptr, bytes_len, outstr_ptr). It validates
  vmctx against a 0x40-entry table of registered VM objects
  (&DAT_6516b178) — unknown ctx -> error 0x14404022 via
  FUN_65120160, no execution; ctx state field (+8): 0 ->
  0x14404022, 1 -> 0x14404023 (busy), 2 -> claims the VM
  (stores tid, state := 1). It then builds the parameter
  block ON ITS STACK: {next = 0, executor = vmctx,
  bytes_ptr, bytes_len, outstr_ptr}, points Dr1 at the
  block (sign-flip encoded, Dr0 = sign flag), Dr3 at the
  generated stub, and calls (Dr3 ^ 0x7fffffff)
  (FUN_6512cbf0, vmctx). CORRECTION to the brief's framing:
  there is NO "spec dword0" at the c840 level — dword0
  lives in b650's spec struct and is unwrapped by
  FUN_651349f0 before c840 is reached. For module
  execution the (bytes, len) image is the ENTIRE input.
- The two 30-B thunks (engine-decomp.c, verified):
  FUN_651349f0(p, l, out) -> c840(*DAT_6516d484, ...) =
  MAIN VM (the b650/salexec invoker; querycif.ocm executes
  here under pfcom's streams). FUN_65134a10(p, l, out) ->
  c840(*DAT_6516d488, ...) = INIT VM (bootstrap's module
  invoker).
- Bootstrap's exact sequence for init.ocm AND region.ocm
  (c8a0 body, decomp-key.c + run-172 live trace):
  (1) fetch by name via FUN_6511b160 (thiscall, ecx =
  0x6516b080 the global cache object, (out_rec, name
  OmgString)) -> (data ptr, size) — pure fetch + cache
  registration, NO parsing; (2) FUN_65134a10(ptr, len,
  &outstr) executes the image in the init VM; results land
  in the out std::string + manager-state side effects.
  Run-172 trace lines confirm verbatim: "fetch-b160:
  this=0x6516b080 name=b'init.ocm'" then "thunk-34a10:
  a1=0x10358b40 a2=0x8e3ec a3=0xf3fef4c" then
  "vminvoke-c840: vmctx=0x103308a8 p2=0x10358b40
  p3=0x8e3ec p4=0xf3fef4c" (vmctx = *DAT_6516d488's
  per-run value, supplied by the thunk itself).
- atpcd.ocm at rest declares NOTHING readable: envelope
  03 02 00 00 | BE16@+4 = 0x0500 | LE32@+6 = 0x00002c10
  (the cross-.ocm format constant) followed by fully
  opaque payload — a strings scan yields only random
  4-char noise (no 'Encrypt', no routine names, no ASN
  tree in the clear; device.sal's clear-text SalAsn
  grammar has no analogue here). The module's parameter
  grammar can therefore only be read from its EXECUTION
  behavior (service requests / VM errors), which is what
  Step 3 observes. Its named routine 'Encrypt' is known
  only from the old lineage's in-VM error report.
- PRE-REGISTERED drive plan for Step 3 (in run 173's s30
  block, all AFTER the live session + Drive A keys):
  (0) fetch "atpcd.ocm" via FUN_6511b160 exactly as
  bootstrap fetches (ecx=0x6516b080); execute the
  FETCHED (ptr, len) — byte-checked == host image.
  (A) FUN_65134a10(fetched, len, outstrA) — the
  bootstrap-identical init-VM execution.
  (B) FUN_651349f0(fetched, len, outstrB) — the main-VM
  form (the VM the burn path's modules execute in).
  Each attempt logs EAX, the out string (size + first
  64 B), and the omglog.txt size delta + new content.
  The harness's standing [tr111] hooks log every c840 /
  thunk / fetch entry with spec previews, so the module's
  own service invokes (if any) appear in run173.txt.

- Header census across all 61 corpus .ocm (this session):
  class "03 01 00 00" modules carry a PLAINTEXT SalAsn
  program at +10 (01 01 04 08 6a 35...): querycif,
  maclist, icv, restore, vmc, netmd, ... — the
  executable-program class. Class "03 02 00 00" modules
  carry an ENCRYPTED payload: atpcd, region, init2,
  ekbman, kdrm, mp4comp, sc, ... init.ocm is a 5-byte
  wrapper around a class-01 program at +5. region.ocm
  (class 02) executes standalone FINE in bootstrap, so
  encryption alone is not the rejection: the rejected
  property is the module's internal type — TID token
  MTDD (vs CDT for programs; both tokens sit in
  salwrap's SML error table at file 0x429fc/0x42a08,
  among ERR_MOD_*/ERR_LYR_* tokens the exception
  message is composed from). VERDICT: atpcd.ocm is a
  LIBRARY module (MTDD). The c840 standalone-image route
  — the way bootstrap runs CDT programs — refuses it by
  design, in either VM. It executes when a program
  stream ADDRESSES it by module record: run 125's OP-A
  ([int 0, module("atpcd.ocm")]) already ran it cleanly.
  (Side note: lgd.ocm shares atpcd's encrypted payload
  PREFIX 4d 47 ca 99 6a b2 but is 26,438 B vs 21,564 B —
  related modules, not copies.)
- The Encrypt door is therefore the typed-stream route
  (the brief's Step 3 fallback shape): the module's
  routines are selected by the records a host stream
  writes before/with the module record (ints 0/1/2 in
  omgatpcd's builders), and — per run 111 — also by a
  NAME string record after the module record, which is
  how 'Encrypt' was entered before its ERR_TYPE.
