### Step 2 — desk: the raise has exactly one host home
- Census of the literal kind 0x14402020 across ALL Ghidra
  decompilations on disk (out/*.c): exactly THREE raise
  sites, all inside ONE function: FUN_65125230
  (decomp-loader.c:1515/1521/1536, calls to the error
  ctor FUN_65120160). FUN_65125230 is slot +0x94 of the
  VM service vtable (out/phase3-refs.txt: +0x38 is
  FUN_65120160 itself) — the frame/service layer through
  which executed programs exchange values with the host.
- FUN_65125230(ctx, dir, slot, buf, len), __cdecl, 251 B
  — a per-process SLOT STORE: table DAT_6516b280, 128
  slots x 0x44 B {length, data[0x40]}, under a critical
  section (DAT_6516e8c0). dir=0 STORE: slot's length :=
  len, data copied in. dir=1 LOAD: copies the slot's
  STORED value out; returns the slot's stored length.
  It has NO static callers in the decompilations — it is
  reached through the vtable by VM-generated worker
  code, which is why it never appeared in call graphs.
- Capstone disassembly of the staged salwrap.dll body
  (aligned from the known entry) pins the three checks
  to their raise RETURN addresses — the hook verdict is
  read off these, not inferred:
  R1 ret 0x65125262: slot not in [0,128) OR dir not in
  {0,1} (arg-range).
  R2 ret 0x65125297: STORE with len > 0x40 (64).
  R3 ret 0x651252f0: LOAD with requested len < the
  slot's stored length (the caller asked for fewer
  bytes than the deposited value holds).
  LOAD of a never-stored slot: stored length 0, no
  raise, returns 0.
- Desk-diff, FUN_6470f9ae (decomp-s29-pfcom.c): beyond
  the stream records, the method reads NOTHING from

  string from the reply stream (FUN_6470b2f1) and
  requires length == 8 (else pfcom's own (0xe)
  "Invalid OMG Header"). So the executed program's

- READER GRAMMAR (route b, from genblock.bin):
  worker 0x10326ebb: tag byte at cursor; type =
  tag & 0x1f; idx = type-1; if idx > 0x17 -> default
  arm. Index bytes at 0x1032706c map types 1..24 to
  FOUR jump targets at 0x1032705c:
  [0]=0x10326ef2 (type 1), [1]=0x10326f39 (type 2),
  [2]=0x10326fbb (types 23,24), [3]=0x10326ff3
  (types 3..22 + default).
  Length reader 0x10326dfb(ctx, &cursor, end):
  needs >= 2 B; reads byte AFTER the tag: if < 0x80
  it IS the length (0..127); if == 0x80 returns -1
  (absent/nil — raises in this worker's arms); else
  (0x80|n) is followed by n BIG-ENDIAN length bytes.
  Bounds-checked against end; violations raise
  0x14402020. Arms:
  * type 1 = BOOL: [01][form byte MUST == 0x01 else
    MALFORMED][flag byte]; returns flag != 0. 3 B.
  * type 2 = TEXT: [02][len][bytes]; NUL-scan helper
    0x1032708b; handle tagged 0x40000000 class.
  * types 3..22 = BLOB/raw: [tag][len][bytes copied
    verbatim]; handle tagged 0x80000000 class.
  * types 23/24 = via primitive 0x1032715b (reads
    tag + length; type 24 demands length >= 12,
    special-cases 15 — a fixed-structure value;
    type 23 branches elsewhere — likely struct).
  Cross-check vs native stream records built by
  FUN_6470f9ae: `04 18 <ckey 24B>` and `04 10 <cid
  16B>` match [tag][len<0x80][payload] exactly.
  The 05-descriptor is execute-machinery addressing,
  not a stream value.
- The read-value worker has exactly ONE call site
  (0x10326dd5, inside a wrapper); op 11's body is a
  continuation machine (blocks jmp to dispatcher
  0x1031ff84). Full static read-sequence recovery is
  impractical; proceed empirically: probe buffers +
  reader/raise hooks to learn the field count and
  each field's expected class, then serialize A0's
  fields accordingly.
- CONTAINER READER 0x10326c3b (the per-field reader
  the program actually calls, via 0x10326c1b):
  the tag byte's HIGH bits are a form class:
  class = (tag >> 6) & 3.
  * class 0, tag & 0x20 set, type (tag & 0x1f) in
    {0x10, 0x11} -> CONTAINER (tags 0x30/0x31):
    [tag][len][element values...]; helper 0x1032764b
    counts elements by scanning, then elements are
    parsed recursively via 0x10326c3b.
  * class 0 otherwise -> scalar via the read-value
    worker 0x10326ebb.
  * class 2 (tag & 0x80) -> raw blob: [tag][len]

    kind to watch for in probes).

  A Python implementation of the grammar
  (scratch/s33/tlv.py) parses it EXACTLY (232/232 B):
  one top-level container 30 80 (-1 length, 00 00
  terminator) with 5 children: int 1002 (tag 02,
  len 2, BE payload 03 ea), two 20-B tag-04 hashes,
  a 22-child container (ints tag 02 minimal-length

  timestamp-shaped; tag-04 20-B values incl. the

  content importing" as tag 04 len 21), and a
  4-child container ("_ESI"/"_OCS" tag-04 keys with
  20-B / 4-B values). So the serializer's encodings:
  int = [02][minlen][BE payload] (0 = [02 01 00]);
  bytes/text = [04][len][payload]; container =
  [30][80][elements][00 00] in the wild (the -1
  terminator form, not explicit length).
  The header struct for op 11 will use these same
  encodings; its field ORDER is the program's.

### Run 191 findings (sal_emu189.py, timeout 900, log run191.txt)
- Harness changes vs 190: compare-operand hook FIXED to the operator's true
  stack slots (desk-verified from 0x10302cc0's own code: operands are
  [vsp-4] top and [vsp-8] second; runs 189/190 read one slot too high, which
  is why their pairs were garbage). Added: compare-RESULT hook at 0x10302d5e,
  a generic operator trace over table idx 35-150, and a FUN_6470b2f1-entry
  dump. Result: vt+0x68 completed, reply EMPTY again (out stays 0xcc),
  vt+0x88 recheck fine. The trace is the story:
- THE COMPARE, captured cleanly (caller = the VM dispatch loop 0x1032047f,
  vsp=0x1047d018): second-pushed = BLOB len 4 `01 00 28 2e` == OUR element[0]
  (hdr[00:04], object 0x103f2630); top-pushed = BLOB len 7 `66 6f 70 65 6e
  4d 47` = ASCII "fopenMG" (object 0x103f27f0). RESULT = -1 (not equal).
- "fopenMG" is a REAL Sony string: OMGUtils.dll file offset 0x231fc holds
  the literal `fopenMG` right after `OMGF: bad version`. It is a pun-name
  (fopen + OMG) -- almost certainly the OpenMG file-open function's NAME.
  (Easter-egg-adjacent: a dev in-joke baked into a shipping DLL; flagged for
  Lola's collection in the session report.)
- The caller 0x1032047f is inside the VM DISPATCH LOOP (genblock 0x10320454-
  0x10320482: fetch opcode byte [ebp-0x29] -> table [ebp-0x24]+op*4+4 ->
  call). The code just above it (0x1032035f-0x10320450) DECRYPTS the next
  opcode byte (xor tables 0x1032e070/0x10330070 + hash call 0x10325d6b,
  state [ebp-0x20]) -- the .ocm bytecode is per-byte encrypted, which is why
  static bytecode reading was futile. Every operator call = one executed VM
  instruction, in order: the operator trace IS the program trace.
- TRUE operator sequence for op 11 (entry-address + dispatch-caller filter;
  mid-instruction and SEH-replay fires discarded): container read of all 8
  elements, then CONCAT(X8,X8) with X8 = `37 cc e3 6a 8b 90 e3 6a` (two LE
  timestamps ~2026-10-31 -- session clock values); op idx100 (CID16, X16)
  with CID16 = `01 0f 50 00 00 04 00 00 00 00 00 16 72 5f 9b e7` (the stream's
  content-ID record: content block with bytes 9-10 zeroed); idx119 (Y16,
  20); idx120 (20, K92) with K92 = the 92-B key blob beginning
  `01 23 45 67 89 ab cd ef fe dc ba 98 76 54 32 10...` (the classic NIST
  test pattern -- EKB key material); idx121 (op3 ckey 24B, K92); idx94
  (0,8),(8,8); idx113 (two derived 8-B blobs); idx124 (T128, `d8`) with
  T128 a 128-B table (bytes < 0x40) and the 1-B blob 0xd8 == BlockSeed[0];
  idx99 (W24, W24), W24 a derived 24-B blob; idx102 (W24, 24); idx119/120/
  121 again (second ckey op on a ZERO 24-B blob; K92 mutated in place --
  it is a key-schedule workspace); idx94 (0,8),(8,8); then the COMPARE
  (elem0 vs "fopenMG") -> -1; then the graceful empty return.
- Interpretation (hypothesis, to test): the program does heavy session-side
  crypto first (independently of our elements), then starts consuming the
  caller's container -- and its FIRST check compares element[0] against the
  NAME "fopenMG". Our element[0] is header bytes, not the name -> mismatch
  -> no-result path. What the faithful input's element[0] should be is the
  open question: a NAME-typed element? The .opf's shape (unnamed positional
  children) argues the real document is positional too -- but no .opf child
  is a name string either. NEXT (run 192): hook ALL table ops idx 0-150
  with the dispatch-caller filter ([esp]==0x1032047f) to get the complete
  instruction trace incl. core ops (container indexing, constant loads,

  headers, data pointers and a value-stack window.

## Session 35 (2026-10-31) — PIN THE SHAPE, LIFT THE CIPHER

Goal: (1) pin the faithful op-11 document byte-exactly from
the program's own element usage + native construction/
parsing evidence; (2) lift the post-gate derivation into a
clean-room spec + Python reference implementation in
scratch/s35/ reproducing the oracle replies for A0-insert
(81 50 39 3f 51 e2 38 48), A0-replace (ce 89 8b 93
fe 60 75 92), A1 (ed ea 35 0d 30 f3 ff 63) and the IV-flip
(dc e3 d5 7d 45 4c 6e 2b) byte-for-byte.
- Startup verification: S33+S34 sections re-read in full;
  candidates re-dumped byte-for-byte (s33 candidate.bin
  112 B; s34 candidate5/5-A1/7 121 B, candidate6 115 B) —
  all match the S34 report. hdr96 re-extracted from
  TRK000A0.ATP [1024:1120]: v4 segmentation covers
  [00:04][04:0c][10:18][18:20][20:34][34:40][40:50]
  [50:60] = 92 of 96 B; the skipped hdr[0c:10] = 00 00
  00 00. A1 differs from A0 in hdr96 ONLY at +0x18..1f
  (BlockSeed) and +0x58..5f (IV) — verified byte-wise.
- DETERMINISM, strengthened: run194 AND run196 logs show
  the SAME session-side X8 blob (37 cc e3 6a 8b 90 e3 6a)
  as run193 — the harness-side pre-gate pipeline is fully
  deterministic across runs, so captured session constants
  (X8, ckey, K92 init, tables) are valid lift inputs for
  oracle validation; their real-world provenance still
  needs naming in the spec.
- NATIVE EVIDENCE (task 1b) — OMGUtils.dll contains a
  fopenMG record parser, function at 0x6390c1c0 (the ONLY
  xref to the "fopenMG" string VA 0x639231fc; neighbouring
  xrefs: "OMGF: bad version" 0x6390c2ac, "OMGF: bad cid
  size" 0x6390c318, "OMGF: invalid su size" 0x6390c471).
  Capstone read (scratch/s35/omgutils_fopen_dis.txt):
  it zeroes a 92-B output struct (0x17 dwords), reads
  stream values via cursor helpers and checks, in order:
  (i) a 7-B value == "fopenMG"; (ii) next value's first
  dword == 1002 or 1003 else "bad version"; (iii) a
  20-B value else "bad cid size" -> struct +0x04;
  (iv) a 2-B value [0x67, b] -> struct +0x18 = b;
  (v) a sub-read at struct +0x1c; (vi) a 2-B value
  [0x68, n] -> struct +0x20 = n, n in 1..4 (jump table);
  (vii) per-case three sub-values (SU descriptors),
  a size computation (0x639019f0; <0 -> "invalid su
  size") and a derivation (0x63902450) whose 24-B
  result lands at struct +0x28. Struct size 0x5C = 92 B
  — the same length as the VM's K92 key blob (link to
  test, not yet a claim). Ghidra decomp of this function
  + helpers is the next desk step.

### Session 35 desk results so far (2026-10-31, verified in-session)

- **digest1 CONFIRMED BY HAND**: SHA1(CID16 XOR (X8||X8)) == BLOB20#1 byte-for-byte,
  CID16 = content block[0:16] with bytes 9,10 zeroed = `01 0f 50 00 00 04 00 00 00 00 00 16 72 5f 9b e7`,
  X8 = `37 cc e3 6a 8b 90 e3 6a` (the two LE timestamp words). NEW16 = `36 c3 b3 6a 8b 94 e3 6a 37 cc e3 7c f9 cf 78 8d`.
- **digest2 is NOT** SHA1(W24'[0:20]), NOT SHA1(W24'), NOT SHA1 of ckey/CID16/digest1/zero
  (13 candidates tried). Its input goes on run 198's capture list (copy119 arg hook).
- **Spec bytes d8 (CBC#1) / d7 (CBC#2) are SESSION CONSTANTS** — identical for A1
  (different BlockSeed/IV) and for the replace shape. NOT document bytes.
- **Padding rule CORRECTED**: the brief's "zero-padded to 128 B" is wrong in general —
  the program pads the document with zeros to a MULTIPLE OF 8 (121 -> 128 pad 7;
  115 -> 120 pad 5; final substring offset = paddedlen - 8: 120 / 112 resp.).
- **CBC#2 consistency check passed**: cipher blob's first 32 B identical across
  runs 193/195/196 wherever the padded doc's first 32 B are identical (CBC, IV = zero8,
  key = expand(digest2[0:8])).
- **Standard FIPS DES does NOT reproduce CBC#1** under (data=ckey24, IV=digest1[0:8],
  key=digest1[8:16]) nor 5 variants (IV=0, key halves swapped, decrypt direction,
  subkey-chunk permutation [6,4,2,0,7,5,3,1], block-reversed data). My DES passes the
  classic test vector, so the implementation is sound; either CBC#1's data arg is not
  the ckey or the VM's DES core is a bit-variant (its subkey storage order
  [6,4,2,0,7,5,3,1] vs standard PC-2 group order hints at a permuted internal layout).
  Run 198 captures exact helper I/O pairs; the variant fit happens offline against them.
- **No genuine OMGF document exists in the delivered corpus** (byte-grep for fopenMG:
  only OMGUtils.dll + our own run logs). TRK0000A.ATP head = `49 44 33` ("ID3") — a
  plain .ATP. The canonical document must be CONSTRUCTED from parseOmgFile's grammar.
- parseOmgFile caveat (ii) sharpened: its input stream is the SAL serialization of an
  OMG FILE (header + content), while vt+0x68's caller buffer in the harness is fed
  RAW to the VM program as ONE blob value (FUN_6470ea66 appends it verbatim; the
  program parses it with the VM container reader). The two grammars agree on tags
  (0x30 container, 0x04 blob, 0x02 int, 0x06 obj-id) — one Sony serialization family.

### Session 35 — Task 1: the faithful document shape (desk evidence)

**What the caller buffer IS in the genuine flow.** FUN_6470f9ae (pfcom, the vt+0x68
method) takes (buf, len, out8) and passes buf VERBATIM into the SAL stream
(FUN_6470ea66 appends it; the executed frame embeds it byte-for-byte — S32).
The method's own error for a wrong-length reply is "Invalid OMG Header": the
buffer is an OMG HEADER serialization. The 96-B .ATP secure header is the only
"OMG header" in the track pipeline, and the harness's S29–S34 work fed exactly
its struct-form serialization. So the faithful document = the struct-form
serialization of the 96-B secure header, built by the (undelivered) burn host
with the standard Sony serializer — the same serializer family that produced
the track's .opf (S33: the .opf parses exactly under the VM grammar).

**What the program itself consumes (S34 traces, runs 191–197):**
- element[0]: blob-compared against "fopenMG" (the ONLY element read by index
  that the traces show; the container-accessor hooks in run 198 will confirm
  the complete list).
- the RAW SERIALIZED BYTES as one blob: padded with zeros to a multiple of 8
  and CBC-processed whole (post-gate). The derivation is over the serialization,
  so EVERY byte-level choice (tags, segmentation, order) changes the reply.
- NOTHING else from the container: digest1/digest2 and both CBC keys are
  session-side (digest2 identical across insert/replace/A1/IV-flip runs).

**Ecosystem grammar (OMGUtils.dll parseOmgFile + its readers, decompiled and
capstone-verified 2026-10-31, out/decomp-s35-omgutils.c + -omgutils2.c):**
the genuine fopenMG-headed document is SEQ { SEQ { objid "fopenMG",
int version in {1002,1003}, blob CID (exactly 20 B), objid ['g', algoType],
int, objid ['h', contType], int, per-type params..., string MAC (8 B),
content (tag 0x04-headed) } }. Wire rules (FUN_6390b3f0/b5a0/c070): length =
1 byte if < 0x80; ints = tag 0x02 + minimal-length BE; blobs = tag 0x04;
obj-ids = tag 0x06 + length + bytes; sequences = tag 0x30. This is the SAME
TLV family as the VM struct grammar (tlv.py) — one Sony serialization family.
CAVEATS: (a) parseOmgFile parses an OMG FILE (header fields + content data) —
a different document from the vt+0x68 header query, though the name gate and
the 8-B MAC slot size-match op 11's 8-B reply; (b) in the genuine grammar the
name element carries tag 0x06 (obj-id), NOT the tag 0x04 our S34 candidates
used — the VM compare is tag-agnostic (blob class either way) but the tag byte
is part of the ciphered serialization, so 04 vs 06 changes the reply;
(c) hdr96 does NOT itself parse as this TLV (it is a fixed-field artifact);
the struct-form document is a SERIALIZATION OF its fields, as the .opf is a
serialization of ProcessFile's fields.

**Canonical document (A0), as the app will build it** — root container,
indefinite form [30 80] ... [00 00], elements in secure-header field order
(the v4 segmentation, S33/S34):
  [0] name: tag 0x06 (canonical) — see fork F1 — len 7 "fopenMG"
  [1] blob hdr[0x00:0x04]  (01 00 28 2e)
  [2] blob hdr[0x04:0x0c]  (00 00 0d 74 00 00 00 00 — frame count at +6)
  [3] blob hdr[0x10:0x18]  (00 01 00 0e 00 00 00 00)
  [4] blob BlockSeed       (d8 83 bf 8b 43 59 d7 c6)
  [5] blob hdr[0x20:0x34]  (the 20-B content block / CID)
  [6] blob hdr[0x34:0x40]  (zeros)
  [7] blob hdr[0x40:0x50]  ("ATX " block)
  [8] blob hdr[0x50:0x60]  (global seed || IV)
  hdr[0x0c:0x10] is NOT carried (it is the one 4-B gap in the header layout;
  S33's v4 segmentation — validated by the program reading the container
  cleanly — omits exactly it).

**Fork F1 (the one surviving byte-level ambiguity): the name element's tag.**
04 = S34's working candidates (replies on record: A0-insert 81 50 39 3f...,
A0-replace ce 89..., A1 ed ea..., IV-flip dc e3...). 06 = the ecosystem's
obj-id convention (parseOmgFile REQUIRES tag 0x06 for this very name).
The oracle cannot distinguish them (both pass the gate; the reply differs
because the cipher covers the raw bytes). Run 199 records the 06-variant's
A0 reply so both candidates are on file; the app should treat the tag as a
one-byte switch to A/B on a real D-NE1 test disc if ever needed. All other
shape choices are pinned by evidence above; segmentation alternatives
(e.g. splitting seed||IV into two elements) change the raw bytes and hence
the reply, but have NO supporting evidence — the v4 segmentation is the
secure header's own field layout and the only one the program has been
observed to consume cleanly end-to-end.

### Session 35 results — THE CIPHER IS LIFTED (2026-10-31, run 198 = sal_emu195.py)

**HEADLINE: querycif op 11 is a keyed MAC built from textbook SHA-1 and
FIPS DES. The clean-room Python lift (scratch/s35/op11.py + des.py, no Sony
code) reproduces ALL FOUR oracle replies byte-for-byte (A0 insert
81 50 39 3f 51 e2 38 48; A0 replace ce 89 8b 93 fe 60 75 92; A1 insert
ed ea 35 0d 30 f3 ff 63; IV-flip dc e3 d5 7d 45 4c 6e 2b).**

Operator semantics (genblock2.bin capstone, scratch/s35/op_dis_prelim.txt,
helper_dis.txt, helper_dis2.txt, core_dis.txt): the VM value model, the
reader grammar, and every operator in the op-11 path were decoded from the
code, then CONFIRMED by run 198's helper hooks (sal_emu195.py: per-op entry/
exit logging with full blob contents, object snapshots in scratch/s35/objs/,
helper-argument capture, full region dump scratch/s35/genfull.bin):
- idx94 SUBSTRING, idx95 CONCAT, idx96 COMPARE (three-way), idx98 LENGTH,
  idx99 XOR (result len = max; tail = longer operand's tail),
  idx101 ZEROBLOB, idx113 DES KEY EXPANSION (standard PC-1/cumulative
  rotations/PC-2 — tables verified byte-for-byte; the 128-B output stores
  each round's 8 six-bit subkey chunks in the fixed order [6,4,2,0,7,5,3,1]
  relative to PC-2 group order — a storage convention only),
  idx114 DES-ENCRYPT block, idx115 DES-DECRYPT block,
  idx118 SHA-1 INIT (the 92-B blob is a SHA-1 context {H[5], bitlen_lo,
  bitlen_hi, 64-B buffer}; the "NIST/EKB pattern" in it was the SHA-1 IV
  0x67452301 0xefcdab89 0x98badcfe 0x10325476 0xc3d2e1f0 all along),
  idx119 SHA-1 UPDATE, idx120 SHA-1 FINAL (standard padding; resets ctx),
  idx122 DES-CBC-ENCRYPT, idx123 DES-CBC-DECRYPT (both textbook CBC over
  full blocks; the helper's tail path is a ciphertext-stealing variant for
  non-multiple-of-8 inputs, untriggered by any observed input).
  The "spec byte" (0xd8 for the idx123 call, 0xd7 for idx122) is a validated
  version tag checked against the execute-context byte [0x10309a30] = 0xc2;
  both dispatch paths are the same CBC — it has NO algorithmic effect.
  The DES core (bit-sliced, delta-swap permutations) is exactly FIPS DES:
  proven by direct Python comparison on CBC#2 (full 128 B), both pair ops,
  and CBC#1 once its mode was identified as DECRYPT.

THE ALGORITHM (op11.py is the reference implementation):
  Inputs — per-track: doc (the struct-form document, below), CID20 (the
  20-B content block, secure header +0x20). Session ceremony (constant per
  session): ckey24 (op3), d2 (20-B stream value, below), X8 (8 B, two LE
  timestamp words carried in the stream).
  1. CID16 = CID20[0:16] with bytes 9,10 zeroed.
     NEW16 = CID16 XOR (X8 || X8).
     digest1 = SHA1(NEW16).
  2. W24 = DES-CBC-DECRYPT(ckey24, IV = digest1[0:8], key = digest1[8:16]).
     (Computed by the program; does not feed the reply — it likely feeds
     other stream values. Recorded for completeness.)
  3. NAME GATE: element[0] of the document container must blob-equal
     "fopenMG" (compare == 0), else the reply is empty (-> the caller's
     "Invalid OMG Header"). Run 198 accessor hooks: in the reply phase the
     program reads exactly ONE document element by index — element[0].
  4. C = DES-CBC-ENCRYPT(doc padded with zeros to a multiple of 8,
     IV = 0, key = d2[0:8]).
  5. reply = DES-ENCRYPT(DES-DECRYPT(C[last 8 bytes], key = d2[8:16]),
     key = d2[0:8]).
  Corrections to the Session 34 brief recorded en route: the padding is to
  a multiple of 8 (NOT "to 128 B" — 128 was 121's rounding); the spec bytes
  d7/d8 are session constants, NOT document bytes (identical for A1 and the
  replace shape); digest2 (= d2 here) is document-independent.

ABOUT d2 (the one input whose derivation is NOT in this lift): d2 is a
20-byte value the program FETCHES from its stream/environment (it is on the
stack by op #15 with no producing op in the reply phase; a container-reader
call between the second SHA-1 final and op #15 is its arrival). It is NOT
computed by op 11. Native side: FUN_6470f9ae appends ONE session-derived
value to the stream via FUN_6470b1ed — sourced from the session getter
vt+0x18 with selector 3 (a std::string out-param). The 32-byte stream value
visible in the assembled frame (scratch/s32/frame-head.bin: 0f d6 ec 7e ...
cc c6) is NOT d2 and SHA1 of it (or of its parts, or combined with
ckey/d1/W24) is NOT d2 (tested). d2's derivation lives in pfcom's session/
key-ceremony layer — the same layer that produces the op3 ckey (Sessions
26–29 territory: the EKB ceremony). For the app, (ckey24, d2, X8) are
ceremony outputs to be lifted from THAT layer; op 11 itself is done.
Phase 2 of the program (after the reply value) computes further stream
values using d2 and the track's .opf document (22 children, read at
indices 16/18/20 et al.): F3 = SHA1(d2 || opfdoc136) = d6 ae 73 74 ...,
F4 = SHA1(d2 || F3) = d9 ce de ed ... — recorded here; they do not affect
the op-11 reply and presumably serve sibling ops.

THE FAITHFUL DOCUMENT (task 1 deliverable): the struct-form serialization
of the 96-B secure header — root container [30 80] ... [00 00], elements:
[0] "fopenMG", then tag-04 blobs of the header fields in order:
hdr[00:04], hdr[04:0c], hdr[10:18], BlockSeed, hdr[20:34] (CID20),
hdr[34:40], hdr[40:50] ("ATX "), hdr[50:60] (global seed || IV);
hdr[0c:10] is not carried. Byte-exact files: scratch/s34/candidate5.bin
(A0, name tag 04), scratch/s35/candidate8.bin (A0, name tag 06),
scratch/s35/candidate8-A1.bin (A1, tag 06).
FORK F1 (the one surviving byte-level ambiguity): the name element's tag —
04 (S34's working candidates; replies on record) vs 06 (the obj-id tag
parseOmgFile REQUIRES for this name in the genuine OMGF grammar). The VM
gate is tag-agnostic; the cipher covers the raw bytes, so the replies
differ. The lift predicts the tag-06 A0 reply = 66 7b 2d 42 4f dc fc cc;
run 199 (sal_emu195 + candidate8) arbitrates the lift on an unseen
document — and if it matches, the app has a validated implementation for
BOTH variants and the 04-vs-06 choice becomes a one-byte A/B on a real
D-NE1 test disc (or a burn-host code find, if one ever surfaces).
Evidence for the rest of the shape: the program consumes the document
ONLY as element[0] + the raw bytes (above); the segmentation is the secure
header's own field layout and the only one observed to run cleanly end to
end (S33 v4 -> S34 candidates); parseOmgFile (OMGUtils.dll, decompiled
out/decomp-s35-omgutils.c) proves the ecosystem's fopenMG document family
uses exactly these tags (30/06/04/02) and wire rules (FUN_6390b3f0/b5a0/
c070: short-form lengths, minimal BE ints) — the same family as tlv.py.
OPEN QUESTION for the app phase (not this session): which header fields
the D-NE1 actually validates — X8/d2 are ceremony inputs whose values the
app must choose self-consistently; only a real burn test arbitrates.
- Run 199 prediction (for the record, written before the run completed):
  the lift gives candidate8 (tag-06 A0) -> 66 7b 2d 42 4f dc fc cc.
  op11.py's self-test CID slice was off by 2 in its first version
  (harmless: the reply is CID-independent — digest1 feeds only W24);
  fixed to c5[49:69] and extended with digest1/W24 trace checks, all pass.
  Note: A0 and A1 share the SAME CID20 (the Ignite set is one track
  written four ways; they differ only in BlockSeed/IV).
- **RUN 199 VERDICT (sal_emu195 + candidate8): oracle reply for the
  tag-06 document = 66 7b 2d 42 4f dc fc cc — EXACTLY the lift's

  (same session inputs). Session 35 deliverables: this note's spec,

## Session 36 (2026-10-31) — THE CEREMONY INPUTS AND WHERE THE REPLY GOES

Goal: (1) check whether the op-11 reply is stored on the real Ignite
discs; (2) trace the reply's consumer inside pfcom; (3) characterize
the ceremony inputs (ckey24, d2, X8) across sessions and trace d2's
source in the vt+0x18 selector-3 getter. Brief claims re-verified
against the S34/S35 sections + scratch/s35/op11.py before starting:
harness ceremony constants (from op11.py, = the runs 193-199 session):
ckey24 = df3963e14568d90162d541585189b3b192c59b7a833fdca9,
d2 = 3d14fb8d372a3b5b17010dbb4fd1b3fecf4a0570,
X8 = 37cce36a8b90e36a.

### Task 1 — the reply is NOT on the real discs (desk, no runs)
- Whole-file scan of TRK000A0-A3.ATP (CD stuff/HIFI/) for all five
  known replies (A0 tag04 8150393f..., A0 tag06 667b2d42..., A1 edea...,
  A0-replace ce89..., A0-IVflip dce3...): ZERO hits.
- Stronger: built A2/A3 documents from their real headers in both
  Fork-F1 variants and computed their predicted replies under the
  harness ceremony (scratch/s36 calc, in-line): A2 tag04 0f6fcc7e
  b6320512, A3 tag04 51a60f0e506c2bab, A2 tag06 0af5fd759a92c894,
  A3 tag06 fa23b7d4e6d8b6bf — none present in the real files either.
- Decisive structural argument: the four real 96-B secure headers
  differ from each other ONLY at +0x18 (BlockSeed) and +0x58 (IV).
  The op-11 reply avalanches with the document, so if it were stored
  in the header the headers would differ at its offset too. They do
  not -> THE REPLY IS NOT A FIELD OF THE .ATP SECURE HEADER.
  (Full-tree scan of every other stage0 file still running at time of
  writing; result appended when it lands.)

- Task 1 addendum (full-tree scan completed): the five known replies
  appear NOWHERE in the entire atrac-cd-stage0 tree (all disc files,
  .STK/.NDB/.STI included; only archive/ excluded). Combined with the
  header-structure argument, the reply is not stored on the disc in
  any per-track file under the harness ceremony; and since the four
  headers differ only at BlockSeed/IV, it is provably not a field of
  the secure header at all, under ANY ceremony.

### Task 2 — the reply's consumer (desk; dynamic confirmation in run 200)
- FUN_6470f9ae IS session vt+0x68 (S29 census). Inside pfcom its
  contract is exhausted by: build stream, invoke, extract first reply
  value as a string, REQUIRE length == 8 (else (0xe) "Invalid OMG
  Header"), memcpy 8 B verbatim to the caller's out pointer
  ([ebp+0x10]). No comparison, no storage, no key schedule, no
  further use inside the function.
- Caller hunt: no direct call sites (it is reached only via the
  vtable). Byte-pattern scan for call [reg+0x68] across every
  delivered DLL is non-specific (every COM interface has a +0x68
  slot); the omgatpcd sites sit in ATL registration code. In the
  pfcom decompilations, the only two vtable+0x68 calls are on
  procfile() (FUN_64705c9f) and salwrap0() (FUN_64715b21) — neither
  is the session interface. S29 already recorded: "No in-corpus
  caller of session +0x68 exists (omgatpcd never touches pfcom
  sessions)"; S31: the burn-side caller was never delivered.
- CONCLUSION: within all delivered material, the reply's ONLY
  consumer is the external caller of vt+0x68 itself — the
  undelivered SonicStage burn host, which receives a bare 8-byte
  value ("OMG header" material per the method's own error string).
  Nothing in pfcom validates the reply's CONTENT, only its LENGTH.
  For the app this means: the reply must be reproduced exactly only
  insofar as a downstream consumer checks it; the only remaining
  candidate validator is the D-NE1 itself, and Task 1 shows the
  value is not carried in the .ATP secure header the player reads.

### Task 3 — ceremony, desk results so far
- X8 SOLVED (harness side): X8 = 37 cc e3 6a 8b 90 e3 6a (LE words
  0x6ae3cc37, 0x6ae3908b). Word0 = 1793313847 = the harness's FROZEN
  time() constant (sal_emu152 lineage: h_time / GetLocalTime /
  GetSystemTimeAsFileTime all pinned to it; 2026-10-29T22:44:07Z).
  So word0 is a WALL-CLOCK time() read. Word1 = 1793298571 =
  word0 - 15276 s exactly. Neither word is stored literally (LE or
  FILETIME form) in any staged file. d2/ckey24 likewise appear in NO
  staged file (byte scan of unicorn/scratch) -> all three are
  computed at runtime.
- SHA-1 battery vs d2 (desk): SHA1 of {seed, seed||seed, ckey24, X8,
  X8||X8, seed||ckey, ckey||seed, STK(172 B), STK||seed, seed||STK,
  maclist.dat, maclist||seed, .opf, seed||.opf} — NO match.
- Cross-run constancy (logs on disk): runs 191/193/195/198/199 (five
  separate harness processes/sessions, S34-S35) ALL show identical
  ckey24 (df3963e1...), d2 (3d14fb8d...), X8 (37cce36a...) — under
  the harness's frozen clock + fixed staged state, the ceremony is
  fully deterministic. Runs 200/201 (sal_emu196) test the clock
  dependence: run 201 shifts every frozen-clock HLE by +3600 s
  (env ATRAC_S36_TSHIFT); predictions — X8 word0 shifts +3600 if it
  is the live time() read; ckey24/d2 shift only if time-seeded.
- d2's channel: d2 is a VM blob by op #15 (run193/198 [s34op] trace:
  idx94 SUBSTRING(d2,0,8)/(8,8) = the two DES keys), read from the
  input stream via the container reader — i.e. stream-carried, past
  the 168-B window S32 dumped. Run 200 dumps the full 1040-B stream
  (scratch/s36/stream-ts0.bin) to locate d2/X8 as stream values and
  identify their serialization slots.

### Runs 200-201 (sal_emu196.py = 195 + S36 hooks + clock-shift probe)
Run 200 (TSHIFT=0, log run200.txt) captures:
- Task 2 DYNAMIC CONFIRMATION: FUN_6470f9ae was entered EXACTLY
  ONCE in the entire session lifecycle (creation, Drive A getters,
  op 11, phase-2 ops), with caller = 0x20000000 — the harness's
  run_with_seh STUB, i.e. the external driver. pfcom NEVER calls
  its own vt+0x68. The reply's only consumer is the external
  caller of the interface; in the real system that is the
  undelivered burn host.
- FUN_64714904 sel=3 (called from FUN_6470f9ae @0x6470fa07):
  out-str == ProcessFile+0x20 str == ckey24 (24 B df3963e1...),
  ProcessFile+0x34 = 0x18 after the call. So vt+0x18 sel 3 returns
  the CKEY (cached at ProcessFile+0x20), NOT d2 — d2 is a distinct
  value that reaches op 11 by a different channel.
- vt+0x68 reply reproduced in 196: 81 50 39 3f 51 e2 38 48
  ([s33reply] extracted string) — baseline integrity confirmed.
- Full-stream dump (scratch/s36/stream-ts0.bin, 1040 B from the
  first reader cursor during vt+0x68): the stream's at-rest form
  is NOT plaintext serialization — after a 30 80 at +14 the bytes
  are high-entropy with recurring motifs (04 08/04 6a/04 62/04 10
  + 02 a9 08 73 ...; 02 49 eb 73-headed ~120-B records). No
  fopenMG/ckey/d2/X8/hdr96 appears literally. Consistent with the
  SAL layer storing stream values encrypted (cf. the .ocm bodies
  and the per-byte opcode decryption in the dispatch loop);
  values are plaintext only as materialized VM objects (the S32
  frame / S35 snapshots). d2/X8 slot identification therefore
  proceeds via the operator traces + the ts0/ts3600 diff, not by
  plaintext search.
- Run 201 (sal_emu196, ATRAC_S36_TSHIFT=3600 — every frozen-clock
  HLE +3600 s, log run201.txt) — THE CLOCK PROBE:
  * ckey24 CHANGED COMPLETELY: df3963e1... -> 1b ab cb da 4f 3e 3e
    c8 4b 75 0b 28 57 d6 a7 64 6f 0a 56 e2 d2 63 e8 55 (identical in
    Drive A and in FUN_6470f9ae's internal sel-3 call). So

  * GET_GLOBAL_SEED UNCHANGED (2a d1 43 87 dd fb 4b 86) — the seed

  * d2 UNCHANGED (3d 14 fb 8d..., 10 log hits) — not time-seeded,
    not ckey-derived.
  * THE REPLY UNCHANGED (81 50 39 3f 51 e2 38 48) — dynamic proof

  * X8 word0 shifted by EXACTLY +3600 (0x6ae3cc37 -> 0x6ae3da47):

    X8 word1 did NOT track (+3600): 0x6ae3908b -> 0x6ae2d6c3
    (w0-w1 offset 15276 s in run 200 vs 97412 s in run 201; no

  * Stream dump ts3600 == ts0 for the first 256 B (the at-rest

- Desk: icv.dat is 20 B but != d2 (no XOR/plain relation; not a
  SHA1 of the pair/omg_id). omg_id.dat is a struct-form doc

- Run 200 op-trace anatomy (for the d2 hunt): ops #1-#14 compute

## SESSION 36 VERDICTS
1. THE REPLY IS NOT ON THE DISCS. Whole-file + whole-tree scans
   for all known/predicted replies (both Fork-F1 tags, tracks
   A0-A3, harness ceremony) = zero hits; and the four real secure
   headers differ ONLY at BlockSeed (+0x18) and IV (+0x58), so a
   document-dependent MAC cannot be a header field under ANY
   ceremony. The reply is a transient interface value.
2. THE REPLY HAS NO DELIVERED CONSUMER. FUN_6470f9ae (session
   vt+0x68) length-checks it (== 8) and memcpys it verbatim to
   the caller's buffer — nothing in pfcom compares, stores, or
   schedules from it. Run 200's caller trace: exactly ONE entry
   in the full lifecycle, from the external driver (stub
   0x20000000); pfcom never calls its own vt+0x68; omgatpcd
   never touches pfcom sessions (S29/S31). The consumer is the
   undelivered SonicStage burn host. For the app: the reply must
   be reproduced exactly ONLY if a downstream check exists; in
   all delivered material the only check is length == 8, and the
   D-NE1 cannot be checking a value that is not on the disc
   (recomputing it would require the player to run the whole
   OpenMG ceremony).
3. CEREMONY END STATES (scratch/s36/ceremony.md):
   - ckey24 = EPHEMERAL (class c): querycif op 6 derives it per
     call from the stable ceremony state + the live wall clock
     at second granularity (runs 201/205). It feeds only W24
     inside op 11, which does not feed the reply; the app does
     not need it for the reply.
   - d2 = CONSTANT OF THE INSTALLED STATE (class b): invariant
     across sessions, processes, wall clock (+1 s, +3600 s),
     pair generations (163<->164), documents and ckeys (runs
     191-205). Provenance pinned by elimination: a deterministic
     function of the installed OpenMG ceremony state (the layer
     that also yields the global seed); stored nowhere in
     plaintext (not in any staged file, not in guest memory
     pre-execute, not in the ProcessFile); materializes only in
     the VM's decrypted execute frame. Its byte-level derivation
     inside the SAL stream layer is NOT lifted — the one open
     item. App consequence: any self-consistent d2 reproduces
     "a" valid op-11 reply; matching LOLA'S d2 byte-for-byte is
     needed only if the payload cipher (still unmapped) also
     consumes d2 — that is the next session's question.
   - X8 = EPHEMERAL (class c): word0 = live time() in Unix
     seconds (tracks the clock exactly, runs 201/205); word1 =
     a path-dependent session/tick-time value (not a clock read,
     not pair state). Neither word feeds the reply (S35 algebra
     + runs 201/205 dynamics). The app supplies its own.
- Harnesses: sal_emu196.py (S36 hooks + ATRAC_S36_TSHIFT clock
  probe), sal_emu197.py (+ d2 hunt), sal_emu198.py (+ ProcessFile
  census/container dumps). Logs run200.txt-run205.txt. Scratch:
  scratch/s36/stream-ts0.bin, stream-ts1.bin, stream-ts3600.bin,
  pair-backup/, ceremony.md.
  Root staging restored to its pre-session state (MD5-verified).
  Nothing uploaded. No new Easter-egg traces.

## Session 37 (2026-10-31) — THE PAYLOAD CIPHER

Goal: find the .ATP payload transform. Ignite set = known plaintext
(pairs/ignite.raw, 1,294,944 B) + 4 encryptions (TRK000A0–A3), each
with own BlockSeed @hdr+0x18 and IV @hdr+0x58; hdr+0x50 = global seed
2a d1 43 87 dd fb 4b 86 in all four. VERIFIED on disk this session:
file sizes (1,296,064 = 1024+96+1,294,944), all header fields, headers
differ ONLY at +0x18/+0x58 (S36 claim re-confirmed). A2 BlockSeed
9b 26 d5 40 b5 3d e5 f7 IV 06 08 d8 15 eb 15 22 7c; A3 BlockSeed
63 2e ad 77 1b de 58 79 IV 57 cd 84 7a 8a ac 46 3f. Order of work:
desk battery (DES family, reusing s35/des.py) → harness (atpcd.ocm)
only if the battery fails. d2 question to be answered either way.

### Desk battery #1 — ⭐ BREAKTHROUGH ON BLOCK SCREENING
2,703 construction/track combos screened over the first 16 blocks
(128 B). Exactly one family hit, on ALL FOUR tracks:
**DES-CBC-ENCRYPT, key = d2[0:8] (3d 14 fb 8d 37 2a 3b 5b), IV = the
track's own header IV @+0x58** — 16/16 leading blocks on A0, A1, A2, A3.
d2[8:16], d2[12:20], BlockSeed-as-key, seed-as-key, all 3DES combos,
CFB/OFB/CTR/ECB, ckey windows, op-11-reply-as-key: all dead.
==> THE PAYLOAD CONSUMES d2: d2[0:8] is the payload DES key.
Full-payload verification next (continuous chain vs per-frame restart).

### ⭐⭐⭐ FULL PAYLOAD MATCH — THE PAYLOAD CIPHER IS SOLVED
**encrypt: DES-CBC, key = d2[0:8], IV = track header IV (+0x58),
continuous chain across the whole payload (no per-frame restart).**
Verified byte-for-byte on ALL FOUR tracks: ignite.raw + (d2[0:8],
per-track IV) reproduces each .ATP payload exactly (1,294,944 B each).
BlockSeed plays NO role in the payload transform; ckey24 and X8 are
NOT consumed by it. The ONLY ceremony input is d2, and only its first
8 bytes, as the DES key — and d2 is the proven constant of the
installed ceremony state (S36). Remaining work: write the clean-room
encrypt_payload() to the atrac-cd-author cipher.py contract.

### Desk batteries #2/#3 + gap-fill — the other three tracks do NOT fit
Readout #7/#8 check (goal files): all 7 sampled tracks are on ONE disc
(Lola's disc 1); Dreamer (E1) / Electric (DC) pairings are the stage0
session's own, lengths exact, frame counts exact. But:
- battery2 (DC/E1, encrypt-side, ~500 combos: own header fields as
  keys, d2 XOR/KDF combos, op-11 replies + digest1/W24 windows for
  their own docs, CFB/OFB implied-IV): 0 hits. CFB/OFB implied IV0
  under d2[0:8] unrecognizable for both.
- battery3 (DECRYPT-side screen — IV-independent for blocks >= 1 —
  ~48 single-DES + 13 3DES keys vs known plaintext for DC/E1, and vs
  the 0x3A frame-head signature for TRK0000A): 0 hits.
- gap-fill (CID20 windows, connum/slot repeats, own-mgd 3DES combos):
  0 hits.
- pt^ct for DC/E1: entropy 8.000, no period <= 4096, reversed-CBC no.
So Talk/Dreamer/Electric are NOT DES-CBC (nor CFB/OFB/CTR/ECB/3DES)
under any key constructible from their headers + the ceremony values
tested. The Ignite construction (key=d2[0:8], IV=hdr IV) is exact but
may be a special case: the Ignite copies were burned from the library
.OMA, the others plausibly from fresh sources. The GENERAL transform
must come from the code: harness route (atpcd.ocm typed stream) next.
Files: scratch/s37/battery1.py, battery2.py, battery3.py.

## Session 38 (2026-10-31) — RUN THE BIND_CONTENT DRIVE (the general payload path)

Goal: drive atpcd.ocm selector 1 (BIND_CONTENT) through the manager
stream machinery, exactly as omgatpcd.dll does natively; first on the
Ignite content (control), then on a non-fitting track (Dreamer /
Electric). Grammar transcribed from out/decomp-omgatpcd.c and
cross-checked line-by-line against out/decomp-s29-atpcd.c (identical
Ghidra renders) BEFORE building anything.

### Grammar transcription (verified from the decomp, this session)

Manager = salwrap1() singleton (harness cell 0x651411dc). omgatpcd's
stream wrappers:
- FUN_61604840 -> vt+0x40 create stream (arg 0x200); returned handle
  stored in the caller's stream local.
- FUN_6160485c -> vt+0x44 destroy stream.
- manager vt[0] execute(in_local, out_local, 0, 0).
- FUN_616048d7 -> SESSION RECORD: p = manager vt+0x8(); desc {p, 0x10};
  append via vt+0x6c.
- FUN_616048a5 -> vt+0x6c append buffer desc {ptr, len}.
- FUN_6160488c -> vt+0x70 append buffer desc {ptr, len} (the FIRST
  buffer record in BIND_CONTENT and media-info; pfcom's FUN_6470f9ae
  also uses +0x70 for its caller-buffer record).
- FUN_6160486f -> vt+0x74 append int (stream, value, 0).
- FUN_616048be -> vt+0x64 append module desc (FUN_61604a42 builds
  {0, 0, std::string name}).
- Readbacks: FUN_61604922 -> vt+0x8c read byte; FUN_6160493e ->
  vt+0x84 read buffer (desc {ptr, len}); FUN_6160495a -> vt+0x80 read
  string (into a caller std::string object); FUN_61604906 -> vt+0x9c
  read int.

OP-A — FUN_616054cd (sole arg: content-id pointer @[ebp+0xc],
must be non-null):
- stream in: [session record][int 0][module "atpcd.ocm"]; execute.
- readback: byte; buffer 16 B (into the caller's CID buffer);
  string #1; string #2.
- native post: omgResolveRepositoryFile(path, 4, cid) +
  omgMkdirHier + OmgString::writeToFile(STRING #2, path) — string #2
  is the repository-file content. Run 125/174 observed shape: buf16
  fresh 16 B; str1 len 24 (key blob); str2 len 44 = struct-form
  envelope `30 80 04 1c | 30 80 04 10 <buf16> 02 01 00 02 01 00
  00 00 04 08 <8 B> 00 00`.

BIND_CONTENT — FUN_6160571e (args: cid @+0xc, target .ATP wide
path @+0x10, out int* @+0x14; any null -> 0x80004003):
- native pre: OmgMmap(target .ATP); require mmap size >= 10 and
  >= id3size + 0x60 (id3size = Atp::getId3TagSize(mmap addr));
  procfile = procfile() vt+0x8(mmap + id3size + 0x20); if
  procfile vt+0x74(0xe) == 0 -> release procfile, return
  0x80040418. .opf string := procfile vt+0x28(). Repository file
  (omgResolveRepositoryFile type 4, by cid) opened via omgutil9
  vt+0xc (access 0xC0000000, disposition 3 OPEN_EXISTING),
  GetFileSize, ReadFile of the WHOLE file -> work buffer.
- stream in: [+0x70 desc {workbuf, worklen}] [+0x74 int =
  OmgMmap::size = FULL target .ATP size] [session record]
  [+0x6c desc {.opf data, .opf len} (FUN_61604d81 builds the desc
  from the .opf std::string)] [+0x74 int 1] [+0x64 module
  "atpcd.ocm"]; execute via manager vt[0].
- readback: byte (discarded); int status (vt+0x9c). Log string
  "ATPCD::BIND_CONTENT: 0x" + 16 CID bytes hex; status > 99 ->
  ": CIF error" appended and procfile vt+0x58(status - 100,
  logstr, 0, 0).
- status == 0 path, in order: string A -> procfile vt+0x2c(A, 1)
  (stored into the procfile); string B -> field +0x00 of an
  auxiliary object (pointer loaded from frame -0x4dc; the object's
  owner is NOT identifiable from this decomp — noted, not needed
  by the drive); string C -> SetFilePointer(workfile, 0) +
  WriteFile(workfile, C data, C length) — C OVERWRITES the
  repository work file (the payload carrier); string D -> aux
  object field +0x1c; int -> stored to caller's out *(ebp+0x14);
  buffer 8 B -> Atp::SecureAudioHeader::setEkbVersion(header +
  0x10, 0x1000e) then memcpy(header + 0x18, buf8, 8); header base
  = mmap addr + id3size. Function returns 0.
- status 1 -> OMGException(6) "...: INVALID_DATA"; 2 ->
  OMGException(0x18) "...: DATA_SIZE_OVER"; 3 ->
  OMGException(0x18) "...: FILE_NO_OVER"; any other nonzero ->
  OMGException(2) "...: unknown status: %d".

Media-info — FUN_61604e96 (selector 2; transcribed, not driven):
stream [+0x70 desc {repo-file content}] [int 2] [module];
readback byte, string (must memcmp-equal caller's 16-B CID else
(0x2) "bad ATP media info file"), int, int -> two caller outs.

*** CORRECTION to the Session 37 summary: its "readback = status
int + 3 strings + int + 8-B buffer" undercounts. Both decomp
renders show FOUR vt+0x80 string reads on the status-0 path
(A, B, C, D), then the int, then the 8-B buffer. The drive reads
four strings. ***

### Run 208 (sal_emu201.py, mode opa,wrap3,wrap2s; log run208.txt) — inner-container placement ACCEPTED; VM page pool dies (NOMEM)

  status 1 INVALID_DATA (payload slot not found); envelope

### Run 209 (sal_emu202.py, opa,wrap3s/m/h; log run209.txt) — slices verdict INVALID_DATA; the full file's only blocker is capacity

- wrap3s (376 B audio), wrap3m (37,600 B), wrap3h (376,000 B),

  consistency is the standing explanation for the slices'
  INVALID_DATA: blob_len + 1120 == size-int 1296064 holds ONLY
  for the full 1,294,944-B payload (1294944 + 1120 = 1296064),
  and the .opf/session frame state (3,444 frames) likewise
  matches only the full file. The full-size wrap3 is therefore
  expected to pass the verdict — its sole blocker is the page
  pool (run 208 NOMEM, ext 0x106fc000 = 275,759,104).
- Discriminator run next: full wrap3 with the emulated heap
  enlarged 128 MiB -> 448 MiB (HEAP_SIZE is a harness constant;
  heap 0x10000000 + 0x1c000000 ends 0x2c000000, clear of the
  VA arena at 0x30000000). Completes -> capacity was heap-
  bound. Same NOMEM ext -> the pool is a fixed internal carve
  and the page manager gets the decomp treatment.

### Run 210 (sal_emu203.py, heap 256 MiB, opa,wrap3; log run210.txt) — NOMEM identical: pool is a FIXED internal carve

- Full wrap3 with HEAP_SIZE doubled (128 -> 256 MiB): dies
  with the IDENTICAL error, IDENTICAL ext: NOMEM_ERR_NO_FREE_
  PAGES kind 0x14402044 ext 0x106fc000 (275,759,104). The
  failing page address does not move with heap capacity, so
  the VM page pool is a fixed-size carve inside salwrap whose
  frontier sits at 0x106fc000 in this boot layout. (A 448 MiB
  heap is impossible without relocating STUB_BASE 0x20000000 /
  VA_BASE 0x30000000 — and pointless until the carve itself is
  understood.) Next: locate the page manager in the salwrap
  decomp (pool base, page size, page count, who sets them).

- HYPOTHESIS (chunked work document): the real work doc may
  carry the audio as MANY small blob elements (per frame),
  which the VM streams with bounded pool usage; a single
  1.29 MB blob forces a whole-blob page load -> NOMEM. This
  fits all evidence (slices INVALID_DATA on frame-count
  grounds; wrap2 slot-not-found; wrap3-full pool death).
  Run 211 tests it: audio as 3,444 per-frame blob elements
  (376 B each) inside the inner container; variant w3c2 sets

### Run 211 (sal_emu204.py, opa,w3c,w3c2; log run211.txt) — chunking does NOT bound the pool; procfile-I/O theory dead

- w3c (audio as 3,444 per-frame blob elements inside the inner

  208's 0x106fc000 for a +13,771-B doc). Pool consumption
  tracks TOTAL payload, not element granularity — the VM
  accumulates pages as it processes, chunked or not.
- Desk check (run 206 log): bb's execute window contains NO
  ReadFile/WriteFile/CreateFile/host-service traffic at all —
  BIND works from the stream records alone; the payload does
  NOT flow through the procfile. The audio must travel in the

- Pool facts so far: fixed frontier ~0x106fc000-0x10703000
  (heap-relative, deterministic per boot layout, immovable by
  HEAP_SIZE 128->256 MiB). 376,000 B payload completes;

128 and 256 MiB; consumption proportional to total payload
(per-frame chunking does not help). Dreamer/Electric BINDs
(1.14-1.26 MB payloads) were therefore NOT run — they would
hit the same wall; running them before the pool yields buys
nothing.

### Run 213 (sal_emu206.py, opa,wrap3; log run213.txt) — RAISE SITE CAPTURED: FUN_651205e0 via FUN_65120480

- The raise hook fired EXACTLY ONCE: kind=0x14402044,
  ext=0x106fc000, **ret=0x6512068e**, ctx=0x107baff8;
  stk also showed 0x6512053a (the caller's caller).
- FUN_65120480 (decomp-loader.c:1582) IS the VM's size-class
  freelist allocator: state st = *(ctx+0x14); region
  [st+0x10, st+0x18]; size classes 3..11 (block = 1<<class,
  else round to 0x400); per-0x400-page directory at st+0x24
  (8 B/page; word+4 = class, word+6 = extent length). On an
  empty class freelist it calls FUN_651205e0(ctx, rounded
  size) to carve a fresh chunk — 0x6512053a is that call.
- FUN_651205e0 disassembled from the real salwrap.dll
  (capstone; it was NOT in the Ghidra renders): an EXTENT
  allocator over the page directory. Free extents are a
  linked list of directory entries rooted at st+0x88
  (entry +0 = next, word+4 = 0xffff marks the end sentinel,
  word+6 = length in pages). On reaching the sentinel it may
  EXTEND the region: new_end_dir = sentinel + req_pages*8;
  the limit is dir + ((st+0x1c - base)>>10)*8; if new_end_dir
  > limit -> push (holder, 0x14402044, data_addr(sentinel))
  and call FUN_65120160 at 0x65120689 (ret 0x6512068e ✓).
  On success it updates st+0x18 (region end) — THE REGION
  GROWS; the hard cap is **st+0x1c**: pool capacity in bytes
  = (st+0x1c - st+0x10), and the directory at st+0x24 must
  physically hold (capacity>>10) entries of 8 B.
  ext in the error = data address of the sentinel = the
  region end at failure (0x106fc000 run 208/213;
  0x10703000 run 211 — bigger doc, end pushed further
  before the directory ran out).
- Fast path (req == 0x400): bump pointer st+0x14 against
  limit base + st+0x4 — a SECOND, smaller cap for single
  pages (st+0x4 = fast-path span).
- So "the pool" = this allocator's region + directory. To
  enlarge: raise st+0x1c AND give the directory real room
  (entries beyond the original allocation would corrupt
  the neighbouring slab allocation). Next: dump st fields
  live (base/end/limit/dir/st+0x4) and find who creates st
  (region + directory allocations) — the creation size is
  the number to change.

### Desk: the pool size is a REGISTRY KNOB (FUN_6511c8a0, decomp-c8a0-helpers.c:191-262)

- c8a0 (the manager/VM init) reads, via omgRegQueryValue:
  local_f8 = 0x400000; reg "SalMemorySize" -> local_f8;
  auStack_e8[0] = 0x96000; reg "SalMemorySize2" -> it;
  pvVar6 = malloc(local_f8); FUN_6512c810(pvVar6, local_f8,
  auStack_e8[0], &DAT_65145010, 0x13000). Same pattern for
  the init VM: malloc(0x358040) fixed, FUN_6512c7e0(buf+0x40,
  0x358000, local_dc /*reg "InitocmMemorySize2", default
  0x2000*/, &DAT_65158050, 0x13000).
- Run 109 already mapped: *DAT_6516d484 = main VM (c810
  arena, "SalMemorySize"), *DAT_6516d488 = init VM.
- Run 213 live: c810 arena malloc'd 0x400000 at 0x103e6f30
  (the default — the harness registry has no SalMemorySize
  value, so omgRegQueryValue MISSes and defaults stand).
  Failing region end 0x106fc000 = arena+0x3120d0: >3 MiB of
  pages consumed, so SalMemorySize2 (0x96000) is NOT the
  region span (likely fiber stack/commit split in
  FUN_651254c0). The knob to turn is SalMemorySize itself.
- Harness registry: REG_TREE keys are full paths
  ('software\\sony corporation\\openmg'); h_RegQueryValueExA
  serves REG_SZ strings only. Whether omgRegQueryValue
  accepts a string form (or demands REG_DWORD) is untested —
  experiment: seed + watch the [tr111] arena-c810 a2.
- Least-invasive fix candidates, in order: (1) seed
  REG_TREE SalMemorySize (data only); (2) teach the harness
  RegQueryValueExA to serve int values as REG_DWORD;
  (3) in-memory patch of the c8a0 default. All are harness
  changes; the Sony file on disk is never touched.
- Run 214 (sal_emu207.py) first: capture st fields at first
  allocator use + at the raise, to name base/limit/dir and

### Run 214 (sal_emu207.py, opa,wrap3; log run214.txt) — st FIELDS CAPTURED; cap = SalMemorySize − ~0.2 MB

  opens HKLM\SOFTWARE\Sony Corporation\OpenMG (string at

### Desk: FUN_6160571e TRANSCRIBED (out/decomp-s29-atpcd.c:2913+) — the REAL BIND_CONTENT contract

The S38 drive never called this method; it hand-rolled
an approximation of its frame. The real COM method
(OmgAtpcdMan::BIND_CONTENT) takes (this, id16, template
Path, outDword):
- arg +0x10 = template .ATP file path (WIDE string):
  OmgMmap maps it; requires size >= tagSize+0x60;
  a procfile stream is opened at file offset
  tagSize+0x20 (inside the secure header, past
  BlockSeed) — the .ocm reads template fields there.
- arg +0xc = pointer to a 16-BYTE repository ID
  (hex-logged "ATPCD::BIND_CONTENT: 0x<16B>"):
  omgResolveRepositoryFile(out, 4, id16) -> repo path;
  opened via omgutil9, GetFileSize + ReadFile — the
  REPO SOURCE FILE (the track's .OMA) read in full.
- The .ocm frame: salwrap1 vt+0x70 registers
  {repoContentPtr, repoSize}; the invoke chain pushes
  [templateMmapSize][templateStreamHandle][int 1]
  [atpcd.ocm]. NO .opf record, NO session record.
- The execute's status int: 0 OK; 1 INVALID_DATA;
  2 DATA_SIZE_OVER; 3 FILE_NO_OVER; >99 CIF error
  (status-100). Nonzero => OMGException with that name.
- On status 0 the METHOD (host-side) does the rest:
  builds the 4 readback strings + int + {ptr,len}
  buf8 pair; writes *arg+0x14 = the int (EKB version);
  calls the template stream vt+0x2c; SetFilePointer
  (repo,0) + WriteFile — THE TRANSFORMED CONTENT
  OVERWRITES THE REPO FILE; stamps the template
  header in its mmap: setEkbVersion(hdr+0x10,
  0x1000e), memcpy(hdr+0x18, buf8, 8) (BlockSeed).
  (hdr = mmap + tagSize; +0x18 == .ATP file offset
  1048 on real discs ✓.)
REINTERPRETATION of all S38/39 BIND evidence: my
hand-rolled "work document" sat in the repo-content
slot. The .ocm judged the TLV docs INVALID_DATA (not
a source file); the 46-B envelope took a ceremony-only
degenerate path (STATUS 0); the pool burn was the
VM's doc-to-value conversion of a 1.29-MB input,
not transform work.

  2913+). Contract: stack args (id16 @+0xc, template path
  WIDE @+0x10, out-dword @+0x14), ECX=this; all three args
  must be nonzero. Body never dereferences `this` beyond
  the FUN_61606150 SEH prologue (verified: all state via
  procfile()/omgutil9()/salwrap0()/salwrap1() singletons)
  => calling the raw address with a scratch `this` is
  faithful.
- Frame order re-derived from the append helpers
  (decomp-omgatpcd.c): vt+0x70 create-with-buffer {repo
  ptr,size}; FUN_6160486f = vt+0x74 append-int (template
  mmap size); FUN_616048d7 = vt+0x6c append-buffer
  {salwrap vt+8 session result, 0x10} — A SESSION RECORD
  **IS** in the frame (the S40 brief's "no session
  record" is wrong; decomp wins); FUN_61604d81 =
  vt+0x6c append-buffer over the std::string that
  procfile vt+0x28 serialized the template stream into
  (takes (ptr,len) from the string at +4/+0x14 — NOT an
  int); FUN_6160486f append-int 1 (selector);

  int -> *outDword, 8-B buffer (493e = vt+0x84) =
  BlockSeed. Status 0 host actions: procfile vt+0x2c
  writes string A into the template stream; WriteFile
  writes STRING B over the repo file at offset 0 (no

  memcpy(template hdr+0x18, buf8, 8).

### Run 221 (sal_emu214.py, ATRAC_S38_MODE='' S39='' S40=bind0) — E_POINTER: the methods take FOUR stack args

- omgatpcd loaded fine (8 exports, DllMain -> 1, init
  table drained); staging worked; the call returned
  instantly: EAX=0x80004003 (E_POINTER), out-dword never
  written, no allocs, repo untouched. The method's own
  arg check ([ebp+0xc]/[ebp+0x10]/[ebp+0x14] all
  nonzero) tripped.
- PROLOGUE DISASSEMBLED (capstone): FUN_6160571e starts
  `mov eax, 0x61606a1a; call 0x61606150`; the helper is
  the MSVC SEH prolog: pushes -1/handler/fs:[0], saves
  caller's EBP at [esp+0xc] slot and does
  `lea ebp, [esp+0xc]` => EBP = (entry ESP)-4, so
  [ebp+8]=stack arg0, [ebp+0xc]=arg1, [ebp+0x10]=arg2,
  [ebp+0x14]=arg3. The epilogue is `ret 0x10` = FOUR
  stack args. All three interface methods (OP-A
  FUN_616054cd checks only [ebp+0xc]; BIND and
  media-info check +0xc/+0x10/+0x14) read their first
  real argument at [ebp+0xc]: **arg0 @[ebp+8] is an
  unused leading slot** in every implementation.
  => Call with args=(0, id16, path, out).
- Second latent blocker in the same log: 'unresolved:
  pfcom.dll!procfile' — pfcom's exports were never
  merged into the global EXPORTS dict (only salwrap's
  were), so omgatpcd's procfile import got a dead
  stub. Fix: EXPORTS.update(pf_exports) before
  load_pe(omgatpcd). (BIND would have hit it right
  after the arg check.)
- OP-A desk bonus (FUN_616054cd full read): its
  readback writes a 16-B buffer back OVER the caller's
  id16 buffer (FUN_6160493e with desc {id16, 0x10}),
  THEN resolves the repo file from those bytes and
  writes the reply string there (omgMkdirHier +
  OmgString::writeToFile). => the 5 random-looking
  .acd files in the mirror are OP-A products named by
  reply bytes — NOT content IDs. BIND does NOT do
  this (its id16 is only read).

### sal_emu215.py = 214 + the two fixes
1. EXPORTS.update(pf_exports) (guarded) before the
   omgatpcd load_pe.
2. BIND call args=(0, _idp, _wp, _outp).
Run 222 = same env as 221.

### Run 222 (sal_emu215.py, bind0) — method RAN; my ID transcription was 17 bytes; module error ERR_TID_MTDD

- Arg fix works: template mmap'd (view = TRK000A0.ATP
  byte-identical, secure header intact), procfile
  stream machinery ran, resolver ran.
- **MY GARBLE (the standing pattern, self-inflicted
  this time): the id16 I staged was bytes.fromhex of
  a 34-char string = 17 bytes. CID20 =
  010f500000040000004f7e16725f9be7b4f37fbb; its
  first 16 bytes are ...9be7 (32 hex chars), NOT
  ...9be7b4. The resolver's own audit lines are the
  ground truth: it requested
  procfile/E7/010f500000040000004f7e16725f9be7.acd
  (subdir = hex(id[15]) = E7) — my disassembly
  decode of the resolver was right; my hex copying
  was wrong. Dreamer/Electric subdirs are likewise
  E7 (their CID20s share the ...725f9be7 waist),
  filenames ...5c8816725f9be7.acd /
  ...e33916725f9be7.acd.**
- The harness's h_CreateFileA CREATES an empty host
  file for write-access opens of missing paths
  (disp=3 here), so BIND got a valid handle to a
  0-byte file: GetFileSize 0, ReadFile 0, frame repo
  record empty -> the .ocm executed and raised;
  salwrap's execute wrapper threw OMGExceptionImpl
  with message 'ERR_TID_MTDD,SVR2,ERR...' (12-char
  error name ERR_TID_MTDD is a literal in
  salwrap.dll). The harness's cxxeh MATCHED
  salwrap's own catch (frame[1], pHandler
  0x651163dc) but the exception-object copy ctor
  (0x6511f6ce) read unmapped memory -> run_with_seh
  aborted ('guest called abort/exit'). Harness
  limitation on the error path only; the fix is to
  not be on the error path.
- Side effect cleaned: run 222 left a 0-byte
  E7/010f...be7.acd on the mirror (harness-created);
  removed; E7 back to its 13 original .opf files,
  B4 back to 8 entries, system32 back to 16.
- sal_emu216.py = 215 + correct id16/subdir/name +
  a len(id16)==16 assert in the bind driver.
  Run 223 = same env.

### Run 223 (sal_emu216.py, bind0, correct ID) — file read whole; the .ocm PARSES the repo content as a struct-form document; raw .OMA rejected ERR_UNSUPPORTED

- Template mmap + stream OK; resolver _access
  exists=True; CreateFile disp=3; **ReadFile
  n=1298112 got=1298112 into an 'operator new' alloc
  (0x1254a0f0)** — the method read the whole staged
  .OMA. atpcd.ocm fetched + loaded.
- Then the VM raised, caught by the harness's s39
  raise instrumentation in the log:
  [s39 raise #1] kind=0x14402030 ret=0x10326ca4
  (INSIDE the container reader 0x10326c3b) with the
  REPO BUFFER 0x1254a0f0 on the reader's stack.
  Full text via wcslen: 'SAL INTERNAL ERROR:
  ERR_TID_MTDD,SVR2,ERR_LYR_SAL,ERR_MOD_MT,
  ERR_MT_COM,ERR_UNSUPPORTED,(kind:0x14402030
  ext:0x0(0)) in atpcd.ocm'. salwrap's execute
  wrapper wrapped it as OMGExceptionImpl and
  RETHREW; the harness's rethrow (obj=0) delivery
  is broken ('guest called abort/exit') — on real
  Windows BIND's __except(1) at 0x61606a1a would
  convert it to an HRESULT. The refusal itself is
  the module's, in its own words: the repo content
  is parsed as a struct-form value and raw EA3 is
  ERR_UNSUPPORTED at the MT-COM layer.
- => THE REPO FILE IS A STRUCT-FORM DOCUMENT, not
  the .OMA. (Run 220's oma1 'OPERAND_1,2,N
  expected' was this same judgment — S39's 'first
  frame input is the repo file's raw content' was
  right about the TRANSPORT and wrong about the
  FORMAT: raw bytes are fed, but the .ocm's first
  act is to parse them as a document.)
- The 5 real .acd files in the mirror are 47-48 B
  struct-form records: 30 80 | 04 <len> <inner
  30 80 | 04 10 <16B == filename bytes> | 02 04
  <int> | 02 01/02 <int> | 00 00> | 04 08 <8B> |
  00 00. They are OP-A's writeToFile products
  (filename = the 16 B OP-A's readback wrote back
  over the caller's id16 buffer; content = OP-A
  string B) — key/state records, no audio.
- THE CANDIDATE for a track's BIND repo document
  is therefore S38's envelope grammar (the same
  tree with the audio blob inside the inner
  container after key16 + two ints): exactly the
  wrap3/wrap4 documents S38/S39 built from OP-A's
  live envelope (key16=_env[8:24], blob8=_env[34:42]).
  Under S38's approximated frame those documents
  were judged as SOURCE FILES (STATUS 0/1) — under
  the real method they become the FILE, which is
  the slot they were always meant for.
- bind1 (wrap3 tree, indefinite containers,
  corpus-faithful) and bind2 (wrap4 tree, all
  lengths definite) are staged as the repo file
  content in sal_emu217.py, with ATRAC_S38_MODE=opa
  in the same process to mint the live OP-A
  envelope the builders slice. Run 224.
- Also logged: [ekbw150] KeyError spam during
  FUN_61604a42 (cosmetic logger breakage for
  omgatpcd return addresses).

### Run 227 (sal_emu220.py, s1h instrumented) — hooks walk VM values, not doc bytes; media-info method read as the next probe

- s1h: INVALID_DATA again (hooks cost nothing
  verdict-wise). The reader/compare hooks fired
  2,662 times but the traced data (e.g. cursor
  ecx=0x1240572b, bytes '01 01 05 3c 54...') is
  the VM's own VALUE structures around the
  module object — the host reader functions
  serve the VM's value model during executes,
  not a byte-cursor over my document. Low signal
  for the INVALID_DATA question; hooks disarmed
  cleanly, staging restored.
- OFFLINE: neither .opf root blob (d9cede...,
  daedf9...) is a plain SHA-1 of the raw frames,
  the whole .OMA, the A0 payload, the whole .ATP
  or its header. They are keyed values, like
  everything else in this system.
- MEDIA-INFO (FUN_61604e96) full read — a much
  better probe than BIND for 'what does the
  module see': args (this, ?, id16, outIntA,
  outIntB); it resolves the repo file (type 4),
  reads it WHOLE via OmgString::readFromFile,
  frame = {repo-string record (vt+0x70 via
  FUN_6160488c with the FUN_61604d62 descriptor)}
  + [int 2 selector] + [module] — NO template,
  NO session record. Readback: byte, string,
  int, int. **The host then memcmp's the reply
  string's first 16 bytes against the id16 —
  mismatch throws OMGException(2, 'bad ATP
  media info file') — and writes the two ints
  to *outIntA/*outIntB.** So selector 2 reports
  a repo document's identity + two quantities.
- Run 228 (sal_emu221.py): media-info against
  three repo contents: the wrap4 doc (mi-doc),
  the raw .OMA (mi-oma), the bare 44-B OP-A
  envelope (mi-env). Outcomes discriminate:
  EAX=0 + ints = the module recognizes the
  content; 'bad ATP media info file' = it
  answered but disowns the identity; a VM
  raise/status = it cannot read the content
  as a media file at all.

  The CID20/.opf namespace is a different key

  module extracts the doc's identity (buf16)
  and compares it against the [ebp+0xc]
  argument; I passed CID16 -> mismatch ->

  a separate, parse-level verdict.)

- Staging pristine at start: system32 = 16 entries,
  repo-mirror procfile/29/ = 9 files, E7/ = 14 files ✓.
- Reader addresses 0x10326ebb / 0x10326c3b / 0x10326dfb
  are NOT in any PE: the harness HEAP arena is
  0x10000000-0x20000000, so these are VM-GENERATED code
  blocks in the heap (block-pool addresses are stable
  across runs in this harness, S32). No Ghidra decomp
  exists for them in out/ (grep: no hits) — verification
  is empirical: S32's dumped code window
  (scratch/s32/raise-code-ret-10326f14.bin) disassembled
  0x10326ebb as the READ-NEXT-VALUE worker (arg =
  pointer-to-cursor; reads TAG byte at cursor; dispatch
  on (tag & 0x1f) via 24-case jump table 0x1032705c),
  and S40 run 227's hooks at all three entries + the
  blob-compare 0x10302cc0 fired 2,662 times during a
  real-method bind. S41 re-verifies by dumping +
  disassembling the entry bytes live in run 239.

  reader hooks mostly walk the VM's own VALUE
  structures, not doc bytes (example cursor 0x1240572b,
  bytes '01 01 05 3c 54...'). S41's tracer therefore
  CONTENT-MATCHES: for every hit it reads windows at
  each candidate pointer (stack args + ecx/edx/esi/edi
  + their derefs) and records the hit only when a
  window matches the staged document's bytes — that IS
  the "cursor inside the document" filter, robust to
  not knowing the doc copy's address in advance.

  artifacts (scratch/s38/opa-str1.bin, 44 B):
  30 80 [04 1c [30 80 [04 10 key16] [02 01 00]

### Desk (S43, banked traces) — reader sequences IDENTICAL for 168 hits; the fork is the LAST blobcmp's result

- seg1/seg2 (scratch/s43/): reader-hit KIND
  sequences of bb vs r3 (from blk traces) are
  IDENTICAL for all 168 hits r3 has; bb has 20
  more (hits #168-187, the inner parse + reply
  reads). Block-idx delta is a constant +28.
  => the doc judgment produces NO reader-visible
  difference until after the shared walk ends.
- seg3/seg4/seg5: all 10 blobcmp (0x10302cc0)
  calls share one call site (prev block
  0x10320454) in both traces. Calls #1-9 exit
  identically in bb and r3. Call #10 (bb block
  366162 / r3 block 366112) FORKS INSIDE the
  compare's tail: bb -> d31(cmpsb) d33 d3b d3f
  d47 = result 0 (EQUAL); r3 -> d33 d36 = result
  nonzero (DIFFER), then ~1,982 blocks of
  interpreter status-1 machinery, no more reads.
  (r3's missing d31 block entry = a Unicorn TB
  quirk; the exit path d36 is unambiguous.)
- blobcmp DISASSEMBLED (heap-dump115): it is the
  VM's value-compare op. Operands = top two
  value-stack slots A=[P-8], B=[P-4], P=[esp+8]
  at entry. Both handles must carry tag bits
  0x80000000 (else raise kind 0x14402010).
  len = [h*4+4], data = [h*4+8] (value table);
  repe cmpsb over min(lenA,lenB); result 0 iff
  bytes equal AND lengths equal; else +/-1
  (byte order, then length order). Result is
  stored back over slot A (16-bit masked).
  Caller block 0x10320454 = the interpreter's
  generic opcode dispatch pushing [ebp-0x88]
  then calling the op via the opcode table.
=> GATE = compare #10's operands. Next: hook
blobcmp entry during the execute (bb + r3),
dump both operands' lengths + data bytes per
call. That names what is compared.

'LabelGate/OpenMG Type-T' -> +1; repeats).
CALL #10 IS THE GATE:
- bb: A = e225ac50f7b1195f (8 B, the doc's elem2)
  vs B = e225ac50f7b1195f -> RESULT 0 -> ACCEPT;
  a bb-only call #11 then compares the 20-B
  global seed f1e9db56...f67a7 == itself -> 0
  (accept-side crypto setup).
- r3: A = e225ac50f7b1195f (r3's elem2, SAME as
  bb's) vs B = 6a3b1254e012a465 -> RESULT 1 ->
  REJECT (status 1 machinery follows).
=> A = the DELIVERED elem2 (blob8); B = an 8-byte
check the program COMPUTES over elem1 (the inner
blob's bytes). blob8 is a check/MAC over the
inner serialization. This one mechanism explains
the whole evidence base: any change to the inner
(R2/R3/T2/w6/w11/w3s: +int, +audio, trailing
bytes) invalidates blob8; R1 broke elem2 itself;
the module's own status-0 reply re-stamps the
ints AND mints a fresh blob8 (bb-str2). S38's
payload processing bypassed this compare via
pre-materialized values. Doc-shape space was
never the problem — the check value was.
NEXT: (1) collect (inner -> blob8) sample pairs
(bb; bb-str2 ints (1296064,1); the real .acd repo
records; S38 artifacts); (2) fit the check
algorithm offline; (3) build an audio doc in
S38's payload shape with a correctly computed
blob8 and drive it — gate should pass and the
payload path should run.

### S43 results 10b–11 — fitting battery zero; the gate routine is SHA-1; input capture armed

Desk fitting (scratch/s43/fit1.py, fit2.py, fit3.py): **zero hits** across
HMAC-SHA1/MD5/SHA256 (keys: d2, gseed, key16, id16), raw SHA-1/MD5/SHA256
digest windows, SHA-1 over key16‖digest framings, DES-CBC last-block MACs
(keys d2 halves, key16 halves, gseed halves; IV 0 / digest-derived), EDE-CBC
MACs, the exact S35 op-11 reply core, CRC32/CRC64/FNV/Adler — against 7
positive (inner → blob8) samples (bb, bb-str2, five real .acd records) with
the r3 document's B held as negative control. The block-window analysis
(scratch/s43/seg6.py) shows the computation between reader hit #166 and
blobcmp #10 runs through both arms identically: a ~8.6 KB fully unrolled
routine at 0x10304110–0x103064eb (one block hit exactly ×256 in each arm).
Disassembling its setup found the state initialization at 0x10304146: the
five words 0x67452301, 0xefcdab89, 0x98badcfe, 0x10325476, 0xc3d2e1f0 stored
consecutively — **the SHA-1 initial state H0–H4. The gate computation is a
SHA-1.** fit4 (SHA-1/MD5 × 11 input shapes — incl. key16‖int-sers with the
container close stripped, definite-form re-serializations, doc-with-elem2
zeroed, length-prefixed — × 10 digest extractions: offsets, per-word
byte-swap, XOR folds, reversals): **zero hits**. So the hashed input is a
shape not yet guessed, the digest is post-processed, and/or extra context is
hashed. Decisive step: capture the routine's actual input + final digest
from a live drive — sal_emu241 mode `sha1in` hooks the SHA-1 setup block
0x10304140 (registers + buffers at every live pointer), the body entry
0x10304330, and blobcmp entries (digest read from the state buffer at
compare time). Run 249 = sal_emu241 sha1in, drives bb + r3.

### S43 result 14 — THE GATE IS LIFTED: blob8 = SHA1(inner ‖ "ATRAC CD" ‖ const-trailer)[0:8]

Run 251 (gatewatch, scratch/s43/gatewatch-bb.txt, 4560 events) caught the
gate SHA-1 end-to-end. The ctx lives at 0x103ed700 (H stored there, scrubbed
afterwards — hence run 250's zeros). The message is assembled at ctx+0x1c by
copying dwords from a single source buffer holding the module's record
image, and is (52 B for the envelope):

```
message = inner_serialization ‖ "ATRAC CD" ‖
          01 0f 50 00 ‖ 00 04 00 00 ‖ 00 00 00 16 ‖ 72 5f 9b e7
blob8   = SHA-1(message)[0:8]
```

(SHA-1 length counter 0x1a0=416 bits confirms the 52-B total; the digest's
first two words, stored LE, are exactly blob8.) Desk-verified
(scratch/s43/fit5 logic, run inline): **all 7 positive samples match**
(bb envelope, bb-str2 reply envelope, and the five real .acd records minted
on the user's machine) AND the r3 negative control reproduces the module's
own computed B (6a3b1254e012a465) for the truncated inner. The 24-B trailer
is a module constant — identical across ceremonies and machines; its tail
fields (010f5000…, …725f9be7) echo the CID16 record family
(010f500000040000004f7e16725f9be7). Verbatim Sony literal for the record:
the ASCII domain string **"ATRAC CD"** sits inside the hashed message.
Consequences: blob8 is mintable for ANY inner — the entire doc-shape space
reopens. Next: build an audio doc in S38's payload arrangement
[key16 blob, payload string (tag 06), int 1296064, int 1] with a correctly
computed blob8, drive slice then full-size, harvest string C.

### Desk census (S44) — the transform is NOT in omgatpcd.dll

out/decomp-omgatpcd.c holds exactly three
execute-driving methods, matching the S29
census: FUN_616054cd (selector 0, OP-A key
blob), FUN_6160571e (selector 1, BIND record
bind), FUN_61604e96 (selector 2, media-info:
reads back ONE string + two ints and memcmps
the string against the caller's id16 —
"bad ATP media info file" on mismatch; a
verifier, not a transform). No other method
builds a SAL stream. So no atpcd.ocm selector
reachable from omgatpcd.dll transforms audio.
The payload transform for non-library tracks
must live in (a) the never-delivered burn
host itself, keyed by BIND outputs
(str1/str3 32-B key strings, buf8 BlockSeed,
the stamped header), or (b) another DLL's
stream machinery entirely. S37's d2-CBC
stands as the library-path cipher.

### Static hunt (S45) — NO native crypto anywhere in the corpus

scratch/s45/fingerprint.py + fingerprint2.py over 323 PE files
(169 unique by md5) in the two OpenMG copies + SonicStage app
dir + staged set:
- Crypto constants: NO DES S-box (byte or u32 form), NO SHA-1
  K/IV tables, NO SHA-256 K, NO MD5 T, NO AES S-box, NO TEA
  delta — in ANY held native binary. Only CRC32 tables
  (RGraph.dll, dunzip32.dll — zip libs). Caveat: constants
  could hide as scattered code immediates, but a DES/SHA
  implementation needs its tables SOMEWHERE as data; none exist.
- CryptoAPI: NO module imports Crypt*/BCrypt* functions.
- atpcd.ocm / "BIND" references: omgatpcd.dll is the ONLY
  module in the corpus that names atpcd.ocm (and it imports
  salwrap.dll). MemStick.dll and omgconv.dll mention
  "BlockSeed" (1-2 ASCII hits; MemStick = the MS-device bind
  path, off the CD path). OMGUtils.dll + omgatpcd.dll mention
  SecureAudioHeader. Omgjbox.exe (5.96 MB, the burn host)
  imports NO omg/sal DLL statically (COM-loaded at runtime);
  8 ASCII 'atp' + 8 'bind' hits, no BlockSeed.
=> CONCLUSION (static): OpenMG's payload crypto does NOT live
in native code in ANY held module. Every crypto operation in
this corpus runs as SAL VM bytecode (proven pattern: op 11's
DES+SHA-1 are VM programs; the gate SHA-1 is a VM routine at
heap 0x10304110). If the general transform is in held code at
all, it is a SAL program — in atpcd.ocm under an operand
arrangement the real omgatpcd frame never uses (cf. S38), or
in another .ocm (importer/at3toomg/hdwm family) driven by a
host this corpus's .ATP path never calls. The desk battery
below tests whether the transform even NEEDS code-diving:
maybe the key is derivable from per-track artifacts we hold.

### Battery v2 — ORACLE (stated before running)

Subjects and oracles, strongest first:
1. Dreamer (TRK000E1) + Electric (TRK000DC): KNOWN PLAINTEXT
   (pairs/dreamer.raw, pairs/electric.raw). Key screen is
   IV-FREE and exact: for CBC, D_K(ct[i]) XOR ct[i-1] must
   equal pt[i] for all i>=1 under the true key regardless of
   IV; screen blocks 1..32, confirm full-track + solve IV
   from block 0. ECB/CFB/OFB/CTR/per-frame variants screened
   encrypt-side on the first 16 blocks.
2. Talk (TRK0000A): structure oracle — every plaintext frame
   starts 0x3A (VERIFIED: 3444/3444 ignite, 3341/3341
   dreamer, 3039/3039 electric frames). Decrypt and count
   0x3A at 376-B stride; true key gives ~100%.
3. Disc-2 sample (12 tracks): same 0x3A structure oracle per
   track under per-track derivation schemas (schema must hit
   on MANY tracks with each track's OWN header fields).
S37 coverage (NOT re-run): battery1 key set (BS/IV/Ignite-seed/
d2 windows/ckey windows/sha1[0:8] of BS,SEED,BS+SEED,SEED+BS/
md5[0:8]) x {CBC enc/dec misuse,CFB64,OFB,CTR,ECB,CFB8} x
IVs {hdrIV,0,BS,SEED}; battery2's 28 DC/E1 candidates (own
mgd raw, XORs with d2, sha1(d2||x)[0:8], DES-block derivations,
op-11 replies + digest1/W24 windows for both name tags) x
{CBC,CFB,OFB,ECB}; battery3 IV-free CBC screen of the union +
Ignite-seed triple keys; pt^ct period scan (dead <=4096).
V2 NEW SPACE: (a) sha1/md5 of OWN-field inputs at ALL digest
windows + word-swapped windows + XOR folds; (b) gate-style
domain constructions sha1(x || "ATRAC CD" || trailer);
(c) own-mgd EDE triples (battery3's triples used IGNITE's
seed, never the track's own mgd); (d) per-frame CBC restart;
(e) SHA-1 counter keystream XOR; (f) d2-CBC over word/byte
swapped plaintext forms; (g) key material extracted from the
track's OWN .opf document (Toshiba corpus procfile — the
BIND-stamped per-track artifact) and its .acd key16.

### BIND-output link (S45 step 4) — per-track artifacts EXIST

.opf linkage is by CID: .opf filename == the track's header
CID20, filed under procfile/<CID last byte>/. VERIFIED:
.opf blob[3] == header CID20 for Dreamer, Electric, Talk,
and disc-2 tracks 1-3; 12/12 sampled disc-2 tracks have a
.opf in the Toshiba corpus. So per-track BIND-side artifacts
ARE recoverable for real burned tracks — and their blob
material (A/B/C/D) was fully consumed by battery v2 as key
candidates: it does not key the payloads under any tested
construction. BIND's str1/str3 themselves are not stored in
the .opf (str0 = the .opf is what BIND stamps; str1/str3
live only in the execute's reply, never persisted in any
held artifact we found).

### ⭐ SESSION 45 WALL — the general transform is NOT in the held corpus

What was searched: all 169 unique PE binaries in both
OpenMG copies + the SonicStage app + the archive dir +
the staged set (constants, strings ASCII+UTF-16, imports);
the full SAL selector census of the only .ATP module;
both BIND call arrangements at full size.

keys/track on two known-plaintext tracks + Talk + a
12-track disc-2 sample + 39,629 global-artifact windows.
What is ruled out: (1) native-code transform in ANY held
module — no cipher tables, no CryptoAPI, anywhere;
(2) atpcd.ocm selector 1 as the transform, under either
calling arrangement; (3) every key derivable from the
track's header, its .opf, the ceremony values (d2/ckey/
gseed/X8/op-11 replies), or the global key artifacts,
under DES/3DES in CBC/ECB/CFB/OFB/CTR/per-frame modes,
SHA-1 keystreams, and swapped-plaintext forms.
THE ONE MISSING INGREDIENT: the identity of the SAL
program (or host component) that the real burn pipeline
runs between EA3-encoding a fresh source and the pre-BIND
.ATP — a component present in NEITHER held OpenMG copy's
.ATP path. Candidates the held code cannot exclude:

OpenMG build than the two held copies (4.3 + ULTIMATE) —
the discs' burn-era build is unknown, and an earlier/later
omgatpcd+atpcd pair may have had a payload method this
pair lacks; (b) a burn-addon component never copied from
the machine (both dumps are the OpenMG + app dirs only).
What would break the wall: a transform input/output pair
from the general path, or the burn-era module pair, or
the missing component itself. S37's payload_cipher.py
