### Task A — static census of the 60 (scratch/s46/census_a.py/.json/.txt)

Stored-form taxonomy (sal_skeleton phase-4/5 tooling, re-run on
the Toshiba copies): 25 class-01 family (shared skeleton +
8192-B shared chunk + per-module u16, opaque continuation),
26 class-02 (no skeleton; ~8.5 KB opaque head clusters),
9 stubs (2 B), init.ocm in its own wrapper class (582,636 B).
EVERY real body is high-entropy (H = 7.78-8.00): the raw .ocm
yields NO plaintext method table and no usable strings (0-16
chance ASCII hits per file; zero crypto/format strings in any
module). Method enumeration from the raw file is impossible
with current tooling — the program exists in plaintext only
after the VM loader de-obfuscates it. The census therefore
follows the S44 entry point: the DRIVER side.

Driver map (PEs in OpenMG+AVLib+app naming each module; every
referencing PE also imports salwrap.dll):
- **omgconv.dll drives the whole conversion family**:
  at3toomg, at3topcm, pcmtoomg, mp3toomg2, msatoomg, msatopcm,
  omgtomsa, Omgtomp3, omgtopcm2, vmcconv, vmc0conv, mp3tovmc,
  vmc. convcom.dll co-drives mp3tovmc, vmc, vmc0conv.
- omgconv2.dll drives kdrm, nc, osss. kdrmcom.dll also kdrm.
- omgmisc.dll drives the importer family (importer,
  importer_key, ktunes, ktunes_key, mss, restore, sc, updater,
  otk_procfile, querypf stubs).
- Device families: hdwmcom/hdwmgmcom (hdwm bind), wmcom
  (mgrms, mgsoft, wm2, wmimport2), OmgNetMD (netmd), omglgd
  (lgd), omglgdimport (lgdimport), OmgiiCOM (omgii), umdcom
  (umd), omgl3 (l3, mqimport), pfcom (migratepf, querycif).
- salwrap.dll itself names the ceremony set (init, init2,
  icv, maclist, region, ekbman, mp4comp, botamoti, kdrm, l3).
- No PE names bot-dnk/hdd-dnk/msa-dnk/umd-dnk (loaded by
  constructed name or salwrap-internal table) or the
  remaining stubs (keyinfo, mp3toomg, nwwm, omgtopcm,
  otk_ake, otk_trans, wm).

Class-01 template clusters: the *toomg/*topcm conversion
modules are near-whole-body identical to each other (common
prefixes 23,561-23,588 B on ~23.6 KB bodies) — ONE program
template parameterized per direction, not 13 distinct
programs. vmcconv == vmc0conv past 23,582 B. Class-02 head
clusters pair atpcd with lgd (CP 8,471), botamoti with
init2/region (8,468), lgdimport with umd (8,468).

S44-transfer desk step: decompile omgconv.dll (the conversion
driver) and census its stream-building methods + selectors,
exactly as S29/S44 did for omgatpcd.dll.

### Task A desk census — omgconv.dll (out/decomp-s46-omgconv.c)

Ghidra project proj-omgconv (scripts/SalDecompileOmgconvS46.java,
patterned on S29's atpcd census): 403 functions >= 80 B.
**25 stream-building functions** reference conversion modules
(scratch/s46/analyze_omgconv2.py, omgconv_census.json):
at3toomg x3, at3topcm x3, mp3toomg2 x1, mp3tovmc x1,
msatoomg x2, msatopcm x3, pcmtoomg x5, Omgtomp3 x1,
omgtomsa x1, omgtopcm2 x3, vmc0conv x2, vmcconv x1 (one
function selects vmc0conv/vmcconv). All use the salwrap
stream-object vtable directly (+0x40 create, +0x6c/+0x70/
+0x74/+0x84 appends, +0x4c/+0x8c/+0x9c/+0xa0 execute and
readback slots) — the same stream class omgatpcd's helpers
wrap, driven here without the wrapper layer.
Shapes of note:
- 7 functions carry the copy-control gate: on a module
  reply value they throw OMGException 0x502 "Watermark :
  NO MORE COPY" / 0x503 "Watermark : PHASE 1 TRIGGER"
  (at3toomg x2, pcmtoomg x3, at3topcm x1 + one more) — the
  *toomg path enforces SCMS-style watermark state returned
  BY the module.
- 2 functions contain byte-copy loops over module output
  buffers and accumulate a byte total (FUN_10019ee0
  at3toomg 1,140 B; FUN_1001f070 pcmtoomg 1,232 B): the
  chunk-moving steps of the conversion pipelines.
- FUN_1001ccf0 (pcmtoomg) references "generateMAC:
  Invalid CID" — the to-OMG path computes the content MAC
  (the .OMA MAC field), consistent with the module doing
  container/rights work while frames pass through.
- Established product check: the library .OMA these
  pipelines produce is CLEAN EA3 (S37: ignite-library.OMA
  frames byte-identical to plaintext). So under omgconv's
  driving, the conversion modules do NOT encrypt audio.
  Whether the same modules transform under a different
  driver's operands is exactly what a census cannot see
  from the driver side alone — see Task C.

### Task B — AVLib native scan (scratch/s46/avlib_scan.py)

All 73 PE binaries in the Toshiba AVLib subtree fingerprinted
with S45's exact constant patterns + brief string set (ascii
and UTF-16LE) + imports/exports (avlib_fingerprint.json,
avlib_deep.json):
- Crypto constants: NO SHA-1 K/IV, NO SHA-256 K, NO MD5 T,
  NO AES S-box, NO TEA delta, NO DES u32 form anywhere.
  CRC32 tables only in the two OmgAp COM components (zip
  machinery, as in S45). **Two genuine DES carriers exist**
  (byte-form S-box, VERIFIED as the complete 512-B table,
  all 8 boxes, followed by DES permutation data):
  OpdClie.dll @0x6f1d8 and sonychk.dll @0x3178. Neither is
  on the CD path: OpdClie is the CLIÉ/FOMA device COM
  component (IPmClie / IOmgApMsControl interfaces, no
  .ATP/.OMA/BlockSeed strings); sonychk.dll is a 24-KB
  single-export checker (_sonychk), no .ATP strings. So
  AVLib is not crypto-free — but its only DES lives in a
  PDA-device component and a license checker.
- CryptoAPI/BCrypt: NO imports in any of the 73.
- salwrap: NO AVLib binary references salwrap at all —
  nothing in AVLib drives SAL modules directly. (16 name
  OMGUtils as a string, none import it — COM/late-bind
  path/format helpers.)
- .ATP vocabulary: ONLY CDRFormatter.dll carries .ATP +
  BlockSeed + SecureAudioHeader together (ascii). The
  device PAC components (Frank/MsPro/Aura + PACAPI) carry
  .OMA/BIND/BlockSeed — Walkman/MS bind paths, off-CD.

In depth (the nine):
- **CDRFormatter.dll** (360,448 B, 195 C++ exports): THE
  ATRAC-CD format + burn engine. Classes CAudioHeader,
  CFringe, CGroup, CPlayList, CSecureAudioHeader,
  CCDRFormatter. CSecureAudioHeader is a pure field
  container: Get/Set for BlockSeed, InitVector, MgCk,
  MgDUpper, MgDLower (the header +0x50 mgd, split),
  Connum, BlockID, EKBVersion — setters/getters ONLY, no
  crypto-named method, and the binary holds NO cipher
  constants. CCDRFormatter::BeginToCreateTrack receives a
  caller-built CSecureAudioHeader; SendAudioBlock(const
  byte*, uint) feeds audio blocks to the writer; Burn/
  BurnFile/RetryBurn write the medium. It formats and
  burns what it is GIVEN — it does not compute the secure
  header's key fields and contains no transform.
- **CDWalkman.dll** (229,376 B): COM server, imports
  CDRFormatter.dll — the CD-Walkman burn driver sitting
  on top of the formatter. SecureAudioHeader string =
  the class name. No crypto constants, no salwrap.
- **OpcAtp.dll** (69,632 B): OmgPcAtp COM component —
  IOmgPcAtp/IOmgPcFile: CanRead/CanPlay/CanConvertFile/
  GetFileInfo over .ATP files; delegates to
  OmgATPFormatChecker + OmgPcATPFringe COM objects
  (elsewhere) after an OpenMG-version check. A format
  CHECKER/reader for PC-side .ATP files — no payload
  writing, no crypto constants, no salwrap.
- **SonyCDWriterSS.dll / OpdCdrSS.dll / PrimoWriterSS.dll**
  (CdrSS subfolder): COM servers over the Primo burning
  engine (powrprof/shlwapi imports only) — raw medium
  writing. No format vocabulary, no crypto.
- **OpcCd.dll / OpcCd2.dll**: COM servers importing
  WINMM — CD audio playback components. **OpcOmg.dll**:
  COM server (urlmon import) — OMG playback/info. None
  reference .ATP creation, salwrap, or any cipher table.

### Task A desk census — convcom.dll + omgconv2.dll

6f93493db94afd28f89404441dae8895 — the largest real SAL

(debug strings): EA3TOPCM, EAT3TOPCM, MP3TOOMG,

### ⭐ Task C candidate — PCMTOATP in omgconv2.dll, driving nc.ocm

The fresh-source .ATP production path EXISTS in held code
and was never censused because S44/S45 followed
omgatpcd.dll only:
- **FUN_61947fce (2,242 B) — "PCMTOATP::GET_RIPPING_CKEY"**:
  builds an nc.ocm stream (ctor FUN_619015fc, the same
  helper shape as omgatpcd's stream ctor): reads a
  procfile object buffer (vt+0x18, class 3), appends a
  blob descriptor (this+0x44/+0x98) via salwrap vt+0x70,
  two string/blob appends (FUN_61901c76/FUN_6190139c),
  then int operands **6** and **0x11 (17)** via
  FUN_61901334, then executes (FUN_61901383). Preconditions:
  a mode field == 2 and a ripping-enable flag byte. The
  ripping content key (ckey) is therefore MINTED BY an
  nc.ocm execute — consistent with S36's finding that
  ckey24 is ephemeral: it is fetched per rip from the
  key module, not derived host-side.
- **FUN_61948d39 (2,312 B) — the PCMTOATP chunk feeder**:
  memcpy's caller PCM into a staging buffer (base
  this+0x44, capacity this+0x94, cursor this+0x98),
  accumulates byte totals (this+0x180/0x184), and on
  buffer-full/flush hands capacity-sized blocks to a
  per-block job (FUN_61947e97 creates it, FUN_61947e68
  feeds it — both sub-80-B helpers, below the decompile
  inventory cutoff), then drains an output queue with
  frame-size arithmetic (this+0x70 = frame size;
  quotient/remainder handling at 61948d39 tail). Shape:
  bulk PCM in -> framed blocks -> nc.ocm job -> framed
  output. THIS is the bulk-audio pipeline the brief
  asked Task A to flag.
- FUN_6194bd84 (1,390 B) is a second GET_RIPPING_CKEY
  site; FUN_6194c3b3 (1,677 B) carries "bad AT3
  encoding size" / "tmpBuf" in the same class region.

What this does NOT yet prove: that nc.ocm's program
performs the payload cipher itself (vs. keying/framing
while a native stage transforms) — omgconv2 contains no
cipher constants (S45 fingerprint: clean) and AVLib's
CD stack contains none either (Task B), so if the
transform is not in nc.ocm it has no identified native
home left in the corpus. Per the brief, STOP here:
no driving, no battery. Session 47 brief = drive the
PCMTOATP pipeline (or its nc.ocm stream directly,
replicating FUN_61947fce's operand order) in the
harness against known PCM and compare against a
general-path .ATP (Dreamer/Electric pair).

## Session 47 — 2026-11-02 (drive the PCMTOATP pipeline: nc.ocm)

GOAL (brief): replicate FUN_61947fce's nc.ocm stream exactly, drive
the FUN_61948d39 feeder's per-block job with a known input block,
and determine WITH BYTE EVIDENCE what nc.ocm does on this path:
(a) transform reproduced vs a real general-path .ATP, or
(b) keys/frames but no cipher (name the next suspect), or
(c) blocked at a named wall.

### Premises VERIFIED before any drive

- omgconv2.dll md5 a56c74ecc3ed65df7414e96d77f52654, 659,456 B —
  verified in BOTH the wineprefix held copy and the Toshiba tree
  (toshiba-trees-2026-11-02/sony-shared/.../OpenMG/omgconv2.dll).
- nc.ocm md5 6f93493db94afd28f89404441dae8895, 62,809 B — verified
  in BOTH places too. Brief's numbers are exact.
- Staging pristine at session start: scratch/root/Windows/system32
  16 files, repo-mirror/procfile/29 9 files, procfile/E7 14 files.
- Oracles: pairs/dreamer.raw = 1,256,216 B (Dreamer EA3 plaintext;
  exactly TRK000E1.ATP size 1,257,336 minus the 1,120 B header).
  pairs/electric.raw = 1,142,664 B (exactly TRK000DC.ATP payload).

### Stream grammar RE-DERIVED from decomp + capstone (S46's map checked)

All omgconv2 stream wrappers call the salwrap manager singleton
through import [0x6195e4d8] = salwrap.dll export salwrap1 (the same
manager the harness's _s38 helpers drive via cell 0x651411dc).
Wrapper slot key (omgconv2 wrapper -> manager vtable slot):
- FUN_619015fc(S, &name): module-descriptor ctor; S = {0, 0,
  std::string name at +8} — identical layout to the harness's
  _s38_module / _s38_mkstream products.
- FUN_61901305(S, n): mgr vt+0x40 create doc buffer (0x200 used).
- FUN_61901351(S, desc): mgr vt+0x70 append BLOB from {ptr,len}.
- FUN_6190136a(S, desc): mgr vt+0x6c append from {ptr,len};
  FUN_61901c76(S, &string) extracts {data,size} from a string
  (SSO: data at +4, size at +0x14, cap at +0x18 — the string
  objects in this class carry a 4-byte prefix) and calls 6190136a.
- FUN_6190139c(S): append SESSION RECORD: p = mgr vt+0x8();
  append {p,16} via vt+0x6c. Same as harness _s38_sessionrec.
- FUN_61901334(S, lo, hi): mgr vt+0x74 append int64.
- FUN_61901383(S, D): mgr vt+0x64 bind stream S to descriptor D.
- EXECUTE: mgr vt+0x0(in_cell, out_cell, 0, 0).
- Readbacks on the out cell: FUN_619013e7 = vt+0x8c read byte;
  FUN_619013cb = vt+0x9c read int (8-byte readback, EAX used);
  FUN_6190141f = vt+0x80 read string; FUN_61901403 = vt+0x84 read
  blob into a {ptr,len} descriptor; vt+0x7c(cell, &struct) reads a
  value object {ptr, len}; vt+0x44(cell) frees a stream.
- FUN_61952248 (called after stream C's execute) is
  CoTaskMemFree — a no-op for interpretation.

### FUN_61947fce (GET_RIPPING_CKEY) — full re-derivation

thiscall, ECX = pipeline object, NINE stack args (the three
interface wrappers at 0x619488c8 / 0x619488f0 / 0x61948918 push 9
values and pass ECX from their own first arg). Arg map (stack
offsets in the callee): +0x08 int (stream C element); +0x0c/+0x10
= {ptr,len} blob descriptor (stream C); +0x14 = pointer to a
20-byte buffer (must be nonzero; the final memcpy writes 20 bytes
there); +0x18 unused-so-far scratch arg; +0x1c = out-pointer
(zeroed at entry); +0x20 = handle (must be nonzero); +0x24 = flag
byte -> this+0x178; +0x28 = mode (must be 2..3) -> this+0x190.
Validation: null +0x14 / +0x1c / +0x20 -> E_POINTER 0x80004003;
mode outside 2..3 -> E_INVALIDARG 0x80070057.
Object fields: +0x00 name string; +0x08 embedded CRITICAL_SECTION
(the entry guard FUN_61908a05 enters the CS at this+8);
+0x20 int (compared against 2); +0x24 string (mutable — stream C's
reply string is read INTO it); +0x44 staging buffer ptr; +0x70
frame size (feeder); +0x90 procfile object (smart ptr); +0x94
staging capacity; +0x98 staged length; +0xf0 8-byte field (stream
C readback target); +0x160 16-byte chain state; +0x170 chain
capacity (0x10); +0x174 counter; +0x178 flag byte; +0x17c saved
cursor; +0x180/+0x184 byte totals; +0x188/+0x189 flags (feeder);
+0x190 mode.
Streams built (ALL bound to a descriptor for "nc.ocm"):
- STREAM A (selector 6) — only when mode==2 AND this+0x98 != 0
  AND this+0x20 != 2: [blob70 {*(this+0x44), *(this+0x98)}]
  [string6c = obj90->vt+0x18(&str, class 3)] [sessrec]
  [string6c = this+0x24 string] [int64 6] [int64 17]. After the
  execute: this+0x17c = this+0x98.
- STREAM B (selector 5) — whenever this+0x20 != 2, inside the
  flag!=0 block, after A: [sessrec] [string6c = result of
  obj90->vt+0x28(&str)] [int64 5] [int64 17]. Readback byte
  (0x8c) + int (0x9c) are extracted, and the extracted value is
  then WRITTEN into the procfile by obj90's sibling call
  procfile()->vt+0x58(value, "PCMTOATP::GET_RIPPING_CKEY", 0, 0)
  — the debug string is literally the procfile record NAME.
- STREAM C (selector 1) — unconditional: [blob70 = the object
  NAME string (this+0x00) extracted by FUN_61901c57] [int64 =
  this+0x20] [blob70 {arg+0x0c, arg+0x10}] [sessrec]
  [int64 = arg+0x08] [string6c = this+0x24] [int64 1]
  [int64 17]. Readback: vt+0x80 read string + vt+0x84 read blob,
  BOTH with descriptor {this+0xf0, 8}; then (only when the entry
  flag == 0) two more vt+0x80 string reads (strA, strB), and
  obj = procfile()->vt+0x20(strA, strB) is stored at this+0x90;
  the final output is 20 bytes memcpy'd from the data of the
  string returned by obj->vt+0x10(&str) into *(arg+0x14). When
  the entry flag != 0, obj90 is instead fetched up front via
  procfile()->vt+0x8(*(arg+0x14)).
So the "ripping ckey" call is mostly RECORD-KEEPING around three
small nc.ocm jobs (selectors 6, 5, 1); the actual per-block
transform lives in the feeder's stream (next).

### FUN_61948d39 (chunk feeder) — full re-derivation

Preconditions: this+0x90 != 0 (else E_UNEXPECTED 0x80004005);
finalize-mode arg (+0x14) in 0..2; out-params zeroed.
Caller data is memcpy'd into the staging buffer at this+0x44 +
cursor this+0x98 (capacity this+0x94); byte totals accumulate at
this+0x180/+0x184. Full blocks are queued as 0x18-byte list nodes
(FUN_61947ce0 copies a 4-dword queue descriptor into node[2..5]:
node[2] = data ptr, node[3] = length, node[4] = capacity,
node[5] = length again — for both queue builds in the feeder the
4th descriptor dword equals the length).
PER-BLOCK STREAM (selector 2), elements in order:
[int64 node[5] = block length] [int64 this+0x17c] [sessrec]
[blob70 {this+0x160, 16} = CHAIN STATE] [int64 lastFlag: 2 iff
mode==2 AND this is the last block, else 0] [blob70 {block data,
block len}] [string6c = this+0x24 string] [int64 2] [int64 17];
bind "nc.ocm"; execute.
PER-BLOCK READBACK: byte via 0x8c; int via 0x9c; a VALUE OBJECT
via vt+0x7c -> {ptr, len} appended to the output list
(FUN_6192fd6e) — these are the framed output blocks; two strings
via 0x80; and min(reply-string size, this+0x170) bytes of one
reply string are memcpy'd into this+0x160 — THE CHAIN STATE
EVOLVES FROM EACH BLOCK'S REPLY (a per-track chained transform,
structurally like CBC feedback but at whole-string granularity).
this+0x17c is reset to 0 after each block.
Finalize modes (arg+0x14): 0 = drain a partial queue node raw;
1 = frame-align trim using frame size this+0x70 (mode-2 objects
get an even-frame adjustment); 2 = pad the tail to a full frame
with zeros and set this+0x188 = 1. The frame arithmetic is
applied to INPUT lengths, so the feeder's input is framed data
(376 B units at 64 kbps), despite the PCMTOATP name.
omgconv2 imports: procfile from pfcom.DLL, salwrap1 + salwrap0
from salwrap.dll, OmgSinf convertToSalSinf from OMGUtils.dll.

### Drive plan (stream-level first, S38–S44 idiom)

sal_emu252 = sal_emu251 + an S47 block (env ATRAC_S47_MODE)
inserted after the S44 block. Other session modes off. Drives:
sel 2 with dreamer.raw blocks (state16 = zeros, ctx = 0, str24
candidates '' then the Dreamer CID hex); sel 1 / sel 5 / sel 6
replications with the same string candidates. Readbacks in the
native order (byte, int, value-object via 0x7c, string, string,
blob) with raise capture via _s39_raises.

### Run 260 — harness bug only (no module evidence)

sal_emu252 (= sal_emu251 + the S47 block before _ctl('after-pfcom'))

### Runs 261 — second harness-side miss (helper signatures)

_s38_mkstream in THIS generation takes a tag and returns
(cell, handle); _s38_desc takes raw bytes; readbacks write into
caller buffers. My S47 block was written against the summarized
signatures and failed all drives with TypeError before any module
code ran. The block was rewritten against the real definitions
(read from sal_emu251.py lines 3496–3650): streams are CELLS,
ints append via slot 0x74 (cell, lo, hi), the module descriptor
is the inline 0x30-byte struct {0,0,string@+8} (parameterized to
"nc.ocm"), readbacks mirror _s38_bind_readback, plus a vt+0x7c
value-object read for the feeder's framed output. Run 262 next.

### Run 262 (sal_emu252) — streams build, nc.ocm loads, executes RAISE

First real drives. The stream machinery worked end to end: all
appends returned the stream handle, the module descriptor bound,
and the manager FETCHED nc.ocm by name from the repo-mirror
(62,809 B read; the [fetch111] machinery). The session record
appended is the known 16 bytes 01 0f 50 00 00 04 00 00 00 00 00 16
72 5f 9b e7. Every execute then RAISED (the SAL modules' normal
error channel — a C++ throw through FUN_65120160):
- sel2-nostr: kind 0x14402020 (ERR_MALFORMED_INPUT), ret
  0x10326c6f, ctx 0x12312ff8, stk[3:11] = 0, 0f3fa208, 0x9fa,
  0x3d, 103ed803, 0, 10326c35, 12312ff8.
- sel2-cid (only the final string differs: Dreamer CID hex):
  kind 0x14402030 (ERR_UNSUPPORTED — the container reader's
  class-1-or-3 gate, cf. S32 notes), ret 0x10326ca4 — the SAME
  ret as S39's oma1/bindscan raise, so the VM image layout is
  stable across modules and runs.
- sel1-nostr: 0x14402020 at 0x10326c6f (stk word5 103f09a3).
- sel6-nostr: 0x14402020 at 0x10326c6f (stk word5 103f09d3).
- sel5-nostr: no raise of its own, but its appends/binds were
  already failing — see the poison note; its verdict is VOID.
POISON NOTE: an uncaught module throw leaves the harness in
"cxxthrow pending dispatch" state (Emu.pending_cxx + guest CRT
state): readbacks immediately after a throwing execute stop
before running, and later drives in the same run degrade
(sel5's int append hit UC_ERR_WRITE_UNMAPPED; its module bind
died inside msvcp71 std::string assign). Stream-level executes
DO still run the module after a prior throw (sel2-cid, sel6
raised genuinely), but NOTHING after a throw is fully trusted.
Mitigations for run 263: clear e.pending_cxx at each drive
start, and instrument instead of guessing —
READER HOOKS at the S32 addresses (container reader
0x10326c3b, read-value worker 0x10326ebb, cmp-01 site
0x10326efa) logging every value pulled (cursor + tag bytes)
and the GOT byte at the expected-0x01 compare, plus a dump of
the built in-stream object. One drive only (sel2-nostr).

### Run 264 (sal_emu254) — the reader's first check, decoded

Reader hooks v2 caught the container reader's single call
before the raise, and the dumped code window
(scratch/s47/vm-reader.bin) lets it be read exactly:
- Wrapper 0x10326c1b(ctx, base, len) calls reader
  0x10326c3b(ctx, &cursor, end = base + len).
- Reader prologue: remaining = end - cursor; if remaining < 2
  -> push (0, 0x14402020, ctx), call error ctor [0x651423d8]
  (ret 0x10326c6f — THE sel2/sel1/sel6 raise). Then: tag =
  *cursor; class = (tag >> 6) & 3; class 1/3 -> 0x14402030
  (call at 0x10326c9e, ret 0x10326ca4 — the sel2-cid raise);
  class 2 -> length reader 0x10326dfb + verbatim copy (raw
  blob, handle | 0x80000000); class 0 + (tag & 0x20) + type
  0x10/0x11 -> container recursion; class 0 otherwise ->
  scalar via the read-value worker.
- The captured call: ctx 0x12312ff8, end 0x103ed803 (stk[3]),
  caller chain ret 0x10326c35 -> 0x10322d39. The end pointer
  is INSIDE the VM image (0x103e0000+ region), NOT in my
  stream's heap data — the reader was parsing an in-image
  buffer whose remaining length is < 2 on the FIRST call, and
  the read-value worker never fired. Exception text (from the
  guest's own formatting): "SAL INTERNAL ERROR:
  ERR_TID_MTDD,SVR2,ERR_LYR_SAL,ERR_MOD_MT,ERR_MT_COM,
  ERR_MALFORMED_INPUT,(kind:0x14402020 ext:0x0(0)) in nc.ocm".
- Readback "poison" theory CORRECTED: the readback getters
  themselves throw OMGExceptions when the out stream lacks the
  expected value (cf. S39 oma1's "OPERAND expected" getter
  errors) — the untouched 0xee/0xdd buffers mean "no value",
  not state corruption. Multi-drive runs are usable; each
  drive is judged on its own raise lines.
OPEN: what in-image buffer (base? len?) the reader was handed,
and what a WORKING module's first reader call looks like.
Run 265 = positive control: hooks moved BEFORE the S38 block
(wrapper 0x10326c1b + reader + worker entries, args + pointed
bytes), one known-good atpcd drive (ATRAC_S38_MODE=bb) in the
same run as sel2-nostr, and dumps of the VM image region
[0x103e0000, 0x10400000) before/after the sel2 execute.

### Run 265 (sal_emu255) — THE EMPTY STRING IS THE WALL (verdict-grade)

Early reader hooks caught the feeder drive's only reader call:
wrapper(ctx=0x12312ff8, base=0x103ed803, len=0x0) -> reader
rem=0 -> MALFORMED. The method struct-parses an argument it
received as (ptr, len=0): the VM's representation of an EMPTY
STRING, pointing at a static image address. The empty
this+0x24 (my str6c b'') is the malformed input — in the
native pipeline this+0x24 is never empty at feeder time.
Positive controls from the same run's ceremony phases: every
healthy wrapper call parses a DOCUMENT in the canonical form
[30 80] ... [00 00] (indefinite root container) — e.g. #225:
base 0x123e7510 len 0xe8 = 232 B = an .opf document
(30 80 02 02 03 ea 04 14 ...). So this+0x24 must carry a
serialized SAL document. Prime candidate: the track's .opf
document itself (this+0x24 is fed by selector 1's reply and,
at pipeline init, very plausibly by OMGUtils'
convertToSalSinf — an omgconv2 import — from the track info).
Note: run 265's atpcd bb control did NOT run ("no OP-A
envelope available — run opa first"); not needed now.
Run 266: sel 2/1/6/5 with str = the raw Dreamer .opf bytes.

### Desk breakthrough — the pipeline has an INIT selector (0); this+0x24 is a living state document

FUN_61948a93 (vtable sibling) is the pipeline INITIALIZE:
guarded by this+0x38 == 0 (the state string's size field —
init only when empty). It fills an At3::Param at this+0x50
(16 dwords), sets this+0x189 = isLossLess, this+0x170 =
isAacRaw ? 16 : 8 (THE CHAIN LENGTH — 8 for normal
ATRAC3plus), this+0x94 = staging capacity, embeds the 96-B
.At3 Header template at this+0xa0 (so stream C's {this+0xf0,
8} readback writes header+0x50 = the MgD field), then runs
SELECTOR 0: [sessrec][int64 argA][int64 argB][int64 0]
[int64 17]. Readbacks in order: byte (0x8c); STRING via
0x80 INTO this+0x24 — the state document is CREATED by
selector 0; BLOB via 0x84 with desc {this+0x160, 0x10} —
the initial chain state; STRING via 0x80 into this+0x1a8;
int via 0x9c into this+0x18c; then CoCreateGuid(this+0x194).
FUN_61949679 is SELECTOR 3 (a state refresh): [sessrec]
[string this+0x24][int 3][int 17], readbacks byte/int/
string/string-into-this+0x24/int — the state doc is passed
back and forth and REWRITTEN by the methods. This explains
the feeder's parse: it struct-parses the state document,
which must come from selector 0 (or a later selector's
reply), never from a foreign file. My earlier drives skipped
init entirely — verdict (c) territory, now with the wall
named exactly: missing selector-0 initialization.
Run 268: drive sel 0 (arg pairs (0,0) then variants), capture
the state document + chain from its readbacks, then drive
sel 2 with the genuine state doc, chain = blob[0:8]
(this+0x170 = 8 for non-AAC), block = dreamer.raw[:1504].

### The At3 mode table (OMGUtils FUN_63901a30, desk-decoded)

At3::getParam(mode) builds a 0x40-B Param per mode index
0..34. Fields: +0x08 bitrate, +0x0c SU size, +0x14 channels,
+0x28 sampling (44100 default), +0x3c 0x10. Modes 0-3 =
ATRAC3 (flag 1) 33k/96, 47k/136, 66k/192, 94k/272; modes
10-15 = the ATRAC3plus family (flag computed): 33k/192,
48k/280, **mode 12 = 64000 bps / SU 376** — Dreamer's exact
configuration (64 kbps, 376-B frames) — 256k/1488, 352k/2048,
192k/1120; mode 16 = 1411200 (PCM lossless). In
FUN_61948a93, getParam's key is the arg at +0x0c (the mode),
appended SECOND in selector 0's stream; the arg at +0x10 is
appended FIRST and is host-side-unexplained (sampling rate
is the leading candidate — 44100 or an enum). The (0,0)
failure therefore likely came from arg+0x10 = 0, since mode
0 itself is legal. Run 269: sel0 with (44100,12), (0,12),
(1,12); verdict per drive = the guest error text (AT3_ERR_*
via the HLE wcslen log) plus any state-doc readback.

### The feeder's flush gate (decomp, exact)

FUN_61948d39 memcpy's input into the staging buffer
(this+0x44, cursor this+0x98, capacity this+0x94 =
0x40000) and queues the staged block + runs the
selector-2 loop ONLY IF (finalize mode != 0) OR the
staging buffer filled completely. My mode-0 feeds of
1504/6016 B therefore only accumulated (totals and
cursor moved; zero stream executes — the 4-line feed
segments). Also corrected: the chain blob in the
per-block stream is {this+0x160, 0x10} (16 B fixed), and
the first stream int is the block node's field [5].

forced flushes — then harvest.

### Walker + accessor DECODED from scratch/s47/vm-typecheck.bin

walker(ctx=arg1, frametop=arg2), native, in the SAL runtime
image (0x1020xxxx region):
  esi = arg2; esi -= 4; idx = [esi];
  edi = accessor(ctx, idx)                    ; call @b9c3, ret b9c8
  ecx = [esi-4]; esi -= 4; r2 = getval(ctx, ecx)  ; call 0x1020b940
  ebx = [esi-4]; esi -= 4;                      ; THIRD word
  if (ebx & 0xc0000000) != 0x80000000 -> throw ERR_TYPE
     0x14402010 via error ctor [0x651423d8]     ; @b9f2
  len = [ebx*4 + 4]                             ; blob length
  quot = len / [edi+4]; copyout(0x1020ba40)(ctx, ebx);
  [edi+8] += quot; r3 = 0x1020bbe0(); [esi] = r3; return esi+4.
So ONE walker call consumes a 3-word group (idx, value, blob
handle) walking DOWNWARD and materializes the class-2 blob.
The THROW we keep hitting is NOT here: it fires INSIDE
accessor(ctx, idx) when idx is the guest-stack address
0x0F3FA208 — i.e. the group's FIRST word is the garbage.
accessor(0x1020bbb0): idx = arg2 (post-push [esp+0xc] =
entry [esp+8]); throws ERR_TYPE iff idx < 0 or >= 0x20
(a <=32-entry descriptor table in ctx). NOTE: S47's accwatch
read idx from entry [esp+0xc]; the disasm says arg2 sits at
entry [esp+8]. S48 logs BOTH offsets to settle it.
copyout 0x1020ba40 dispatches on handle class: class1 ->
0x1020bab0, class2 -> 0x1020bae0 (blob copy via [0x651423a4]),
class3 -> 0x1020bb20 (recursive over a count-prefixed list).
The 0x1047d024 "caller" in raise stacks is a BYTECODE pc in
nc.ocm's loaded image (that region is bytecode data, verified
by disassembly of img-before47.bin) — the walker serves a
bytecode-level marshal opcode inside selector 2's method.

### Accessor + siblings DECODED (fresh dump, scratch/s48/vm-typecheck.bin)

accessor(ctx, idx): if idx<0 or >=0x20 -> throw ERR_TYPE;
else return 0x1020BFB0 + idx*16. THE TABLE IS STATIC (in
the runtime image), 32 entries x 16 B:
{+0 flag/first, +4 elem size, +8 cursor (walker-advanced),
+0xC ?}. It is a DYNAMIC TYPE-DESCRIPTOR registry:
0x1020b990 = find-first-free (scans for dword[0]==0,
returns index or -1). AT S48 BLOCK START (post-ceremony)
THE WHOLE TABLE IS ZEROS — no type has ever been
registered in the harness process. getval (0x1020b940)
resolves group word 2: class 0 -> word & 0xFFFF immediate;
class 1 -> arena scalar (byte at [w*4+1] = negate flag,
dword at [w*4+4]); class 2/3 -> ERR_TYPE. Sibling walkers:
0x1020bbf0 (same group shape; if descriptor[0]==0 it
CREATES a fresh 12-B-header value object via 0x1020bc60
and stores a new class-2 handle into the frame) and
0x1020bcb0 (third variant). So idx is a TYPE ID handed out
by registration; sel2's frame carries 0xFFFF = "no type"
where a registered type id belongs.
OPEN (run 285): who calls find-free / writes the table
(the registrar), what the walker's caller loop at
0x1032047f expects, whether the table gains entries at
any point in a run, and whether a stream-level sel2 hits
the identical frame slot.

- The walker "caller" 0x1032047f is the VM interpreter's
  DISPATCH: it stores its current-value register [ebp-0x88]
  at the operand-stack pointer, advances it, then calls
  handler_table[opcode] (call [ecx + op*4 + 4] at
  0x1032047b). So the failing idx operand [top-4] IS the
  interpreter's current value at that opcode — the result
  of the preceding bytecode computation: a lookup that
  returned -1 (0xFFFF). The walker/marshal opcode handlers
  sit in the dispatch table at 0x10330850 / 0x103318fc;
  the handler IMMEDIATELY BEFORE the walker trio is
  0x1020b840.
- 0x1020b840 = the DECLARE-TYPE opcode handler ("REG"):
  entry: push ecx; call find-free (id = first free slot;
  on table-full it writes 0xFFFFFFFF at [arg3-0x18] and
  returns). It consumes SIX frame operands via getval,
  writes table entry {flag=1, elemsize = base(kind) x dim,
  cursor=0, +0xC = operand 2}, and pushes the new id back
  onto the frame. Kind->base size jump table at
  0x1020b91c: kind 0->2, 1->4, 2->2, 3->4, 4->8, 5->8.
- Census of the immediate 0x1020BFB0 (type-table address)
  across the full 6 MB VM image dump (vmimg-*.bin): only
  FOUR code sites reference it — REG (0x1020b878),
  find-free (0x1020b993), the accessor (0x1020bbda), and a
  scatter-constants opcode handler at ~0x1020bd95 that
  uses 0x1020bfb0 only as the END bound of its own static
  table at 0x1020bf90. NO free/reset writer of type
  entries exists in the runtime image. Types are declared
  ONLY by method bytecode executing the DECLARE opcode.

serialized with len 0) or the method itself (an output/
local slot it then re-reads)? Instrument: MEM_WRITE watch
on 0x103F09B0..0x103F09C0 through the flush phase, logging
writer PC + ebp backchain per write.

### Run 289 (sal_emu277) — the empty value is the RESULT OF A
### SLICE whose computed length is 0; producer fully decoded

Write-watch on desc@0x103F09B0 through the flush: the slot
is a LIFO-recycled temporary. Three writers: (A) a salwrap
free/clear routine (0x6511ff20/48/4a) zeroing the descriptor
and linking it into a free list (+0 = next slot); (B) the
reader's value constructor (~0x103257fb..0x10325820, the
worker called from reader 0x10326d64) building healthy
temporaries in it (len 0xc0, then 4, then 4 across the
flush); (C) — immediately before the fatal wrap — the
value allocator 0x103029d0 building {byte0 0, byte1 1,
byte2 0, byte3 0x10; len 0; ptr = desc+3}.

DESK DECODE of (C) and its caller (vmimg-10300000.bin):
- 0x103029d0 = alloc_value(ctx, len): allocates a 12-B
  header via manager import [0x651423a0]; [desc+1] =
  (len <= 1) — THE INLINE FLAG (byte1 of the +0 tag word);
  if inline, data ptr = desc+3 (data lives in the
  descriptor tail); else a separate len-sized allocation.
  *** The fatal tag 0x10000100 is exactly "inline value,
  not yet committed": byte1 = inline flag. ***
- Its caller (~0x10302880-0x103029c5) is a SLICE/subvalue
  operation: operands are tagged handles (tag = handle>>30;
  0 = small int via movsx; 1 = descriptor whose len is read
  at [h*4+4], NEGATED when its inline flag is set). If
  start >= source_len it calls alloc_value(ctx, 0) at
  0x10302945 (ret 0x1030294a); else it clamps the count to
  the remaining length and allocs+copies at 0x1030295f
  (ret 0x10302964; rep movsd/movsb from src data + start).
  Result handle stored to [ebx] = 0x80000000 | (len>>2)
  form... (stored dword at 0x10302997).
- So: *** sel2's method SLICES one of its values and gets
  an EMPTY result, then the parse opcode (0x10322d0a) tries
  to parse that empty slice and raises 0x14402020. *** The
  wall is not a missing input element per se — it is a
  slice whose (source, start, count) resolve to nothing in
  our drive.
- Arena census (all 3 run-288 snapshots): NO value
  descriptor anywhere holds the staged data (no len 8192 /
  1504); the data exists only as the type-0 cursor count.
  The method's value world = doc values + small values +
  slices; the cipher data path runs through the type table.

Run 290 instruments alloc_value (flush phase): every
alloc's len + return site + the slice-body frame
(hook_esp+12: source handle at +0x10, its descriptor +
data head) — the len==0 alloc's frame names the source
value, the start, and the count operand.

Run 290 (sal_emu278, first attempt) INVALID: the new
alloc hook's callback had the wrong arity (3 params vs
Unicorn's 4 for UC_HOOK_CODE), raised TypeError inside the
first execute, and the exception poisoned the whole method
ladder (wrap total = 1). Fixed to (uc, addr, size, _u);
rerun as run 291. (Instrumentation note for future briefs:
hook callbacks that raise inside an execute propagate into
the guest SEH path and invalidate the run — always match
the (uc, addr, size, user) signature exactly.)

### Run 291 (sal_emu278) — the empty value's producer is a
### ZEROFILL opcode helper; an XOR cipher primitive is in
### the same opcode family

Alloc census in the flush (caller ret -> len): the method
copies the repository key (alloc len 16), builds values of
92/20/128, and makes TWO HEALTHY slices (ret 0x10302964)
of a 20-B value 0e246fdbd61c7f5fa8d7a16663043c08
4b680145: bytes [0..8] and [8..16]. The sequence runs
TWICE inside the single flush call (two execute passes).
The FATAL alloc is #22: len 0, ret 0x103031ff — NOT the
slice. Its caller (function at 0x103031e0) also produced
alloc#9 len 24 in the first pass (same frame shape, walker
operand frame 0x1047d024 in frame).

DESK DECODE of 0x103031e0 = ZEROFILL: reads an operand
handle off the VM value stack, computes its length via
the tag-decoding helper 0x10302b90, alloc_value(ctx,
that_length), ZERO-FILLS the new value (rep stosd/stosb),
stores the result handle back to the stack slot. Pass 1:
operand len 24 -> a 24-B zero buffer. Pass 2: operand
len 0 -> the empty value the parse opcode then chokes on.

DESK DECODE of the neighbouring helper 0x103030b0 =
*** XOROP: pops two tag-2 values, requires
len_long % len_short == 0 (else raises 0x14402020),
allocs len_long, and fills it with the BYTE-WISE XOR of
the two data buffers, the shorter repeated quotient
times (loop at 0x10303182: mov cl,[edx]; xor cl,[ebp];
mov [ebx-1],cl). *** A genuine cipher primitive inside
the VM's opcode set — the method's grammar includes an
explicit XOR-with-repeating-key operation. Whether sel2's
method reaches XOROP before dying is the next question.

Working model: pass structure per execute = key copy,
value builds, slices of the 20-B value, ZEROFILL sized
from an operand, doc parses, marshal, then pass 2 whose
ZEROFILL operand reads len 0. Prime suspect for the
len-0 operand: a handle to a value the (mis-typed)
marshal consumed/freed instead of transforming — the
free routine zeroes descriptor lens (run 289 SEQ A), so a
dangling handle reads length 0. Run 292 hooks ZEROFILL
(0x103031e0) and XOROP (0x103030b0) entries: operand
handles + descriptors + lens, in ckey (healthy contrast)
and the flush.

Run 292 (sal_emu279) PARTIAL: ZEROFILL fired 11 times by
flush end (2 in the flush: #10 pass 1, #11 pass 2, the
latter immediately before the fatal wrap), and XOROP
(0x103030b0) fired ZERO times in ckey/feed phases — the
method does not reach the XOR step before dying. But the
ZF hook read its stack-slot arg at [esp+4] (the ctx arg);
the true arg is [esp+8] (ZEROFILL reads [esp+0x14] after
3 pushes). Operand identity therefore still open; fixed
in sal_emu280 (also: handle-desc decode extended to tag-1
handles, desc = (h & 0x3fffffff) << 2; ZF/XOR/alloc
counters added to the summary). Rerun as run 293.

### Run 293 (sal_emu280) — THE EMPTY VALUE FULLY IDENTIFIED:
### ZEROFILL sizes from a small-int length slot in the
### marshal operand frame; pass 2's slot holds 0

ZF#10 [flush pass 1]: stack-slot pointer arg0 =
0x1047d024 — the marshal operand frame's +0x1c address
itself (the S48 idx-slot address; the VM operand stack
for this method IS the operand frame at 0x1047d008).
Operand = the dword at [arg0-4] = frame+0x18 = INT 24
-> ZEROFILL(24) (alloc#9), method proceeds: doc parses
(wrap#5/#6), marshal (patched) completes.
ZF#11 [flush pass 2]: same slot, operand = INT 0 ->
ZEROFILL(0) -> alloc#22's empty inline value -> the
parse opcode (0x10322d0a) reads it -> reader raises
0x14402020. *** The empty value is a zero-length scratch
buffer the method allocates with a length taken from an
operand-stack int; in pass 2 that int is 0. ***
Summary counters: ZEROFILL total 12, XOROP total 0,
allocs 146, wraps 9. XOROP never fires anywhere in the
drive — the cipher step remains downstream.
Ckey contrast: ZF operands there are healthy small ints
(8, 4, 24, 24, 56, 24) read from the same frame range —
the frame is the session-shared VM operand stack.

What writes frame+0x18 = 24 in pass 1 but 0 in pass 2 is
the one remaining unknown in the chain (execute setup
from the stream vs method-computed length vs a reply
length the native feeder threads between the two passes
of the flush call). Run 294 patches the operand slot to
24 at ZF entry when it reads 0 in the flush — a supply
experiment: if pass 2 then proceeds (second marshal,
XOROP, reply, feeder out-params), the wall is exactly
this length; wherever it stops next names the next
missing piece.

### Run 294 (sal_emu281) — supply experiment: patching the
### length to 24 moves the wall exactly one parse further

ZF-PATCH fired (operand slot 0x1047d020: 0 -> 24 at
ZF#11). Result: wrap#7 parsed the 24 zero bytes WITHOUT
raising (desc @0x103F09B0 relocated data 0x103f3fe0,
len 24, all zero) — the reader accepts an all-zero
24-B document of empty elements. The method continued
and immediately hit wrap#8: ANOTHER empty parse, desc
@0x103F2520 = {10000100, 0, desc+3}, cv 0x840fc948 —
a child element of the just-parsed zero document (the
neighbourhood @0x103F2500 holds a handle vector
840fc920/28/30 = the parsed doc's children). Same raise
0x14402020; flush still aborts, out-params zero.
*** Supplying the LENGTH alone is not sufficient: the
method expects the pass-2 buffer to arrive FILLED with
a real serialized document. In the real session that
content is pass 1's marshal output / reply value —
which our drive never produces, because the marshal
runs under the patched type 0 instead of sel2's own
(undeclared) type. The two S49 walls are one root
cause: the missing DECLARE-TYPE for sel2's data type
(S48), cascading through the marshal into every
downstream length and content in pass 2. ***

### SESSION 49 VERDICT — (b), with the supply experiment
### as the demonstration

1. WHO requests the empty parse: sel2's METHOD BYTECODE
   inside the VM. All 9 wrap calls in the canonical drive
   return to the single parse-opcode arm 0x10322d39
   (ret verified at runtime, run 288); no manager
   reply-readback path is involved.
2. THE EMPTY VALUE: a zero-length scratch buffer minted
   by the ZEROFILL opcode helper (0x103031e0) via
   alloc_value (0x103029d0). ZEROFILL sizes the buffer
   from a small-int length held in the VM operand stack
   — for the flush, the dword at 0x1047d020 (marshal
   operand frame 0x1047d008 + 0x18). Pass 1 of the flush:
   24 -> healthy 24-B buffer, doc parses, marshal. Pass
   2: 0 -> empty inline value {tag 0x10000100 (inline
   flag set, uncommitted), len 0, data ptr = desc+3} at
   desc 0x103F09B0, and the parse opcode raises
   ERR_MALFORMED_INPUT 0x14402020 in reader 0x10326c3b.
3. SUPPLY TEST: forcing the length to 24 (run 294) lets
   the parse succeed on zeros and moves the wall one
   step: the method next parses a child element of that
   (empty) document and raises identically at desc
   0x103F2520. The missing thing is therefore CONTENT —
   the serialized reply/output document of the pass-1
   marshal — not a length our drive can fake.
4. Root cause (unchanged from S48, now proven to be
   the whole cascade): sel2's data type is never
   DECLAREd in our drive order; the type-0 patch lets
   the marshal consume the blob into the wrong table
   and produce no reply value, so pass 2 inherits
   zero lengths and zero contents.
5. The opcode set contains an explicit XOR primitive
   (XOROP, 0x103030b0: byte-wise XOR, shorter operand
   repeated, len ratio must be integral) and healthy
   slices/ZEROFILLs in ckey — but XOROP fires ZERO times
   in the whole drive (summary counters, run 293/294).
   The cipher step is downstream of the type
   declaration. Whether nc.ocm itself ciphers remains
   OPEN; no output bytes exist yet.
6. Task 4 (desk): within every method driven so far
   (sel0/1/2/5/6, real feeder, ckey) the only DECLARE is
   ckey's (type 0, elemsize 4). Un-driven candidates
   named for S50: FUN_61949679 (sel3 finalizer; stream
   [sess][doc][int 3][int 17] — driveable at stream
   level) and FUN_61939aef (KDRM integration; executes
   kdrm.ocm + nc.ocm with trailing int 0x1e and carries
   extra session docs/ints). NEXT STEP: drive sel3 (and
   the broker variants) BEFORE the feed in the canonical
   ladder, REG-hook both images' DECLARE handlers, and
   watch for a declaration of the type sel2's marshal
   looks up; alternatively gatewatch writes to operand
   frame+0x18 during a REAL session-shaped drive to find
   who supplies 24 vs 0. No new Sony dev-trace strings

## Session 50 — 2026-11-02 (find the DECLARE for sel2's type; flush UNPATCHED)

GOAL (brief): the S48+S49 root cause is one missing event: sel2's
data type is never DECLAREd in our drive order, so its marshal
looks up a type id, gets -1 (0xFFFF), and everything downstream
(pass-2 reply document, XOROP cipher step) starves. Mission:
(1) learn the lookup key — what sel2's marshal actually looks
up, so the needed declaration can be recognized; (2) drive the
un-driven methods BEFORE the feed (sel3 = FUN_61949679, then
FUN_61939aef's kdrm/nc executes, then broker variants) with REG
hooks on the DECLARE handler, watching for a declaration of
sel2's type; (3) if it fires, run the flush with NO idx patch
and watch the marshal, pass 2, XOROP count, and feeder
out-params; byte-compare any output vs Dreamer's .ATP
payload; (4) fallback: gatewatch writes to the length slot
0x1047d020 and desk-assess the synthetic-declaration route.

### Desk census (pre-drive): there is exactly ONE DECLARE
### handler and ONE type table in the whole VM

- The brief's "REG hooks on BOTH VM images" resolves
  empirically. From the S48 vmimg dumps (vmimg-10200000.bin,
  vmimg-10300000.bin): bytes at 0x1030b840 (the 0x103 image at
  the DECLARE RVA) are unrelated code — NOT a second DECLARE
  handler. The type-table address 0x1020BFB0 is referenced
  from exactly four code sites, ALL in the 0x102 image: REG
  0x1020b878, find-free 0x1020b993, the accessor 0x1020bbda,
  scatter-constants ~0x1020bda6 (end-bound only) — S48's
  census re-confirmed. There are NO references to a
  hypothetical second table at 0x1030BFB0 in either image.
- The two dispatch tables (0x1033084c and 0x103318f8, in the
  manager image) BOTH open with the same handler window:
  [0x1020b840 DECLARE, 0x1020b9b0 walker/marshal, 0x1020bbf0,
  0x1020bcb0, 0x10325304, ...]. So one handler hook at
  0x1020b840 + a write-watch over the table catches every
  declaration regardless of which table dispatches it.
- Dispatch mechanics for the task-1 trace (capstone decode
  of the S48 walker-caller dump, vmimg-10300000.bin): the
  handler-call arm at 0x10320454 stores cv [ebp-0x88] at the
  operand-stack pointer [ebp+0x10], advances it, pushes
  (ctx = [ebp+8], top), loads opcode = [ebp-0x29] & 0xff and
  table = [ebp-0x24], and calls [table + op*4 + 4] at
  0x1032047b (ret 0x1032047f — the genuine-hit return). The
  handler returns the new stack pointer in eax; cv is then
  reloaded from [newtop - 4]. So a code hook at 0x1032047b
  sees, per builtin invocation: opcode (eax), table (ecx),
  handler target dword, cv BEFORE ([ebp-0x88]), top
  ([esp+4]). A second arm at 0x10320434-0x10320450 tail-jmps
  (jmp eax at 0x10320450) to a handler loaded the same way —
  hooked too, so no builtin invocation escapes the trace.
- Candidate streams desk-derived from out/decomp-s46-
  omgconv2.c: sel3 FUN_61949679 (size 1027) builds

  pipe object (its [ebp+8]) + two non-null out-ptrs

  and "nc.ocm" with [blob vt+0x70 = CW2A(arg string) bytes]

- S50 harness = sal_emu282 (copy of sal_emu281) with a new

### Task 1 — the marshal's operand semantics (trace + handler decodes)

Flush dispatch trace (ladder A; ring of the last 1,103 dispatches of the
flush execute; marshal at [1102]):
[1100] op 0x68 (0x103249ea) cv 0x1 → 0x5103; [1101] op 0x25 (0x10320dd1)
cv 0x5103 → 0xffff; [1102] op 0xee = MARSHAL (0x1020b9b0), cv = 0xffff,
top = 0x1047d028. Frame-write watch: the ONLY 0xFFFF write to the idx
slot 0x1047d024 in the whole flush came from **pc 0x1032045d — the
dispatcher's own current-value store** (0x10320454 block: [operand_ptr]
← [ebp−0x88], ptr += 4, then call handler with the advanced ptr).

Handler decodes (manager image): op 0x25 (0x10320dd1) is a **SWAP**:
cv ↔ [operand_ptr−4]. Op 0x68 (0x103249ea) is a loop/array-index arm
(decrementing counters at [ebp−0x4c]/[ebp−0x48], wrap 0x3e) that
computed 0x5103 from cv 0x1. Op 0x1a (0x10322be5) is a binary value-op
arm (helper 0x1032628b) that in this window turned cv 0x1 into 0xffff —
ordinary value arithmetic can produce −1.

**Conclusion: there is NO lookup at the marshal.** The walker's idx is
the interpreter's current value [ebp−0x88] at the marshal dispatch,
stored by the dispatcher itself into [top−4]. A type id is a raw table
index (accessor 0x1020bbb0: bounds 0..0x1F, returns table+idx·16; the
flag is not consulted). No find-by-name / find-by-spec helper exists
anywhere (S48 census: the table is referenced from exactly 4 code
sites). The only producer of ids is the DECLARE opcode's result. In
sel2's method the id reaches the marshal through ordinary stack traffic
(a SWAP surfacing a slot value); in our drives that value is −1, i.e.
the bytecode's dataflow never carries a declared id — consistent with
S49: the declaration (and with it the id, most plausibly carried in an
earlier execute's reply/state) never happens in our drive order. The
0xFFFF writes to the length slot 0x1047d020 came from ordinary
store-local arms at 0x10323ce8 / 0x10320ae8 / 0x10322525 writing
computed values — pass-2's slot is a recycled local, not a length
anyone "forgot" to store; its healthy pass-1 content (24) is likewise
computed in-method.

### DECLARE grammar (handler 0x1020b840, fully decoded this session)

Six getval-resolved operands consumed top-down from the operand stack;
find-free (0x1020b990) returns the first flag==0 slot (or the handler
writes 0xFFFFFFFF as the result at [top−0x18] when the table is full).
Entry written: +0 flag = 1; +4 elemsize = base(kind) × dim, kind→base
via jump table 0x1020b91c (0→2, 1→4, 2→2, 3→4, 4→8, 5→8, else 2);
+8 cursor = 0; +0xc fieldC = operand #2. ckey's instance: operands
[0x?03 handle, 0, 0, 1, 2, …] → (kind 0 → base 2) × (dim 2) = elemsize 4,
fieldC 0. The new id is delivered back to the bytecode as the opcode's
result value.

**Synthetic-declaration assessment (task 4, desk):** a stream cannot
mint a type — streams only invoke existing methods by selector; DECLARE
is an opcode inside method bytecode, not a stream element. No staged
.ocm method driven so far declares anything but ckey's (kind 0, dim 2,
fieldC 0). A fabricated table entry via harness write would still need
sel2's bytecode to carry THAT id into cv (it carries −1 by dataflow we
do not control) — so fabrication reduces to the S48 idx-slot patch,
which S49 showed completes the marshal but produces no pass-2 reply,
because the marshal's serialization under a borrowed/fabricated type is
not the method's expected grammar. The declaration must come from an
existing, undriven method.

### SESSION 50 VERDICT — (c): no driven candidate declares sel2's type

Across runs 295–296, with the DECLARE handler (0x1020b840) hooked on
the interpreter and a write-watch on the type table, the ONLY DECLARE
executions in any drive remain ckey's (one per ladder; (kind 0, dim 2,
fieldC 0) → entry (1,4,0,0)). XOROP total across both runs: 0. No
output bytes; the flush still dies at the marshal (idx 0xFFFF) in every
ladder. nc.ocm ciphers or not: still unsettled — no output exists.

**Lookup semantics (task 1) — learned, and it is not a lookup:** the
marshal's type operand is the interpreter's current value [ebp−0x88]
at the marshal dispatch, stored by the dispatcher itself (0x1032045d)
into [top−4]; the accessor (0x1020bbb0) treats it as a raw index into
the 32-slot table at 0x1020BFB0. No find-by-name/spec helper exists in
either VM image (4 referencing sites total). Ids are produced ONLY as
DECLARE results and must reach the marshal as ordinary dataflow; in
sel2's method the value arrives via stack traffic (final step: op 0x25
SWAP at 0x10320dd1 surfacing a slot holding −1), and in our drive order
no declared id ever enters that dataflow.

**Eliminated with evidence:**
- sel3 / FUN_61949679 (the post-feed finalizer, driven pre-feed):
  stream-level execute CLEAN (0 raises) — declares nothing. (Native
  call in ladder B faulted in the harness — inconclusive, superseded
  by the clean stream drive of the same bytecode.)
- FUN_61939aef's kdrm.ocm execute ([blob][int 0x2b]): died on an
  ERR_TYPE_VECTOR judgment of the stand-in blob; no DECLARE before the
  death. Partially explored.
- FUN_61939aef's nc.ocm execute (selector 0x1e, corrected 9-element
  stream): ran deep, died on a guest C++ throw over stand-in doc
  contents; no DECLARE in the executed portion. Partially explored.
- Broker variants (IOmgPcmToAtpBroker v2–v5): eliminated at desk
  level — brokers are COM forwarding classes (their vtable slots are
  marshaling stubs, S47 census); they run no VM bytecode locally and
  drive the same nc.ocm selector family (0/1/2/3/5/6) through the same
  server-side object. No new selector, module, or stream family exists
  in any broker vtable.
- Synthetic declaration via a hand-built stream: impossible as a
  stream (DECLARE is method bytecode, not a stream element); a
  harness-fabricated table entry still leaves sel2's dataflow carrying
  −1, reducing to the S48 patch, which S49 proved yields no pass-2
  reply. Dead end, desk-closed.

**Named next step (S51):** an exhaustive stream-level selector census
of nc.ocm itself — drive EVERY selector the module exposes (the S46
census counted its methods; only 0/1/2/3/5/6 have ever been driven)
with generic stream shapes and the REG hook armed. The declaration, if
it lives in nc.ocm, is in an undriven selector — most plausibly a
session-setup/first-use method the real host runs when a burn session
opens, before the pipeline object is ever fed. If nc.ocm's full census
declares nothing, the candidate set moves to the other staged .ocm
modules' setup methods (the real host's session-open path), and to
rebuilding the kdrmnc drive with faithful KDR:PCCK + procfile docs so
FUN_61939aef's tail executes.

No new Sony dev-trace strings surfaced in S50 (runs 295–296 logs and
the handler decodes contain none).

## Session 51 — 2026-11-02 (exhaustive nc.ocm selector census + sel2 dataflow trace)

GOAL (brief): the S48-S50 root cause is one missing event: sel2's
data type is never DECLAREd in our drive order, and the id must
reach sel2's marshal as ordinary dataflow. If the declaration
lives in nc.ocm, it is in a selector nobody has driven. Mission:
(1) enumerate nc.ocm's FULL selector list; (2) drive every
undriven selector at stream level after a canonical init+ckey
ladder, REG hook + type-table write-watch + XOROP counter armed;
(3) trace BACKWARD from the SWAP at 0x10320dd1 to find which of
sel2's parsed INPUT elements feeds the -1 slot; (4) if a new
DECLARE fires (or task 3 names a fixable stream element),
assemble the ladder and run the flush UNPATCHED; (5) only if
the census completes clean: start the faithful kdrmnc rebuild.

### Task 1 desk enumeration (pre-drive): what "selector" is,
### and what is actually known of nc.ocm's set

- Stream grammar pinned from the NATIVE builds in
  out/decomp-s46-omgconv2.c (append order via FUN_6190139c
  sessrec / FUN_61901334 append-int (vt+0x74) / FUN_61901351
  append-blob (vt+0x70) / FUN_61901c76 append-string (vt+0x6c)
  / FUN_61901383 append-module (vt+0x64), then execute vt[0]):
  [args in method-specific order][int SELECTOR][int 0x11]
  [module]. The selector is the int immediately before the
  constant 0x11 trailer (same grammar as atpcd S10 readout:
  write-int(selector) then write-module).
- Feeder FUN_61948d39's per-block stream, element by element
  (decomp lines 50966-50979): [int chunk-desc[5]] [int
  this+0x17c] [sessrec] [blob chain {this+0x160, 0x10}]
  [int mode (2 iff last chunk of a mode-2 call, else 0)]
  [blob data {chunk ptr, chunk len}] [string state doc
  this+0x24] [int 2] [int 0x11] [module nc.ocm]. S50's
  hand-built stream-sel2 already matches this order exactly.
- Init FUN_61948a93: [sessrec][int arg1][int arg2][int 0]
  [int 0x11] -> selector 0. Sel3 FUN_61949679: [sessrec]
  [string this+0x24][int 3][int 0x11] -> selector 3.
  FUN_61939aef's nc.ocm execute ends [int 0][int 0x1e] ->
  selector 0x1e = 30 (already driven in S50, partially).
- So the DRIVEN set is {0,1,2,3,5,6,30} — 30 was driven by
  S50's kdrmnc stream and is easy to overlook in the brief's
  "0/1/2/3/5/6" phrasing; recorded here so the census treats
  it as driven (partially: died on stand-in docs).
- S46's census counted METHODS FROM THE DRIVER SIDE ONLY
  (omgconv2's stream builders). The raw .ocm yields NO
  method table (program is plaintext only after the VM
  loader de-obfuscates it; H = 7.997). The module's true
  selector set is therefore NOT desk-enumerable from the
  file: enumeration must be runtime — (a) dump the formed
  image after load and parse its header/symbol table
  (format per readout #10: name at +0x3E (8 B, 'bc'-marked),
  type +0x46, cflag +0x47, entry u32 +0x4C, sym_off u32
  +0x50, sym_cnt u16 +0x56, reloc +0x58/+0x5C; symbol
  entries 8 B {name_off rel. to table base, value}, from
  out/decomp-loader.c FUN_65124ad0/FUN_651249f0), and
  (b) probe selectors at stream level with calibrated
  signatures (known-method vs absurd-selector controls).
- Second host: census_a.txt names omgdec.ax as ALSO driving
  nc.ocm — its selector set was never censused; probing
  covers the plausible range regardless of host.
- Probe range decision: 4, 7-29, 31-33, 36, 40 at stream
  level + absurd control 0x55. Selector values are host-
  chosen ints; kdrm.ocm takes 0x2b from its driver, so the
  space is not bounded by 6 — but every nc.ocm selector
  evidenced anywhere is <= 30, and 0..40 + controls is the
  proportionate census. If the formed image's symbol table
  names more, the range extends to cover them.

[int 0][doc][sel][17]; E = feeder-shaped with sel swapped

### Run 300 (tr v2, sal_emu287) — TASK 3 ANSWERED: the -1 is
### computed inside sel2; no stream element carries it

Full-trace + frame-write-watch on BOTH the hand-built
stream-sel2 and the genuine feeder flush (LT ladder).
The two executes are structurally IDENTICAL: 1103 rows,
857 frame writes, marshal at row 1102, same write PCs,
same values modulo run-varying handles. Our stream is a
faithful reproduction of the native one.
The chain, from the traces (identical in both):
- The marshal's index slot is the frame local at
  0x1047D020 (marshal frame 0x1047D008 + 0x18 — the same
  dword S49 found feeding pass 2's ZEROFILL size).
- Its biography in the execute: 0x18 (the doc length 24,
  written at row 1062 by 0x10302f6c), 0x1f, 0x3, the doc
  handle (0x840fc2xx, rows 1071-1079), the data/blob
  handle (0xc40fe8xx, row 1093's operand), then 0xffff.
- Row 1093: op 0x1a (binary value-op arm, handler
  0x10322be5, helper 0x1032628b) with cv_in = 1 and the
  top stack slot = the handle of the SECOND parsed
  value (the 16-B value parsed at row 1087's wrap from
  the value arena at 0x103F25C0/D0 — the chain blob's
  parsed form) produces cv_out = 0xffff = -1.
- Rows 1094+: store-local arms (0x10323ce8 at 1095,
  0x10320ae8 at 1098, 0x10322525 at 1100) write the -1
  into 0x1047D020; row 1100's op 0x68 computes the
  0x??03 marshal 'val' operand; row 1101 SWAP (handler
  0x10320dd1; its own write at 0x10320df2 displaces
  0x??03 into the slot) surfaces the -1 into cv; row
  1102 MARSHAL consumes it as the raw type index.
=> The brief's hypothesis (a stream int element supplied
as -1/0) is REFUTED: every int in both streams is
accounted for, and the -1 is manufactured by sel2's own
bytecode from (immediate 1, parsed-chain-value handle).
Semantics that fit a (value, 1) -> -1 result: a LOOKUP
(find the type id registered for this value/kind) that
misses in our session. In the real host the registry it
consults may be populated by state our drive order never
creates. Two wrap parses precede it (row 1086: the 24-B
state doc; row 1087: the 16-B chain value) — the lookup
operand is the CHAIN value, i.e. the value whose type
the marshal will consume: sel2 asks "which registered
type describes this chain/blob?" and gets NOT FOUND.
Run 301 follows the two threads this opens: (i) init
selector 0's arg1 has only ever been driven as 1 —
FUN_61948a93 validates arg1 in 0..2 (S47: (0,.) threw,
but that probe paired it with junk); arg1=2 is untested
at stream AND native level — a different session kind
may register/declare what (1,12) does not; (ii) a sel2
input-variant matrix (chain/doc/data/ints) watching the
marshal idx, to confirm no input flips the -1.

### Run 301 (t4, sal_emu288) — init discriminant + variant
### matrix: the -1 is invariant to every feeder-side input

- Stream sel0 arg1 probes: (0,12) and (1,12) run the full
  init (disp 1143, doc+chain reply); (2,12) and (3,12)
  take a shorter quiet path (disp 1048, no reply, no
  raise). No DECLARE from any.
- Native ladder W2 (init arg1=2): init succeeds with its
  own doc (265f37ec...), ckey declares entry 0 as usual,
  feed clean, FLUSH DIES AT THE SAME WALL: walker group
  idx = 0xffff, XOROP 0, out-params untouched. The
  session discriminant is not the missing state.
- sel2 variant matrix (all: disp 1103, walker idx
  0xffff, raise ERR_TYPE at the accessor 0x1020bbd0):
  control / chain = 16 zero bytes / data = 1504 zero
  bytes / chunk int 376 + 376 data / mode int 2. The
  op-0x1a lookup result does not depend on the chain
  content, the audio data, the chunk descriptor int, or
  the mode int. Combined with run 300's chain, the miss
  is a function of session state OUTSIDE the feeder
  stream entirely — the registry the lookup consults is

### Task 1 desk (Ghidra 12.1.4, raw-binary imports of the S48
### vmimg dumps at their arena bases; output
### scratch/s52/decomp-s52-103.c + decomp-s52-102.c)

VALUE MODEL (both images agree; FUN_10325a4b == FUN_1020b940,
the value->int conversion, exists as a copy in each image):
a value is a tagged dword. tag = value >> 30.
  tag 0: immediate int, payload = value & 0xFFFF (16-bit).
  tag 1: boxed int; desc = value<<2 (tag bits shift out);
         payload = [desc+4], sign byte at [desc+1] (negate).
  tag 2: aggregate "document" class (doc handle 0x84...).
  tag 3: aggregate "vector" class (chain handle 0xc4...).
  desc+0 = refcount byte (FUN_1032595b increfs it, saturating
  at 0xFF, for any handle with top bits set); FUN_103259db
  decrefs per class, freeing via FUN_1032586b/89b/8db at 0.

HELPER 0x1032628b = checked VECTOR-ELEMENT GET, full stop:
  i = value->int(ctx, idxval);
  if ((vec & 0xC0000000) != 0xC0000000) raise ERR_TYPE
     0x14402010;
  count = u16 at [vec*4 + 2];
  if (i < 0 || i >= count) raise ERR_TYPE_VECTOR 0x14402014
     (raise call at 0x103262e5, ret 0x103262eb — the census's
     "reader" site; method dispatch is this same GET on a
     module's method vector, hence identical rejections);
  elem = [vec*4 + 4 + i*4]; incref elem; decref vec, idxval;
  return elem.
Its sibling 0x1032631b is the matching SET (op 0x1b arm
0x10322c12): stores a new element, returns the old.
The op-0x1a arm (0x10322be5, listing in decomp-s52-103.c) is
exactly: cv = GET(ctx, pop(), cv); jmp loop-head. The
neighbour arm at 0x10322c66 makes a NEW tag-3 value:
cv = (FUN_1032582b(ctx, sign-extended-16 cv) >> 2) |
0xC0000000 — a vector allocator entry point.

*** There is NO registry in op 0x1a. *** It is a plain
bounds-checked vector index. At row 1093 it computes
GET(chain_value, 1) and returns the chain value's element
[1] verbatim = 0x0000FFFF (the immediate-int -1/65535 bit
pattern). sel2's bytecode then uses that element AS the
marshal's type index. So the question the brief calls "the
registry" decomposes into: (a) what value SHOULD sit at
chain[1] — a DECLARE-minted type id; (b) who puts it there.

MARSHAL SIDE (0x102 image, decomp-s52-102.c):
- FUN_1020b990: first-free-slot scan of the type table
  ([entry+0]==0 = unused; 32 entries to 0x1020C1B0).
- DECLARE (FUN_1020b840): pops 6 operands, converts each;
  entry = {+0: 1 (in-use), +4: base(kind) * count,
  +8: 0 (cursor), +0xC: fieldC}; base(kind): kind 1,3 -> 4;
  kind 4,5 -> 8; else 2. Returns the slot index as a value
  (or 0xFFFFFFFF if the table is full). ckey's (kind 0,
  dim 2, fieldC 0) -> entry 0 = (1, 4, 0, 0) as recorded.
- Accessor (FUN_1020bbb0): idx<0 or >0x1F raises ERR_TYPE
  0x14402010 (raise ret 0x1020bbd0 = S48's gatewatch site);
  else returns 0x1020BFB0 + idx*16. The index is the RAW
  stack dword — NOT passed through value->int.
- Walker (FUN_1020b9b0): entry = accessor(int at [top-4]);
  the blob operand [top-0xC] must be tag 2; bloblen =
  [blobdesc+4]; entry cursor [entry+8] += bloblen /
  [entry+4]; then FUN_1020ba40 SERIALIZES the blob value
  out through the callback at [0x651423a4] (tag 1:
  elements recursively + free; tag 2: payload + 12-B
  header; tag 3: count*4+4 bytes). Result value = 0.
  So the "marshal" is a typed consume/output of the blob:
  the declared type supplies the element size by which
  the blob's length is counted.
- The 4th type-table "reference" from S50's census
  (0x1020bda6) is a FALSE POSITIVE: the dword 0x1020BFB0
  appears there only as bytes inside a MOV AL,0xbf in an
  unrelated pair-loop function. Real users of the table:
  exactly find-free, DECLARE, accessor.

### Task 1 continued — the reader: what the parsed chain
### value IS (decomp-s52-103-reader.c)

Descriptor layouts (allocators via callback [0x651423a0]):
FUN_1032582b(ctx, n) = tag-3 vector descriptor: 4 + 4n B,
{rc byte @+0, flag byte @+1, count u16 @+2, elements @+4},
zero-filled. FUN_1032579b = same shape for tag-1 boxed
values. Tag-2 byte-string descriptor (FUN_103257db alloc;
FUN_1032589b free): 12 B {rc, sign, u16, len @+4, ptr @+8}.

BER reader FUN_10326c3b / FUN_10326ebb (the parse opcode's
engine):
- universal constructed tag 0x30/0x31: count elements
  (FUN_1032764b), allocate a vector (FUN_1032582b), parse
  each element recursively -> tag-3 handle.
- INTEGER: immediate if it fits (FUN_1032708b/FUN_103270db;
  -1 encodes as the immediate 0x0000FFFF), else boxed
  (FUN_10325a9b) -> tag-1 handle.
- OCTET STRING (default primitive): byte-string -> tag-2.
- class-2 (context) tag: raw bytes -> tag-2 byte-string.
So the chain element's serialized form — a SEQUENCE whose
element [1] is INTEGER -1 — parses to a tag-3 vector
[2, 0x0000FFFF, "", ""], and GET(chain, 1) returns the
0x0000FFFF verbatim. The -1 is IN the serialized form the
manager placed in the arena, not computed from session
state. Two questions remain, both empirical: (i) confirm
the serialized bytes in a live drive (run 304 wrap dumps);
(ii) WHO writes that serialized form (run 304 arena
write-watch over 0x103F0000-0x10410000 during feed/flush),
and what would make its field [1] a type id instead of -1.

### Runs 304-305 (probe + narrow watch, sal_emu291/292) —
### the record's biography and the serializer

Run 305 (identical ladder; doc/chain reproduce byte-exact:
a6f8204c... / 56abba3f...) put every event on one sequence
clock. The flush execute's micro-sequence (seq numbers):
 863-864  method dispatch GETs (module vec [0x11], method
          vec [2] = sel2)
 865-870  0x1030320f (value machinery, ZEROFILL region)
          zeroes a fresh 24-B arena slot at 0x103FA0C0
 871-890  0x103065ac writes 20 B of digest-like data at
          0x103FA0E0 (separate buffer; not the record)
 891-896  *** 0x1020ada8 / 0x1020adab (0x102 image) writes
          the ENVELOPE (04 10 + record + zero pad) into
          0x103FA0C0 ***
 906      wrap A: parse opcode reads the envelope ->
          byte-string value (reader copy loop 0x1032703a
          copies the 16 content bytes to 0x103F25D0)
 919      wrap B: parse opcode reads the record ->
          5-field vector {1, -1, "", "", ""}
 926-931  0x10328289 builds a cell {cksum, record handle
          0xC40FE840, data ptr 0x1294A0E0, ..., len 0x2000}
 932      GET(record, 1) -> -1 (run 304: result 65535)
So the record is NOT transcribed from the stream at
execute setup: it is serialized ON DEMAND, inside the
execute, by 0x102-image code, from a live value the
manager holds, the first time the method's parse opcode
touches that element. The -1 is a field of that live
value. Ghidra's take on FUN_1020ada8 is register-mangled
(it sits among DES-round helpers 0x1020b3b0/0x1020b760/
0x1020b7a0 — the 0x102 image's seal/open machinery); the
two store PCs are its 8-B-chunk output loop.
Blob-desc stream elements carry ONLY {ptr, len}
(_s38_desc = 8-B descriptor; the native builder
FUN_61901351 takes the same pair) — no type field exists
in the stream grammar. So field [1] is filled from
manager/session state, or is a fixed default of the
materialized value.
WHICH element materializes as the record is still open
(candidates: the sessrec — a manager-built live value;
the doc; the chain blob). Run 306 (stream level) ablates
one element at a time and watches the record parse.

before ckey FUN_61947fce). Decompiled body (out/
decomp-s46-omgconv2.c): guarded on *(this+0x38) != 0; it
builds a stream on module "nc.ocm":
  FUN_61901c76(stream, this+0x24)   ; append the DOC
  FUN_6190139c(...)                 ; append sessrec
  FUN_61901334(stream, 4, 0)        ; int64 4
  FUN_61901334(stream, 0x11, 0)     ; int64 0x11 trailer
  FUN_61901383 -> execute           ; = SELECTOR 4
then destroys both streams without reading a new doc
back into this+0x24 — consistent with sel4 MUTATING the
manager-side doc value in place (the 24-B token in
this+0x24 is a handle to it). It is NOT called by
init/ckey/feeder/refresh (no sel4 execute ever appeared
in any ladder) — its caller sits in a layer the harness
bypasses. Xref hunt + ordering next; then run 308:
drive sel4 (stream [doc, sessrec, 4, 0x11]) between
ckey and the feeder flush and watch GET(doc,1).

## SESSION 52 VERDICT (option (b)/(c) hybrid)

TASK 1 — THE "REGISTRY" DOES NOT EXIST. Op 0x1a (arm
0x10322be5) calls helper 0x1032628b exactly once
(0x10322bff), and the helper is a bounds-checked
VECTOR-ELEMENT GET: the operand must be a tag-3 value
(top bits 0xC0000000) else ERR_TYPE 0x14402010; count =
u16 at descriptor+2; index <0 or >=count raises
ERR_TYPE_VECTOR 0x14402014 (raise call 0x103262e5, return
site 0x103262eb — the S51 census's rejection site, because
module method dispatch is this same GET on the module's
method vector). A hit returns the element verbatim (with
refcount incref/decref via FUN_1032595b/FUN_103259db). No
key, no class, no registry, no writer. S51's "registry"
was a hypothesis; the brief's premise is corrected here.
Value model (both VM images agree): value = tagged
dword, tag = value>>30; tag0 immediate int (payload =
value & 0xFFFF, -1 == 0x0000FFFF), tag1 boxed int, tag2
byte-string/document (desc {rc, sign, u16, len@+4,
ptr@+8}), tag3 vector (desc {rc, flag, count u16 @+2,
elements @+4}). The marshal's type table has exactly
three native users: find-free FUN_1020b990, DECLARE
FUN_1020b840 (a VM opcode; pops 6 operands; entry =
{1, base(kind)*count, cursor 0, fieldC}; kind 1,3 -> 4;
4,5 -> 8; else 2), accessor FUN_1020bbb0 (raw index, no
value->int conversion; the S48 "4th reference" at
0x1020bda6 is a false positive). The walker FUN_1020b9b0
consumes the blob via the declared type's element size
and SERIALIZES it out (callback [0x651423a4]) — a typed
consume/output, not an in-place transform.

WHAT THE -1 ACTUALLY IS (runs 304-305, byte-exact): the
feeder method parses the STATE DOC element into a
5-field record {INTEGER 1, INTEGER -1, OCTETSTRING "",
OCTETSTRING "", OCTETSTRING ""} (serialized
30 80 02 01 01 02 01 ff 04 00 04 00 04 00 00 00 inside a
24-B envelope 04 10 <record> <6 x 00>). Run 305's
sequence clock shows the biography: arena slot zeroed
(seq 865-870, 0x1030320f), the envelope WRITTEN by
0x102-image code at seq 891-896 (store PCs 0x1020ada8 /
0x1020adab — a serialize-on-demand of the manager-held
doc value), parse wrap A seq 906, reader copy seq
907-910 (0x1032703a), parse wrap B seq 919, GET(record,1)
seq 932 -> 0x0000FFFF. Run 307's ablation: removing the
doc element removes the record parse entirely; the
record is byte-identical across ceremonies whose 24-B
docs differ (d89c2993... vs a6f8204c...) — the record is
the doc VALUE's structure {version=1, type=-1, "", "",
""}, not a decode of the token bytes. Stream blob-desc
elements carry only {ptr, len}; no type field exists in
the stream grammar. So field [1] can only be filled by
whoever mints or replaces the doc VALUE.

TASK 3 — WRITER HUNT, ALL CANDIDATES CLOSED:
- init/sel0 mints the doc with [1]=-1 — invariant to
  discriminant arg1 (1/2, stream and native), chain
  content, audio, chunk/mode ints, prior clean sel30,
  the kdrm drive (S51 + S52 controls).
- ckey (native sel 6/5/1): declares type 0 (entry
  (1,4,0,0)) but does NOT replace the doc (doc bytes
  after ckey == init's; run 308 token readback).
- sel4 via FUN_61947eaf (native, run 308): executes for
  real (EAX=0); its method parses the doc, reads [0]=1
  twice and [1]=-1 once, and exits. Doc token
  byte-identical after. A doc-state QUERY, not a writer.
- sel3/refresh: native FUN_61949679 returns E_FAIL
  0x80004005 in all three calling variants with ZERO
  stream activity (run 309) — an early guard on
  object/arg state the fabricated object doesn't
  satisfy (guard condition unidentified; the function's
  doc-store FUN_6190141f -> this+0x24 is real but
  unreachable here). At stream level (run 311) sel3
  dispatches, reads [1], and returns an EMPTY reply —
  nothing to store even if the native path ran.
- sel30/kdrmnc (S51): completes clean, no DECLARE,
  flush after still -1.
- The full base ceremony (init.ocm/ekbman/maclist
  session-open) runs in every harness session — the doc
  still comes out unbound, so the EKB/init ceremony as

- osss.ocm / omgdec.ax: desk only. omgdec.ax drives the

  Neither was driven; no doc step was identified in

TASK 4 — THE HONEST FLUSH: NOT ACHIEVED. No drive in
S52 populated field [1]. Every flush (native ladder
runs 304/305/308/309; stream runs 307/311) died

feeder out-params untouched (0xEE fill intact). No

BRIEF VERIFICATION (against the note + binaries, before any

ONE CORRECTION, from the primary record: run309.log prints
the refresh return as decimal 2147500035 = 0x80004003 =

S53 brief, copying it) said 0x80004005 E_FAIL. The log

### Task 1 (desk) — THE GUARD IS NAMED: E_POINTER on the
### two out-pointer args; no object-state guard in refresh

FUN_61949679 head, instruction level (capstone on the
pristine repo-mirror omgconv2.dll, md5 a56c74ec...):
  61949679 mov eax,6195ba08; call FUN_61955250 (thunk)
  61949690 esi = [ebp+8]            ; arg1 = ctx object
  lock-guard ctor on (ctx ? ctx+4 : 0) via FUN_61908a05
  619496ae ecx = [ebp+0xc]          ; arg2 (out ptr 1)
  if (arg2) *arg2 = 0
  619496bc eax = [ebp+0x10]         ; arg3 (out ptr 2)
  if (arg3) *arg3 = 0
  619496c5/cf if (arg2==0 || arg3==0) -> 61949a7f exit
  exit path returns 0x80004003 (E_POINTER, per decomp
  LAB_61949a95's twin at 619496c7->61949a7f; decomp shows
  the shared tail returning uVar9=0x80004003 there).
That is the WHOLE head guard. Run 309's three variants —
(0,0,0), (obj,0,0), () — all passed NULL for arg2 AND
arg3, so all three died at this check before any stream
activity, exactly as observed (SEH rounds=0, zero wraps).
There is NO check of ctx fields before the execute: ctx
(arg1) is only dereferenced when building the stream
(ctx+0x24 = the doc string) and in post-processing.
Signature (from the full decomp):
  HRESULT refresh(ctx, void** ppHdr, int* pHdr2)
    stream built natively: [sessrec][doc ctx+0x24][3][0x11]
      ("nc.ocm", via FUN_6190139c/61901c76/61901334/61901383)
    execute via salwrap1 vt+0 -> reply ctx
    reply extraction: FUN_619013e7 (status byte),
      FUN_619013cb (-> a 64-bit-ish local pair -0x48/-0x44),
      FUN_6190141f(reply, &tmpStr); FUN_6190141f(., ctx+0x24)
      = the reply's string element REPLACES the doc string
      at ctx+0x24 (the S52 doc-store, confirmed in decomp)
    post block A (only if ctx+0x178 char == 0): calls the
      interface at ctx+0x90: vt+0x2c (str,1), vt+0x38 with
      key "_OCS" (DAT_6196b454) = FUN_61903b20(ctx+0x180),
      vt+0x38 with key "_ESI" (DAT_6196b44c) =
      FUN_61903b20(ctx+0x174) (also stored to ctx+0x1a4).
      HAZARD for our fabricated obj: ctx+0x90 == NULL ->
      deref fault HERE (after the doc store).
    post block B: mode = ctx+0x20:
      ==0: Atp::AudioHeader::setTotalSF(ctx+0xa0,
             ctx+0x9c / ctx+0x5c)   (div; +0x5c hazard if 0)
      1..2: EA3-header tweaks gated on ctx+0x103 byte and
             ctx+0x189 (isLossLess, set by init); benign
             when those are 0.
    tail: FUN_61947d71(ctx, arg3) builds the header block
      (mode 0: emptyId3Tag template + ctx+0xa0 Atp::Header
      0x60B; modes 1-2: copy of ctx+0x100 EA3 block 0x60B);
      *arg2 = its return; cleanup; return S_OK (0).

Cross-check vs the family's other guards (why S52's
"object-state guard" guess was plausible but wrong):
- INIT FUN_61948a93: guard is ctx+0x38 != 0 -> E_FAIL
  0x80004005. ctx+0x38 = the doc string's LENGTH field
  (doc string object at ctx+0x24: ptr@+0x04 rel, size@
  +0x14 rel = abs +0x38, cap@ +0x18 rel = abs +0x3c —
  matches run309's token dump size=0x18 cap=0x1f and the
  harness pre-seeds +0x3c=0xF). So init refuses to run
  when a doc already exists ("session already open").
- SEL4 FUN_61947eaf (__thiscall): guard is the INVERSE —
  this+0x38 == 0 -> silent no-op; it queries only when a
  doc exists. Consistent semantics, same field.
- CKEY FUN_61947fce: guards are arg-shape checks
  ([ebp+0x28] mode in 2..3 -> else E_INVALIDARG
  0x80070057; NULL checks on [ebp+0x14]/[ebp+0x1c]/
  [ebp+0x20]).
- FUN_61947e00 (getter): same E_POINTER idiom as refresh
  (param_2/param_3 NULL -> 0x80004003).
So the family idiom is: E_POINTER for NULL out-args,
E_FAIL for state guards. Refresh simply has no state
guard of its own.

What a REAL ctx carries that ours lacks (constructor
cross-check): init itself populates +0x20 (mode, from
its arg3), the At3::Param at +0x50, +0x189, +0x170,
+0xa0 header (mode 0 only), +0x94, +0x40, doc@+0x24,
chain@+0x160, string@+0x1a8, +0x18c, GUID@+0x194 — our
ladder gets all of that for free by running real init.
What init does NOT set: ctx+0x90 (the metadata interface
used by refresh's post block A) and ctx+0x100 (the EA3
header block read in mode 1-2 paths / FUN_61947d71). In a
real session the host/broker installs +0x90 at object
construction and the EA3 block when a track is loaded.
Plan: drive refresh with valid out-pointers on the
ladder-built object as-is first (run 312); if it faults
in post block A, satisfy +0x90 with a logging stub
vtable (slots +0x2c/+0x38) — the brief-sanctioned stub —
and/or set +0x178=1 as a control (run 313).
